Appendix A
Appendix of Section
3

We say that a binary relation
{\mathcal{R}}
over single-input-single-output trees is a tree simulation if it complies with the rules given in Definition
3.2
, i.e., if for every
({\mathsf{W}}_{1},{\mathsf{W}}_{2})\in{\mathcal{R}}
there is a rule with
{\mathsf{W}}_{1}\lesssim{\mathsf{W}}_{2}
in its conclusion and it holds
({\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}_{1},{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}_{2})\in{\mathcal{R}}
if
{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}_{1}\lesssim{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}_{2}
is in the premise of the rule. It is required that all other premises hold as well.

Lemma A.1.

If
{\mathcal{R}}{\subseteq}\lesssim
is a tree simulation and
({\mathsf{W}},{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime})\in{\mathcal{R}}
then
\mathtt{act}({\mathsf{W}})=\mathtt{act}({\mathsf{W}}^{\prime})
.

Lemma A.2.

(1)

If
\mathcal{B}^{({\sf p})}\not=\mathcal{B}^{({\sf q})}
and there are
{\mathsf{W}}_{1}
and
{\mathsf{W}}_{2}
such that

{\mathsf{W}}=\mathcal{B}^{({\sf p})}.{\mathsf{W}}_{1}
and

{\mathsf{W}}=\mathcal{B}^{({\sf q})}.{\mathsf{W}}_{2}
, then one of the following holds:

(a)

{\mathsf{W}}=\mathcal{B}^{({\sf p})}.\mathcal{B}^{({\sf q})}_{1}.{\mathsf{W}}_{2}
,
where
\mathcal{B}^{({\sf q})}=\mathcal{B}^{({\sf p})}.\mathcal{B}^{({\sf q})}_{1}
and
{\mathsf{W}}_{1}=\mathcal{B}^{({\sf q})}_{1}.{\mathsf{W}}_{2}
;

(b)

{\mathsf{W}}=\mathcal{B}^{({\sf q})}.\mathcal{B}^{({\sf p})}_{1}.{\mathsf{W}}_{1}
,
where
\mathcal{B}^{({\sf p})}=\mathcal{B}^{({\sf q})}.\mathcal{B}^{({\sf p})}_{1}
and
{\mathsf{W}}_{2}=\mathcal{B}^{({\sf p})}_{1}.{\mathsf{W}}_{1}
.

(2)

If
\mathcal{A}^{({\sf p})}\not=\mathcal{A}^{({\sf q})}
and there are
{\mathsf{W}}_{1}
and
{\mathsf{W}}_{2}
such that

{\mathsf{W}}=\mathcal{A}^{({\sf p})}.{\mathsf{W}}_{1}
and

{\mathsf{W}}=\mathcal{A}^{({\sf q})}.{\mathsf{W}}_{2}
, then one of the following holds:

(a)

{\mathsf{W}}=\mathcal{A}^{({\sf p})}.\mathcal{A}^{({\sf q})}_{1}.{\mathsf{W}}_{2}
,
where
\mathcal{A}^{({\sf q})}=\mathcal{A}^{({\sf p})}.\mathcal{A}^{({\sf q})}_{1}
and
{\mathsf{W}}_{1}=\mathcal{A}^{({\sf q})}_{1}.{\mathsf{W}}_{2}
;

(b)

{\mathsf{W}}=\mathcal{A}^{({\sf q})}.\mathcal{A}^{({\sf p})}_{1}.{\mathsf{W}}_{1}
,
where
\mathcal{A}^{({\sf p})}=\mathcal{A}^{({\sf q})}.\mathcal{A}^{({\sf p})}_{1}
and
{\mathsf{W}}_{2}=\mathcal{A}^{({\sf p})}_{1}.{\mathsf{W}}_{1}
.

(3)

If
{\mathsf{W}}=\mathcal{B}^{({\sf p})}.{\mathsf{W}}_{1}
and
{\mathsf{W}}=\mathcal{A}^{({\sf q})}.{\mathsf{W}}_{2}
, for some
{\mathsf{W}}_{1}
and
{\mathsf{W}}_{2}
,
where
\mathcal{B}^{({\sf p})}
is not an I-sequence, then
{\mathsf{W}}=\mathcal{A}^{({\sf q})}.\mathcal{B}^{({\sf p})}_{1}.{\mathsf{W}}_{1}
,
where
\mathcal{B}^{({\sf p})}=\mathcal{A}^{({\sf q})}.{\mathcal{B}^{({\sf p})}_{1}}
and
{\mathsf{W}}_{2}=\mathcal{B}^{({\sf p})}_{1}.{\mathsf{W}}_{1}
.

Lemma A.3.

Let
{\mathcal{R}}{\subseteq}\lesssim
be a tree simulation.

(1)

If
(\mathcal{B}^{({\sf p})}.{\sf p}!\ell({\mathsf{S}}).{\mathsf{W}},{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime})\in{\mathcal{R}}
then

{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}=\mathcal{B}^{({\sf p})}_{1}.{\sf p}!\ell({\mathsf{S}}_{1}).{\mathsf{W}}_{1}\text{ or }{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}={\sf p}!\ell({\mathsf{S}}_{1}).{\mathsf{W}}_{1},\text{ where }{\mathsf{S}}\leq\vcentcolon{\mathsf{S}}_{1}.

(2)

If
(\mathcal{A}^{({\sf p})}.{\sf p}?\ell({\mathsf{S}}).{\mathsf{W}},{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime})\in{\mathcal{R}}
then

{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}=\mathcal{A}^{({\sf p})}_{1}.{\sf p}?\ell({\mathsf{S}}_{1}).{\mathsf{W}}_{1}\text{ or }{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}={\sf p}?\ell({\mathsf{S}}_{1}).{\mathsf{W}}_{1},\text{ where }{\mathsf{S}}_{1}\leq\vcentcolon{\mathsf{S}}.

Proof.

(1)

The proof is by induction on the structure of context
\mathcal{B}^{({\sf p})}
. The basis step is included in the induction step if we notice that the lemma holds by definition of
\lesssim
for
({\sf p}!\ell({\mathsf{S}}).{\mathsf{W}},{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime})\in{\mathcal{R}}.

For the inductive step, we distinguish two cases:

(a)

Let
\mathcal{B}^{({\sf p})}={\sf q}!\ell^{\prime}({\mathsf{S}}^{\prime}).\mathcal{B}^{({\sf p})}_{2}
and
{\sf p}\neq{\sf q}.

Then,
({\sf q}!\ell^{\prime}({\mathsf{S}}^{\prime}).\mathcal{B}^{({\sf p})}_{2}.{\sf p}!\ell({\mathsf{S}}).{\mathsf{W}},{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime})\in{\mathcal{R}}

could be derived by rules
[
ref-out
]
or
[
ref-
{\mathcal{B}}
]
.

(i)

If the rule applied is
[
ref-out
]
then we have

{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}={\sf q}!\ell^{\prime}({\mathsf{S}}^{\prime\prime}).{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime\prime}\text{ and }{\mathsf{S}}^{\prime}\leq\vcentcolon{\mathsf{S}}^{\prime\prime}\text{ and }(\mathcal{B}^{({\sf p})}_{2}.{\sf p}!\ell({\mathsf{S}}).{\mathsf{W}},{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime\prime})\in{\mathcal{R}}.

By induction hypothesis

{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime\prime}=\mathcal{B}^{({\sf p})}_{1}.{\sf p}!\ell({\mathsf{S}}_{1}).{\mathsf{W}}_{1}\text{ or }{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime\prime}={\sf p}!\ell({\mathsf{S}}_{1}).{\mathsf{W}}_{1},\text{ where }{\mathsf{S}}\leq\vcentcolon{\mathsf{S}}_{1}.

Both cases follow directly.

(ii)

If the rule applied is
[
ref-
{\mathcal{B}}
]
then we have

{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}=\mathcal{B}^{({\sf q})}_{1}.{\sf q}!\ell^{\prime}({\mathsf{S}}^{\prime\prime}).{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime\prime}\text{ and }{\mathsf{S}}^{\prime}\leq\vcentcolon{\mathsf{S}}^{\prime\prime}\text{ and }(\mathcal{B}^{({\sf p})}_{2}.{\sf p}!\ell({\mathsf{S}}).{\mathsf{W}},\mathcal{B}^{({\sf q})}_{1}.{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime\prime})\in{\mathcal{R}}.

By induction hypothesis one of the following holds:

(A)

If
\mathcal{B}^{({\sf q})}_{1}.{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime\prime}=\mathcal{B}^{({\sf p})}_{1}.{\sf p}!\ell({\mathsf{S}}_{1}).{\mathsf{W}}_{1}

then,

•

either
\mathcal{B}^{({\sf q})}_{1}=\mathcal{B}^{({\sf p})}_{1}\text{ and }{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime\prime}={\sf p}!\ell({\mathsf{S}}_{1}).{\mathsf{W}}_{1}\text{ and }

\begin{array}[]{l}\qquad\qquad{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}=\mathcal{B}^{({\sf q})}_{1}.{\sf q}!\ell^{\prime}({\mathsf{S}}^{\prime\prime}).{\sf p}!\ell({\mathsf{S}}_{1}).{\mathsf{W}}_{1},\end{array}

•

or, if
\mathcal{B}^{({\sf q})}_{1}\not=\mathcal{B}^{({\sf p})}_{1}
, by Lemma
A.2

\begin{array}[]{l}\mathcal{B}^{({\sf q})}_{1}.{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime\prime}=\mathcal{B}^{({\sf q})}_{2}.\mathcal{B}^{({\sf p})}_{3}.{\sf p}!\ell({\mathsf{S}}_{1}).{\mathsf{W}}_{1},\text{ where }\mathcal{B}^{({\sf q})}_{2}.\mathcal{B}^{({\sf p})}_{3}=\mathcal{B}^{({\sf p})}_{1}\text{ and }\\
\qquad\qquad{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}=\mathcal{B}^{({\sf q})}_{2}.\mathcal{B}^{({\sf p})}_{3}.{\sf p}!\ell({\mathsf{S}}_{1}).{\mathsf{W}}_{1},\text{ or }\\
\mathcal{B}^{({\sf q})}_{1}.{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime\prime}=\mathcal{B}^{({\sf p})}_{1}.{\sf p}!\ell({\mathsf{S}}_{1}).\mathcal{B}^{({\sf q})}_{3}.{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime\prime},\text{ where }\mathcal{B}^{({\sf q})}_{1}=\mathcal{B}^{({\sf p})}_{1}.{\sf p}!\ell({\mathsf{S}}_{1}).\mathcal{B}^{({\sf q})}_{3}\text{ and }\\
\qquad\qquad{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}=\mathcal{B}^{({\sf p})}_{1}.{\sf p}!\ell({\mathsf{S}}_{1}).\mathcal{B}^{({\sf q})}_{3}.{\sf q}!\ell^{\prime}({\mathsf{S}}^{\prime\prime}).{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime\prime}.\end{array}

(B)

If
\mathcal{B}^{({\sf q})}_{1}.{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime\prime}={\sf p}!\ell({\mathsf{S}}_{1}).{\mathsf{W}}_{1}
, where
{\mathsf{S}}\leq\vcentcolon{\mathsf{S}}_{1}
,
then, either

\mathcal{B}^{({\sf q})}_{1}={\sf p}!\ell({\mathsf{S}}_{1})\text{ and }{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime\prime}={\mathsf{W}}_{1}

or, by Lemma
A.2

\mathcal{B}^{({\sf q})}_{1}.{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime\prime}={\sf p}!\ell({\mathsf{S}}_{1}).\mathcal{B}^{({\sf q})}_{2}.{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime\prime},\text{ where }\mathcal{B}^{({\sf q})}_{1}={\sf p}!\ell({\mathsf{S}}_{1}).\mathcal{B}^{({\sf q})}_{2}.

In both cases the proof follows directly.

(b)

Let
\mathcal{B}^{({\sf p})}={\sf q}?\ell^{\prime}({\mathsf{S}}^{\prime}).\mathcal{B}^{({\sf p})}_{2}
.
Then,
({\sf q}?\ell^{\prime}({\mathsf{S}}^{\prime}).\mathcal{B}^{({\sf p})}_{2}.{\sf p}!\ell({\mathsf{S}}).{\mathsf{W}},{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime})\in{\mathcal{R}}

could be derived by rules
[
ref-in
]
or
[
ref-
{\mathcal{A}}
]
.

(i)

If
[
ref-in
]
is applied then we get this case by the same reasoning
as in the first part of the proof.

(ii)

If the rule applied is
[
ref-
{\mathcal{A}}
]
then

{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}=\mathcal{A}^{({\sf q})}_{1}.{\sf q}?\ell^{\prime}({\mathsf{S}}^{\prime\prime}).{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime\prime}\text{ and }{\mathsf{S}}^{\prime\prime}\leq\vcentcolon{\mathsf{S}}^{\prime}\text{ and }(\mathcal{B}^{({\sf p})}_{2}.{\sf p}!\ell({\mathsf{S}}).{\mathsf{W}},\mathcal{A}^{({\sf q})}_{1}.{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime\prime})\in{\mathcal{R}}.

By induction hypothesis we have only the case

\mathcal{A}^{({\sf q})}_{1}.{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime\prime}=\mathcal{B}^{({\sf p})}_{1}.{\sf p}!\ell({\mathsf{S}}_{1}).{\mathsf{W}}_{1}
,
where
{\mathsf{S}}\leq\vcentcolon{\mathsf{S}}_{1}
, since the case

\mathcal{A}^{({\sf q})}_{1}.{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime\prime}={\sf p}!\ell({\mathsf{S}}_{1}).{\mathsf{W}}_{1}
is not possible.
By Lemma
A.2
we have

\mathcal{A}^{({\sf q})}_{1}.{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime\prime}=\mathcal{A}^{({\sf q})}_{1}.\mathcal{B}^{({\sf p})}_{3}.{\sf p}!\ell({\mathsf{S}}_{1}).{\mathsf{W}}_{1}
,
where
\mathcal{A}^{({\sf q})}_{1}.\mathcal{B}^{({\sf p})}_{3}=\mathcal{B}^{({\sf p})}_{1}
.

(2)

The proof is by induction on the structure of context
\mathcal{A}^{({\sf p})}
and follows by similar reasoning.

∎

By definition of
\lesssim
we consider related pairs by peeling left-hand side trees from left to right, i.e. by matching and eliminating always the leftmost tree. The proof of transitivity requires also to consider actions that are somewhere in the middle of the left-hand side tree. For that purpose, we associate each binary relation
{\mathcal{R}}
over SISO trees with its extension
{\mathcal{R}}^{+}
as follows:

\begin{array}[]{lclcl}{\mathcal{R}}^{+}&=&{\mathcal{R}}&\cup&\{(\mathcal{B}^{({\sf p})}.{\mathsf{W}},\mathcal{B}^{({\sf p})}_{1}.{\mathsf{W}}_{1}):(\mathcal{B}^{({\sf p})}.{\sf p}!\ell({\mathsf{S}}).{\mathsf{W}},\mathcal{B}^{({\sf p})}_{1}.{\sf p}!\ell({\mathsf{S}}_{1}).{\mathsf{W}}_{1})\in{\mathcal{R}}\}\\
&&&\cup&\{(\mathcal{A}^{({\sf p})}.{\mathsf{W}},\mathcal{A}^{({\sf p})}_{1}.{\mathsf{W}}_{1}):(\mathcal{A}^{({\sf p})}.{\sf p}?\ell({\mathsf{S}}).{\mathsf{W}},\mathcal{A}^{({\sf p})}_{1}.{\sf p}?\ell({\mathsf{S}}_{1}).{\mathsf{W}}_{1})\in{\mathcal{R}}\}\\
&&&\cup&\{(\mathcal{B}^{({\sf p})}.{\mathsf{W}},{\mathsf{W}}_{1}):(\mathcal{B}^{({\sf p})}.{\sf p}!\ell({\mathsf{S}}).{\mathsf{W}},{\sf p}!\ell({\mathsf{S}}_{1}).{\mathsf{W}}_{1})\in{\mathcal{R}}\}\\
&&&\cup&\{(\mathcal{A}^{({\sf p})}.{\mathsf{W}},{\mathsf{W}}_{1}):(\mathcal{A}^{({\sf p})}.{\sf p}?\ell({\mathsf{S}}).{\mathsf{W}},{\sf p}?\ell({\mathsf{S}}_{1}).{\mathsf{W}}_{1})\in{\mathcal{R}}\}.\end{array}

Lemma A.4.

If
{\mathcal{R}}{\subseteq}\lesssim
is a tree simulation then
{\mathcal{R}}^{+}
is a tree simulation.

Proof.

We discuss some interesting cases. Let
(\mathcal{B}^{({\sf p})}.{\mathsf{W}},\mathcal{B}^{({\sf p})}_{1}.{\mathsf{W}}_{1})\in{\mathcal{R}}^{+}\setminus{\mathcal{R}}.

(1)

If
\mathcal{B}^{({\sf p})}={\sf q}!\ell^{\prime}({\mathsf{S}}^{\prime}).\mathcal{B}^{({\sf p})}_{2}
, then,

({\sf q}!\ell^{\prime}({\mathsf{S}}^{\prime}).\mathcal{B}^{({\sf p})}_{2}.{\sf p}!\ell({\mathsf{S}}).{\mathsf{W}},\mathcal{B}^{({\sf p})}_{1}.{\sf p}!\ell({\mathsf{S}}_{1}).{\mathsf{W}}_{1})\in{\mathcal{R}}

could be derived by two rules:

(a)

if the rule applied is
[
ref-out
]
then

\mathcal{B}^{({\sf p})}_{1}={\sf q}!\ell^{\prime}({\mathsf{S}}^{\prime\prime}).\mathcal{B}^{({\sf p})}_{3}\text{ and }(\mathcal{B}^{({\sf p})}_{2}.{\sf p}!\ell({\mathsf{S}}).{\mathsf{W}},\mathcal{B}^{({\sf p})}_{3}.{\sf p}!\ell({\mathsf{S}}_{1}).{\mathsf{W}}_{1})\in{\mathcal{R}},\text{ where }{\mathsf{S}}^{\prime}\leq\vcentcolon{\mathsf{S}}^{\prime\prime}.

By definition of
{\mathcal{R}}^{+},
we get
(\mathcal{B}^{({\sf p})}_{2}.{\mathsf{W}},\mathcal{B}^{({\sf p})}_{3}.{\mathsf{W}}_{1})\in{\mathcal{R}}^{+}
.

(b)

if the rule applied is
[
ref-
{\mathcal{B}}
]
then

\mathcal{B}^{({\sf p})}_{1}.{\sf p}!\ell({\mathsf{S}}_{1}).{\mathsf{W}}_{1}=\mathcal{B}^{({\sf q})}.{\sf q}!\ell^{\prime}({\mathsf{S}}^{\prime\prime}).{{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime\prime}}

and by Lemma
A.2
we distinguish two cases

(i)

\mathcal{B}^{({\sf p})}_{1}.{\sf p}!\ell({\mathsf{S}}_{1}).{\mathsf{W}}_{1}=\mathcal{B}^{({\sf q})}.{\sf q}!\ell^{\prime}({\mathsf{S}}^{\prime\prime}).\mathcal{B}^{({\sf p})}_{3}.{\sf p}!\ell({\mathsf{S}}_{1}).{\mathsf{W}}_{1},\text{ where }\mathcal{B}^{({\sf p})}_{1}=\mathcal{B}^{({\sf q})}.{\sf q}!\ell^{\prime}({\mathsf{S}}^{\prime\prime}).\mathcal{B}^{({\sf p})}_{3}

Then, by
[
ref-
{\mathcal{B}}
]
we get

(\mathcal{B}^{({\sf p})}_{2}.{\sf p}!\ell({\mathsf{S}}).{\mathsf{W}},\mathcal{B}^{({\sf q})}.\mathcal{B}^{({\sf p})}_{3}.{\sf p}!\ell({\mathsf{S}}_{1}).{\mathsf{W}}_{1})\in{\mathcal{R}}

and
{\mathsf{S}}^{\prime}\leq\vcentcolon{\mathsf{S}}^{\prime\prime}
.
By definition of
{\mathcal{R}}^{+},
we have

(\mathcal{B}^{({\sf p})}_{2}.{\mathsf{W}},\mathcal{B}^{({\sf q})}.\mathcal{B}^{({\sf p})}_{3}.{\mathsf{W}}_{1})\in{\mathcal{R}}^{+}.

(ii)

\mathcal{B}^{({\sf p})}_{1}.{\sf p}!\ell({\mathsf{S}}_{1}).{\mathsf{W}}_{1}=\mathcal{B}^{({\sf p})}_{1}.{\sf p}!\ell({\mathsf{S}}_{1}).\mathcal{B}^{({\sf q})}_{2}.{\sf q}!\ell^{\prime}({\mathsf{S}}^{\prime\prime}).{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime\prime},\text{ where }\mathcal{B}^{({\sf p})}_{1}.{\sf p}!\ell({\mathsf{S}}_{1}).\mathcal{B}^{({\sf q})}_{2}=\mathcal{B}^{({\sf q})}.

Then, we apply similar reasoning as in the first case.

(2)

if
\mathcal{B}^{({\sf p})}={\sf q}?\ell^{\prime}({\mathsf{S}}^{\prime}).\mathcal{B}^{({\sf p})}_{2}

and

({\sf q}?\ell^{\prime}({\mathsf{S}}^{\prime}).\mathcal{B}^{({\sf p})}_{2}.{\sf p}!\ell({\mathsf{S}}).{\mathsf{W}},\mathcal{B}^{({\sf p})}_{1}.{\sf p}!\ell({\mathsf{S}}_{1}).{\mathsf{W}}_{1})\in{\mathcal{R}}
, then by Lemma
A.3
we have

\mathcal{B}^{({\sf p})}_{1}.{\sf p}!\ell({\mathsf{S}}_{1}).{\mathsf{W}}_{1}=\mathcal{A}^{({\sf q})}_{1}.{\sf q}?\ell^{\prime}({\mathsf{S}}^{\prime\prime}).{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime\prime}

and
{\mathsf{S}}^{\prime\prime}\leq\vcentcolon{\mathsf{S}}^{\prime}
.
By Lemma
A.2

\mathcal{B}^{({\sf p})}_{1}.{\sf p}!\ell({\mathsf{S}}_{1}).{\mathsf{W}}_{1}=\mathcal{A}^{({\sf q})}_{1}.{\sf q}?\ell^{\prime}({\mathsf{S}}^{\prime\prime}).\mathcal{B}^{({\sf p})}_{3}.{\sf p}!\ell({\mathsf{S}}_{1}).{\mathsf{W}}_{1},\text{ where }\mathcal{B}^{({\sf p})}_{1}=\mathcal{A}^{({\sf q})}_{1}.{\sf q}?\ell^{\prime}({\mathsf{S}}^{\prime\prime}).\mathcal{B}^{({\sf p})}_{3}.

Then, by
[
ref-
{\mathcal{A}}
]
we get

(\mathcal{B}^{({\sf p})}_{2}.{\sf p}!\ell({\mathsf{S}}).{\mathsf{W}},\mathcal{A}^{({\sf q})}_{1}.\mathcal{B}^{({\sf p})}_{3}.{\sf p}!\ell({\mathsf{S}}_{1}).{\mathsf{W}}_{1})\in{\mathcal{R}}
,
and consequently

(\mathcal{B}^{({\sf p})}_{2}.{\mathsf{W}},\mathcal{A}^{({\sf q})}_{1}.\mathcal{B}^{({\sf p})}_{3}.{\mathsf{W}}_{1})\in{\mathcal{R}}^{+}
.

∎

Proof of Lemma
3.3

Proof.

Reflexivity is straightforward: any SISO tree
{\mathsf{W}}
is related to itself by a coinductive derivation which only uses rules
[
ref-in
]
,
[
ref-out
]
, and
[
ref-end
]
in Def.
3.2
.

We now focus on the proof of transitivity. If
{\mathsf{W}}_{1}\lesssim{\mathsf{W}}_{2}
and
{\mathsf{W}}_{2}\lesssim{\mathsf{W}}_{3}
then there are tree simulations
{\mathcal{R}}_{1}
and
{\mathcal{R}}_{2}
such that
({\mathsf{W}}_{1},{\mathsf{W}}_{2})\in{\mathcal{R}}_{1}
and
({\mathsf{W}}_{2},{\mathsf{W}}_{3})\in{\mathcal{R}}_{2}.

We shall prove that relation

{\mathcal{R}}={\mathcal{R}}_{1}\circ{\mathcal{R}}_{2}^{+}

is a tree simulation that contains
({\mathsf{W}}_{1},{\mathsf{W}}_{3})
. It follows directly from definition of
{\mathcal{R}}^{+}
that
({\mathsf{W}}_{1},{\mathsf{W}}_{3})\in{\mathcal{R}}
and it remains to prove that
{\mathcal{R}}
is a tree simulation.
Assuming that
({\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}_{1},{\mathsf{W}}_{3}^{\prime})\in{\mathcal{R}}
we consider the following possible cases for
{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}_{1}:

(1)

{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}_{1}=\mathtt{end}:
By definition of
{\mathcal{R}}
there is
{\mathsf{W}}_{2}^{\prime}
such that
(\mathtt{end},{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}_{2})\in{\mathcal{R}}_{1}
and
({\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}_{2},{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}_{3})\in{\mathcal{R}}_{2}^{+}.
Since
{\mathcal{R}}_{1}
and
{\mathcal{R}}_{2}^{+}
are tree simulations, it holds by
[
ref-end
]
that
{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}_{2}=\mathtt{end}
and also
{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}_{3}=\mathtt{end}.

(2)

{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}_{1}={\sf p}!\ell({\mathsf{S}}).{\mathsf{W}}:
By definition of
{\mathcal{R}}
there is
{\mathsf{W}}_{2}^{\prime}
such that
({\sf p}!\ell({\mathsf{S}}).{\mathsf{W}},{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}_{2})\in{\mathcal{R}}_{1}
and
({\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}_{2},{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}_{3})\in{\mathcal{R}}_{2}^{+}.
Since
{\mathcal{R}}_{1}
is tree simulation, using definition of
\mathcal{B}
-sequence and applying
[
ref-out
]
or
[
ref-
\mathcal{B}
]
, we get three possibilities for
{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}_{2}
:

(a)

{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}_{2}={\sf p}!\ell({\mathsf{S}}_{1}).{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}
with
{\mathsf{S}}\leq\vcentcolon{\mathsf{S}}_{1}
and
({\mathsf{W}},{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime})\in{\mathcal{R}}_{1}:

Since
{\mathcal{R}}_{2}^{+}
is tree simulation and
({\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}_{2},{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}_{3})\in{\mathcal{R}}_{2}^{+}
, there are two possibilities for
{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}_{3}
(by
[
ref-out
]
or
[
ref-
\mathcal{B}
]
):

(i)

{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}_{3}={\sf p}!\ell({\mathsf{S}}_{2}).{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime\prime}
and
{\mathsf{S}}_{1}\leq\vcentcolon{\mathsf{S}}_{2}
and
({\mathsf{W}}^{\prime},{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime\prime})\in{\mathcal{R}}_{2}^{+}:
Then, by transitivity of
\leq\vcentcolon
and definition of
{\mathcal{R}}
we get

{\mathsf{S}}\leq\vcentcolon{\mathsf{S}}_{2}\text{ and }({\mathsf{W}},{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime\prime})\in{\mathcal{R}}_{1}\circ{\mathcal{R}}_{2}^{+}={\mathcal{R}}.

(ii)

{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}_{3}=\mathcal{B}^{({\sf p})}.{\sf p}!\ell({\mathsf{S}}_{2}).{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime\prime}
and
{\mathsf{S}}_{1}\leq\vcentcolon{\mathsf{S}}_{2}
and
({\mathsf{W}}^{\prime},\mathcal{B}^{({\sf p})}.{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime\prime})\in{\mathcal{R}}_{2}:
Then, by transitivity of
\leq\vcentcolon
and definition of
{\mathcal{R}}
we get

{\mathsf{S}}\leq\vcentcolon{\mathsf{S}}_{2}\text{ and }({\mathsf{W}},\mathcal{B}^{({\sf p})}.{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime\prime})\in{\mathcal{R}}_{1}\circ{\mathcal{R}}_{2}^{+}={\mathcal{R}}.

(b)

{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}_{2}={\sf q}!\ell^{\prime}({\mathsf{S}}^{\prime}).\mathcal{B}^{({\sf p})}.{\sf p}!\ell({\mathsf{S}}_{1}).{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}
with
{\mathsf{S}}\leq\vcentcolon{\mathsf{S}}_{1}
and

(9)

({\mathsf{W}},{\sf q}!\ell^{\prime}({\mathsf{S}}^{\prime}).\mathcal{B}^{({\sf p})}.{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime})\in{\mathcal{R}}_{1}\text{ and }\mathtt{act}({\mathsf{W}})=\mathtt{act}({\sf q}!\ell^{\prime}({\mathsf{S}}^{\prime}).\mathcal{B}^{({\sf p})}.{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}):

Since
({\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}_{2},{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}_{3})\in{\mathcal{R}}_{2}^{+},
we have two cases (by
[
ref-out
]
or
[
ref-
\mathcal{B}
]
):

(i)

{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}_{3}=\mathcal{B}^{({\sf q})}_{11}.{\sf q}!\ell^{\prime}({\mathsf{S}}^{\prime\prime}).{{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime\prime}}
with
{\mathsf{S}}^{\prime}\leq\vcentcolon{\mathsf{S}}^{\prime\prime}
and
(\mathcal{B}^{({\sf p})}.{\sf p}!\ell({\mathsf{S}}_{1}).{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime},\mathcal{B}^{({\sf q})}_{11}.{{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime\prime}})\in{\mathcal{R}}_{2}^{+}.

By Lemma
A.3
, we have that

{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}_{3}=\mathcal{B}^{({\sf p})}_{1}.{\sf p}!\ell({\mathsf{S}}_{2}).{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime\prime\prime}\text{ and }{\mathsf{S}}_{1}\leq\vcentcolon{\mathsf{S}}_{2}.

By Lemma
A.2
, there are two possibilities:

(A)

{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}_{3}=\mathcal{B}^{({\sf q})}_{11}.{\sf q}!\ell^{\prime}({\mathsf{S}}^{\prime\prime}).\mathcal{B}^{({\sf p})}_{2}.{\sf p}!\ell({\mathsf{S}}_{2}).{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime\prime\prime}
and
{\sf p}!\not\in\mathtt{act}(\mathcal{B}^{({\sf q})}_{11}):

(10)

\begin{split}({\sf q}!\ell^{\prime}({\mathsf{S}}^{\prime}).\mathcal{B}^{({\sf p})}.{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime},\mathcal{B}^{({\sf q})}_{11}.{\sf q}!\ell^{\prime}({\mathsf{S}}^{\prime\prime}).\mathcal{B}^{({\sf p})}_{2}.{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime\prime\prime})\in{\mathcal{R}}_{2}^{+}\text{ and }\\
\mathtt{act}({\sf q}!\ell^{\prime}({\mathsf{S}}^{\prime}).\mathcal{B}^{({\sf p})}.{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime})=\mathtt{act}(\mathcal{B}^{({\sf q})}_{11}.{\sf q}!\ell^{\prime}({\mathsf{S}}^{\prime\prime}).\mathcal{B}^{({\sf p})}_{2}.{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime\prime\prime})\end{split}

Hence, we conclude from (
9
) and (
10
) that

({\mathsf{W}},\mathcal{B}^{({\sf q})}_{11}.{\sf q}!\ell^{\prime}({\mathsf{S}}^{\prime\prime}).\mathcal{B}^{({\sf p})}_{2}.{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime\prime\prime})\in{\mathcal{R}}_{1}\circ{\mathcal{R}}_{2}^{+}\text{ and }\mathtt{act}({\mathsf{W}})=\mathtt{act}(\mathcal{B}^{({\sf q})}_{11}.{\sf q}!\ell^{\prime}({\mathsf{S}}^{\prime\prime}).\mathcal{B}^{({\sf p})}_{2}.{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime\prime\prime}).

(B)

{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}_{3}=\mathcal{B}^{({\sf p})}_{1}.{\sf p}!\ell({\mathsf{S}}_{2}).\mathcal{B}^{({\sf q})}_{12}.{\sf q}!\ell^{\prime}({\mathsf{S}}^{\prime\prime}).{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime\prime}
and
{\sf q}!\not\in\mathtt{act}(\mathcal{B}^{({\sf p})}_{1}):

The proof is similar to the previous case.

(ii)

{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}_{3}={\sf q}!\ell^{\prime}({\mathsf{S}}^{\prime\prime}).{{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime\prime}}
with
{\mathsf{S}}^{\prime}\leq\vcentcolon{\mathsf{S}}^{\prime\prime}
and
(\mathcal{B}^{({\sf p})}.{\sf p}!\ell({\mathsf{S}}_{1}).{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime},{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime\prime})\in{\mathcal{R}}_{2}^{+}.

(c)

{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}_{2}={\sf q}?\ell^{\prime}({\mathsf{S}}^{\prime}).\mathcal{B}^{({\sf p})}.{\sf p}!\ell({\mathsf{S}}_{1}).{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}
with
{\mathsf{S}}\leq\vcentcolon{\mathsf{S}}_{1}
and

(11)

({\mathsf{W}},{\sf q}?\ell^{\prime}({\mathsf{S}}^{\prime}).\mathcal{B}^{({\sf p})}.{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime})\in{\mathcal{R}}_{1}\text{ and }\mathtt{act}({\mathsf{W}})=\mathtt{act}({\sf q}?\ell^{\prime}({\mathsf{S}}^{\prime}).\mathcal{B}^{({\sf p})}.{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}):

Since
({\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}_{2},{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}_{3})\in{\mathcal{R}}_{2}^{+},
we have two cases (by
[
ref-in
]
and
[
ref-
\mathcal{B}
]
):

(i)

{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}_{3}={\sf q}?\ell^{\prime}({\mathsf{S}}^{\prime\prime}).{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime\prime}
and
{\mathsf{S}}^{\prime\prime}\leq\vcentcolon{\mathsf{S}}^{\prime}
and
(\mathcal{B}^{({\sf p})}.{\sf p}!\ell({\mathsf{S}}_{1}).{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime},{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime\prime})\in{\mathcal{R}}_{2}^{+})
:

(ii)

{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}_{3}=\mathcal{A}^{({\sf q})}.{\sf q}?\ell^{\prime}({\mathsf{S}}^{\prime\prime}).{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime\prime}
and
{\mathsf{S}}^{\prime\prime}\leq\vcentcolon{\mathsf{S}}^{\prime}
and
(\mathcal{B}^{({\sf p})}.{\sf p}!\ell({\mathsf{S}}_{1}).{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime},\mathcal{A}^{({\sf q})}.{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime\prime})\in{\mathcal{R}}_{2}^{+})
:

(3)

{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}_{1}={\sf p}?\ell({\mathsf{S}}).{\mathsf{W}}:
The proof in this case follows similarly.

∎

Lemma A.5.

Let
\llbracket{\mathsf{T}}\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SI}}}=\{{\mathsf{V}}_{j}:j\in J\}
and let
Y=\{{\mathsf{W}}_{j}:j\in J\}
, where
{\mathsf{W}}_{j}\in\llbracket V_{j}\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SO}}}
for
j\in J
.
Then, there is
{\mathsf{U}}\in\llbracket{\mathsf{T}}\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SO}}}
such that
\llbracket{\mathsf{U}}\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SI}}}\subseteq Y
.

Proof.

In this proof we consider the coinductive interpretation of the definition of tree
{\mathsf{T}}.

(1)

{\mathsf{T}}=\mathtt{end}
:
Since
\llbracket{\mathsf{T}}\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SI}}}=\{\mathtt{end}\}
, by selecting
U=Y=\mathtt{end}
the case follows.

(2)

{\mathsf{T}}=\texternal_{i\in I}{\sf p}?\ell_{i}({\mathsf{S}}_{i}).{\mathsf{T}}_{i}
:
Assume
\llbracket{\mathsf{T}}\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SI}}}=\{{\sf p}?\ell_{i}({\mathsf{S}}_{i}).{\mathsf{V}}^{\prime}_{j_{i}}:{\mathsf{V}}^{\prime}_{j_{i}}\in\llbracket{\mathsf{T}}_{i}\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SI}}},j_{i}\in J_{i},i\in I\}
. Then,

Y=\{{\mathsf{W}}_{j}\in\llbracket{\mathsf{V}}_{j}\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SO}}}:j\in J\}=\{W_{j_{i}}={\sf p}?\ell_{i}({\mathsf{S}}_{i}).{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}_{j_{i}}:{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}_{j_{i}}\in\llbracket{\mathsf{V}}_{i}\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SO}}},j_{i}\in J_{i},i\in I\}.

Since for
i\in I
we have
\llbracket{\mathsf{T}}_{i}\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SI}}}=\{{\mathsf{V}}^{\prime}_{j_{i}}:j_{i}\in J_{i}\}
and

Y_{i}=\{{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}_{j_{i}}\in\llbracket{\mathsf{V}}^{\prime}_{j_{i}}\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SI}}}:j_{i}\in J_{i}\}
, we can apply coinductive hypothesis and obtain
{\mathsf{U}}_{i}\in\llbracket{\mathsf{T}}_{i}\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SO}}}
, such that
\llbracket{\mathsf{U}}_{i}\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SI}}}\subseteq Y_{i}
.
Hence, for
{\mathsf{U}}=\texternal_{i\in I}{\sf p}?\ell_{i}({\mathsf{S}}_{i}).U_{i}
we have
{\mathsf{U}}\in\llbracket{\mathsf{T}}\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SO}}}
and
\llbracket{\mathsf{U}}\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SI}}}\subseteq Y
.

(3)

{\mathsf{T}}=\tinternal_{i\in I}{\sf p}!\ell_{i}({\mathsf{S}}_{i}).{\mathsf{T}}_{i}
:
Assume
\llbracket{\mathsf{T}}\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SI}}}=\{\tinternal_{i\in I}{\sf p}!\ell_{i}({\mathsf{S}}_{i}).{\mathsf{V}}^{\prime}_{j_{i}}:{\mathsf{V}}^{\prime}_{j_{i}}\in\llbracket{\mathsf{T}}_{i}\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SI}}},j_{i}\in J_{i},i\in I\}
. Then,

Y=\{{\mathsf{W}}_{j}\in\llbracket{\mathsf{V}}_{j}\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SO}}}:j\in J\}=\{{\mathsf{W}}_{k_{i}}={\sf p}!\ell_{i}({\mathsf{S}}_{i}).{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}_{k_{i}}:{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}_{k_{i}}\in\llbracket{\mathsf{V}}_{i}\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SO}}},j_{i}\in K_{i}\subseteq J_{i},i\in N\subseteq I\}

We claim that there is
i\in I
such that for all
j_{i}\in J_{i}
holds
\{{\sf p}!\ell_{i}({\mathsf{S}}_{i}).\llbracket{\mathsf{V}}_{j_{i}}\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SO}}}\}\cap Y\not=\emptyset
.
To prove the claim let us assume the opposite: for all
i\in I
there is
j_{i}\in J_{i}
such that
\{{\sf p}!\ell_{i}({\mathsf{S}}_{i}).\llbracket{\mathsf{V}}_{j_{i}}\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SO}}}\}\cap Y=\emptyset
.
For such
j_{i}
’s, let us consider
{\mathsf{V}}=\tinternal_{i\in I}{\sf p}!\ell_{i}({\mathsf{S}}_{i}).{\mathsf{V}}_{j_{i}}
.
Since
{\mathsf{V}}\in\llbracket{\mathsf{T}}\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SI}}}
and
\llbracket{\mathsf{V}}\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SO}}}\cap Y=\emptyset
we obtain a contradiction with the definition of
Y
.

Let us now fix
i\in I
for which the above claim holds.
Let
Y^{\prime}=\{{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}_{k_{i}}:{\sf p}!\ell_{i}({\mathsf{S}}_{i}).{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}_{k_{i}}\in Y\}
. For
{\mathsf{T}}_{i}
we have
\llbracket{\mathsf{T}}_{i}\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SI}}}=\{{\mathsf{V}}^{\prime}_{j_{i}}:j_{i}\in J_{i}\}
and for each
j_{i}\in J_{i}
there is
{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}_{j_{i}}\in\llbracket{\mathsf{V}}^{\prime}_{j_{i}}\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SO}}}
such that
{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}_{i_{j}}\in Y^{\prime}
.
Hence, we can apply coinductive hypothesis and get
{\mathsf{U}}^{\prime}\in\llbracket{\mathsf{T}}_{i}\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SO}}}
for which
\llbracket{\mathsf{U}}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SI}}}\subseteq Y^{\prime}
holds.
By taking
{\mathsf{U}}={\sf p}!\ell_{i}({\mathsf{S}}_{i}).{\mathsf{U}}^{\prime}
we can conclude
{\mathsf{U}}\in\llbracket{\mathsf{T}}\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SO}}}
and
\llbracket{\mathsf{U}}\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SI}}}\subseteq\{{\sf p}!\ell_{i}({\mathsf{S}}_{i}).{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}_{j_{i}}:{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}_{j_{i}}\in Y^{\prime}\}\subseteq Y
.

∎

Lemma A.6.

For any tree
{\mathsf{T}}
we have

\forall{\mathsf{U}}\in\llbracket{\mathsf{T}}\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SO}}}\;\forall{\mathsf{V}}\in\llbracket{\mathsf{T}}\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SI}}}\;\exists{\mathsf{W}}\quad\text{such that}\quad{\mathsf{W}}\in\llbracket{\mathsf{U}}\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SI}}}\cap\llbracket{\mathsf{V}}\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SO}}}.

Proof.

By coinduction on the definition of tree
{\mathsf{T}}.

(1)

{\mathsf{T}}=\mathtt{end}
:
Since
\llbracket{\mathsf{T}}\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SO}}}=\llbracket{\mathsf{T}}\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SI}}}=\{\mathtt{end}\}
,
the proof follows directly.

(2)

{\mathsf{T}}=\texternal_{i\in I}{\sf p}?\ell_{i}({\mathsf{S}}_{i}).{\mathsf{T}}_{i}
:
Then,

\displaystyle{\mathsf{U}}\in\{\texternal_{i\in I}{\sf p}?\ell_{i}({\mathsf{S}}_{i}).{\mathsf{U}}^{\prime}:{\mathsf{U}}^{\prime}\in\llbracket{\mathsf{T}}_{i}\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SO}}}\}

\displaystyle{\mathsf{V}}\in\{{\sf p}?\ell({\mathsf{S}}_{i}).{\mathsf{V}}^{\prime}:{\mathsf{V}}^{\prime}\in\llbracket{\mathsf{T}}_{i}\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SI}}},i\in I\}

By coinductive hypothesis for all
i\in I
we have

\forall{\mathsf{U}}^{\prime}\in\llbracket{\mathsf{T}}_{i}\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SO}}}\;\forall{\mathsf{V}}^{\prime}\in\llbracket{\mathsf{T}}_{i}\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SI}}}\;\exists{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}\quad\text{such that}\quad{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}\in\llbracket{\mathsf{U}}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SI}}}\cap\llbracket{\mathsf{V}}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SO}}}.

Thus, for all
{\mathsf{U}}\in\llbracket{\mathsf{T}}\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SO}}}
,
i\in I
and

{\mathsf{V}}\in\{{\sf p}?\ell({\mathsf{S}}_{i}).{\mathsf{V}}^{\prime}:{\mathsf{V}}^{\prime}\in\llbracket{\mathsf{T}}_{i}\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SI}}}\}

we obtain there exists
{\mathsf{W}}={\sf p}?\ell({\mathsf{S}}_{i}).{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}
, such that

{\mathsf{W}}\in\llbracket{\mathsf{U}}\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SI}}}\cap\llbracket{\mathsf{V}}\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SO}}}

(3)

{\mathsf{T}}=\tinternal_{i\in I}{\sf p}!\ell_{i}({\mathsf{S}}_{i}).{\mathsf{T}}_{i}
: Follows by a similar reasoning.

∎

See
3.8

Proof.

Reflexivity is straightforward from
Lemma
A.6

and reflexivity of
\lesssim
.

We now focus on the proof of transitivity. Assume that
{\mathsf{T}}_{1}\leqslant{\mathsf{T}}_{2}
and
{\mathsf{T}}_{2}\leqslant{\mathsf{T}}_{3}.

From
{\mathsf{T}}_{1}\leqslant{\mathsf{T}}_{2}
, by Definition
3.7
, we have

(12)

\forall{\mathsf{U}}_{1}\in\llbracket{\mathsf{T}}_{1}\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SO}}}\;\forall{\mathsf{V}}_{2}\in\llbracket{\mathsf{T}}_{2}\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SI}}}\;\exists{\mathsf{W}}_{1}\in\llbracket{\mathsf{U}}_{1}\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SI}}}\;\exists{\mathsf{W}}_{2}\in\llbracket{\mathsf{V}}_{2}\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SO}}}\quad{\mathsf{W}}_{1}\lesssim{\mathsf{W}}_{2}

From
{\mathsf{T}}_{2}\leqslant{\mathsf{T}}_{3}
, by Definition
3.7
,

(13)

\forall{\mathsf{U}}_{2}\in\llbracket{\mathsf{T}}_{2}\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SO}}}\;\forall{\mathsf{V}}_{3}\in\llbracket{\mathsf{T}}_{3}\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SI}}}\;\exists{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}_{2}\in\llbracket{\mathsf{U}}_{2}\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SI}}}\;\exists{\mathsf{W}}_{3}\in\llbracket{\mathsf{V}}_{3}\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SO}}}\quad{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}_{2}\lesssim{\mathsf{W}}_{3}

Let us now fix one
{\mathsf{U}}_{1}\in\llbracket{\mathsf{T}}_{1}\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SO}}}
.
By (
12
) we have that

(14)

\forall{\mathsf{V}}_{2}\in\llbracket{\mathsf{T}}_{2}\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SI}}}\;\exists{\mathsf{W}}_{2}\in\llbracket{\mathsf{V}}_{2}\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SO}}}\;\exists{\mathsf{W}}_{1}\in\llbracket{\mathsf{U}}_{1}\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SI}}}\text{ such that }{\mathsf{W}}_{1}\lesssim{\mathsf{W}}_{2}

and let
Y
be the set of all such
{\mathsf{W}}_{2}
’s. By Lemma
A.5
, there exist
{\mathsf{U}}\in\llbracket{\mathsf{T}}_{2}\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SO}}}
such that
\llbracket{\mathsf{U}}\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SI}}}\subseteq Y
.
Now from (
13
) we have

\forall{\mathsf{V}}_{3}\in\llbracket{\mathsf{T}}_{3}\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SI}}}\;\exists{\mathsf{W}}_{2}\in\llbracket{\mathsf{U}}\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SI}}}\;\exists{\mathsf{W}}_{3}\in\llbracket{\mathsf{V}}_{3}\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SO}}}
such that
{\mathsf{W}}_{2}\lesssim{\mathsf{W}}_{3}
.
Then, we conclude by transitivity of
\lesssim
that

\forall{\mathsf{U}}_{1}\in\llbracket{\mathsf{T}}_{1}\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SO}}}\;\forall{\mathsf{V}}_{3}\in\llbracket{\mathsf{T}}_{3}\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SI}}}\;\exists{\mathsf{W}}_{1}\in\llbracket{\mathsf{U}}_{1}\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SI}}}\;\exists{\mathsf{W}}_{3}\in\llbracket{\mathsf{V}}_{3}\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SO}}}\quad{\mathsf{W}}_{1}\lesssim{\mathsf{W}}_{3}.

∎

A.1.
Further Examples

We illustrate the refinement relation with an example.

Example A.7.

Consider
{\mathbb{W}}_{1}=\mu\textbf{t}.{\sf p}!\ell({\mathsf{S}}).{\sf q}?\ell^{\prime}({\mathsf{S}}^{\prime}).\textbf{t}
and
{\mathbb{W}}_{2}=\mu\textbf{t}.{\sf q}?\ell^{\prime}({\mathsf{S}}^{\prime}).{\sf p}!\ell({\mathsf{S}}).\textbf{t}
. Their trees are related by the following coinductive derivation:

\operatorname{\mathcal{T}}\!\left({{\mathbb{W}}_{1}}\right)\lesssim\operatorname{\mathcal{T}}\!\left({{\mathbb{W}}_{2}}\right){\sf q}?\ell^{\prime}({\mathsf{S}}^{\prime}).\operatorname{\mathcal{T}}\!\left({{\mathbb{W}}_{1}}\right)\lesssim{\sf q}?\ell^{\prime}({\mathsf{S}}^{\prime}).\operatorname{\mathcal{T}}\!\left({{\mathbb{W}}_{2}}\right)\operatorname{\mathcal{T}}\!\left({{\mathbb{W}}_{1}}\right)\lesssim\operatorname{\mathcal{T}}\!\left({{\mathbb{W}}_{2}}\right)

Next we give a simple asynchronous subtyping example.

Example A.8 (Asynchronous subtyping).

Let
{\mathbb{T}}={\sf p}!\ell_{1}({\mathsf{S}}_{1}).\texternal{\sf q}?\left\{\begin{array}[]{@{}l@{}}\ell_{3}({\mathsf{S}}_{3}).\mathtt{end}\\
\ell_{4}({\mathsf{S}}_{4}).\mathtt{end}\end{array}\right.\;\;\text{and}\;\;{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}=\tinternal{\sf p}!\left\{\begin{array}[]{@{}l@{}}\ell_{1}({\mathsf{S}}_{1}).{\sf q}?\ell_{3}({\mathsf{S}}_{3}).\mathtt{end}\\
\ell_{2}({\mathsf{S}}_{2}).\mathtt{end}\end{array}\right.
We show that
{\mathbb{T}}\leqslant{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}
. Notice that
{\mathbb{T}}
is a SO type and
{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}
is a SI type: hence, by Def.
3.7
, we only need to show that there are

{\mathsf{W}}\in\llbracket\operatorname{\mathcal{T}}\!\left({{\mathbb{T}}}\right)\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SI}}}
and

{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}\in\llbracket\operatorname{\mathcal{T}}\!\left({{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}}\right)\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SO}}}
such that

{\mathsf{W}}\lesssim{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}
.
Since:

\begin{array}[]{ccl}\llbracket\operatorname{\mathcal{T}}\!\left({{\mathbb{T}}}\right)\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SI}}}&=&\left\{\,{\sf p}!\ell_{1}({\mathsf{S}}_{1}).{\sf q}?\ell_{3}({\mathsf{S}}_{3}).\mathtt{end},\;{\sf p}!\ell_{1}({\mathsf{S}}_{1}).{\sf q}?\ell_{4}({\mathsf{S}}_{4}).\mathtt{end}\,\right\}\\
\llbracket\operatorname{\mathcal{T}}\!\left({{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}}\right)\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SO}}}&=&\left\{\,{\sf p}!\ell_{1}({\mathsf{S}}_{1}).{\sf q}?\ell_{3}({\mathsf{S}}_{3}).\mathtt{end},\;{\sf p}!\ell_{2}({\mathsf{S}}_{2}).\mathtt{end}\,\right\}\end{array}

we have that
{\mathsf{W}}\lesssim{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}
holds for

{\mathsf{W}}={\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}={\sf p}!\ell_{1}({\mathsf{S}}_{1}).{\sf q}?\ell_{3}({\mathsf{S}}_{3}).\mathtt{end}
, by reflexivity of
\lesssim
.

Next we consider a complex example of asynchronous subtyping which
contains branching, selection and recursion.
This example is undecided by the algorithm in

(
Bravetti et al. 2019a
;
Bravetti et al. 2019b
)
(it returns “unknown”)
but we can reason by our rules using the SISO decomposition method
as demonstrated below.

Example A.9.

Consider the examples of session types
M_{1}
and
M_{2}
from

(
Bravetti et al. 2019b
, Example 3.21)
, here denoted by
{\mathbb{T}}
and
{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}
, respectively, where

\small\begin{array}[]{ll}\begin{array}[]{l}{\mathbb{T}}=\mu\textbf{t}_{1}.\texternal{\sf p}?\left\{\begin{array}[]{@{}l@{}}{\mathit{{\ell_{1}}}}{({\mathsf{S}}_{1}).}{\sf p}!\ell_{3}({\mathsf{S}}_{3}).{\sf p}!\ell_{3}({\mathsf{S}}_{3}).{\sf p}!\ell_{3}({\mathsf{S}}_{3}).\textbf{t}_{1}\\
{\mathit{{\ell_{2}}}}{({\mathsf{S}}_{2})}.\mu\textbf{t}_{2}.{\sf p}!\ell_{3}({\mathsf{S}}_{3}).\textbf{t}_{2}\end{array}\right.\end{array}&\begin{array}[]{l}{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}=\mu\textbf{t}_{1}.\texternal{\sf p}?\left\{\begin{array}[]{@{}l@{}}{\mathit{{\ell_{1}}}}{({\mathsf{S}}_{1}).}{\sf p}!\ell_{3}({\mathsf{S}}_{3}).\textbf{t}_{1}\\
{\mathit{{\ell_{2}}}}{({\mathsf{S}}_{2})}.\mu\textbf{t}_{2}.{\sf p}!\ell_{3}({\mathsf{S}}_{3}).\textbf{t}_{2}\end{array}\right.\end{array}\end{array}

{\sf p}!
{\mathbb{T}}
:
{\sf p}?
\ell_{1}({\mathsf{S}}_{1})
{\sf p}!
\ell_{2}({\mathsf{S}}_{2})
\ell_{3}({\mathsf{S}}_{3})
{\sf p}!
\ell_{3}({\mathsf{S}}_{3})
{\sf p}!
\ell_{3}({\mathsf{S}}_{3})
\ell_{3}({\mathsf{S}}_{3})
{\sf p}!
{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}
:
{\sf p}?
\ell_{1}({\mathsf{S}}_{1})
\ell_{3}({\mathsf{S}}_{3})
{\sf p}!
\ell_{2}({\mathsf{S}}_{2})
\ell_{3}({\mathsf{S}}_{3})

In
(
Bravetti et al. 2019a
;
Bravetti et al. 2019b
)
,
if the algorithm returns “true” (“false”), then the considered types are (are not) in the subtyping relation. The algorithm can return “unknown”, meaning that the algorithm cannot check whether the types are in the subtyping relation or not.

For the considered types,
the algorithm in
(
Bravetti et al. 2019a
;
Bravetti et al. 2019b
)

returns “unknown” and thus it can
not
check that
{\mathbb{T}}\leqslant{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}
, which is, according to the authors of
(
Bravetti et al. 2019a
;
Bravetti et al. 2019b
)
, due to the complex accumulation patterns of these types which cannot be recognised by their theory.

Here our approach comes into the picture and we demonstrate that the two decomposition functions into SO and SI trees are sufficiently
fine-grained to recognise the complex structure of these types and to prove that
{\mathbb{T}}\leqslant{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}
. We show that types
{\mathbb{T}}
and
{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}
are in the subtyping relation
{\mathbb{T}}\leqslant{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}
, i.e., the corresponding session trees are in the subtyping relation

\operatorname{\mathcal{T}}\!\left({{\mathbb{T}}}\right)\leqslant\operatorname{\mathcal{T}}\!\left({{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}}\right)
by showing that

\forall{\mathsf{U}}\in\llbracket{\mathsf{T}}\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SO}}}\quad\forall{\mathsf{V}}^{\prime}\in\llbracket{\mathsf{T}}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SI}}}\quad\exists{\mathsf{W}}\in\llbracket{\mathsf{U}}\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SI}}}\quad\exists{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}\in\llbracket{\mathsf{V}}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SO}}}\qquad{\mathsf{W}}\lesssim{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}

where
{\mathsf{T}}=\operatorname{\mathcal{T}}\!\left({{\mathbb{T}}}\right)
and
{\mathsf{T}}^{\prime}=\operatorname{\mathcal{T}}\!\left({{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}}\right)
.
For the sake of simplicity, let us use the following notations and abbreviations:

\begin{array}[]{ll}{\mathsf{W}}_{1}=\operatorname{\mathcal{T}}\!\left({\mu\textbf{t}.{\sf p}?\ell_{1}({\mathsf{S}}_{1}).{\sf p}!\ell_{3}({\mathsf{S}}_{3}).\textbf{t}}\right)&{\mathsf{W}}_{2}={\sf p}?\ell_{2}({\mathsf{S}}_{2}).\operatorname{\mathcal{T}}\!\left({\mu\textbf{t}.{\sf p}!\ell_{3}({\mathsf{S}}_{3}).\textbf{t}}\right)\\
{\mathsf{W}}_{3}=\operatorname{\mathcal{T}}\!\left({\mu\textbf{t}.{\sf p}?\ell_{1}({\mathsf{S}}_{1}).{\sf p}!\ell_{3}({\mathsf{S}}_{3}).{\sf p}!\ell_{3}({\mathsf{S}}_{3}).{\sf p}!\ell_{3}({\mathsf{S}}_{3}).\textbf{t}}\right)&\end{array}

\begin{array}[]{ll}\pi_{1}\equiv{\sf p}?\ell_{1}({\mathsf{S}}_{1}).{\sf p}!\ell_{3}({\mathsf{S}}_{3})&\pi_{1}^{n}\equiv\underbrace{\pi_{1}.\dots.\pi_{1}.}_{\text{n}}\\
\pi_{3}\equiv{\sf p}?\ell_{1}({\mathsf{S}}_{1}).{\sf p}!\ell_{3}({\mathsf{S}}_{3}).{\sf p}!\ell_{3}({\mathsf{S}}_{3}).{\sf p}!\ell_{3}({\mathsf{S}}_{3})&\pi_{3}^{n}\equiv\underbrace{\pi_{3}.\dots.\pi_{3}.}_{\text{n}}\end{array}

Then
\llbracket{\mathsf{T}}\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SO}}}=\{{\mathsf{T}}\}
since
{\mathsf{T}}
is a SO tree, whereas

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\llbracket{\mathsf{T}}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SI}}}&=&\{{\mathsf{W}}_{1},{\mathsf{W}}_{2},\pi_{1}.{\mathsf{W}}_{2},\pi_{1}^{2}.{\mathsf{W}}_{2},\ldots,\pi_{1}^{n}.{\mathsf{W}}_{2},\dots\}\\
\end{array}

then
{\mathsf{U}}={\mathsf{T}}
and

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\llbracket{\mathsf{U}}\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SI}}}&=&\{{\mathsf{W}}_{3},{\mathsf{W}}_{2},\pi_{3}.{\mathsf{W}}_{2},\pi_{3}^{2}.{\mathsf{W}}_{2},\ldots,\pi_{3}^{n}.{\mathsf{W}}_{2},\dots\}\\
\end{array}

Notice that all
{\mathsf{V}}^{\prime}\in\llbracket{\mathsf{T}}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SI}}}
are SISO trees hence
\llbracket{\mathsf{V}}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SO}}}=\{{\mathsf{V}}^{\prime}\}
and
{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}={\mathsf{V}}^{\prime}
.

We show now that for all
{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}\in\llbracket{\mathsf{T}}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SI}}}
there is a
{\mathsf{W}}\in\llbracket{\mathsf{U}}\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SI}}}

such that
{\mathsf{W}}\lesssim{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}
by showing:

(1)

{\mathsf{W}}_{3}\lesssim{\mathsf{W}}_{1}

(2)

\pi_{3}^{n}.{\mathsf{W}}_{2}\lesssim\pi_{1}^{n}.{\mathsf{W}}_{2}
,
n\geq 0

(1)

The tree simulation for
{\mathsf{W}}_{3}\lesssim{\mathsf{W}}_{1}
is

\begin{array}[]{lcl}{\mathcal{R}}&=&\{({\mathsf{W}}_{3},{\mathsf{W}}_{1}),\\
&&(({\sf p}!\ell_{3}({\mathsf{S}}_{3}))^{3}.{\mathsf{W}}_{3},{\sf p}!\ell_{3}({\mathsf{S}}_{3}).{\mathsf{W}}_{1}),\\
&&(({\sf p}!\ell_{3}({\mathsf{S}}_{3}))^{2}.{\mathsf{W}}_{3},{\mathsf{W}}_{1}),\\
&&({\sf p}!\ell_{3}({\mathsf{S}}_{3}).{\mathsf{W}}_{3},{\sf p}?\ell_{1}({\mathsf{S}}_{1}).{\mathsf{W}}_{1}),\\
&&({\mathsf{W}}_{3},({\sf p}?\ell_{1}({\mathsf{S}}_{1}).{\sf p}?\ell_{1}({\mathsf{S}}_{1}))^{n}.{\mathsf{W}}_{1}),\\
&&(({\sf p}!\ell_{3}({\mathsf{S}}_{3}))^{3}.{\mathsf{W}}_{3},({\sf p}?\ell_{1}({\mathsf{S}}_{1}).{\sf p}?\ell_{1}({\mathsf{S}}_{1}))^{n}.{\sf p}!\ell_{3}({\mathsf{S}}_{3}).{\mathsf{W}}_{1}),\\
&&(({\sf p}!\ell_{3}({\mathsf{S}}_{3}))^{2}.{\mathsf{W}}_{3},({\sf p}?\ell_{1}({\mathsf{S}}_{1}).{\sf p}?\ell_{1}({\mathsf{S}}_{1}))^{n}.{\mathsf{W}}_{1}),\\
&&({\sf p}!\ell_{3}({\mathsf{S}}_{3}).{\mathsf{W}}_{3},({\sf p}?\ell_{1}({\mathsf{S}}_{1}).{\sf p}?\ell_{1}({\mathsf{S}}_{1}))^{n}.{\sf p}?\ell_{1}({\mathsf{S}}_{1}).{\mathsf{W}}_{1})\mid n\geq 1\}\end{array}

where
({\sf p}?\ell_{1}({\mathsf{S}}_{1}).{\sf p}?\ell_{1}({\mathsf{S}}_{1}))^{n}\equiv\underbrace{{\sf p}?\ell_{1}({\mathsf{S}}_{1}).{\sf p}?\ell_{1}({\mathsf{S}}_{1}).\ldots{\sf p}?\ell_{1}({\mathsf{S}}_{1}).{\sf p}?\ell_{1}({\mathsf{S}}_{1}).}_{\text{2}n}\;\;n\in\mathbb{N}
and

({\sf p}!\ell_{3}({\mathsf{S}}_{3}))^{i}\equiv\underbrace{{\sf p}!\ell_{3}({\mathsf{S}}_{3}).\ldots{\sf p}!\ell_{3}({\mathsf{S}}_{3})}_{\text{i}}\;\;i=1,2,3
.

Proof.
The trees
{\mathsf{W}}_{3}
and
{\mathsf{W}}_{1}
are related by the following coinductive derivations:

for
n\geq 1

{\mathsf{W}}_{3}\lesssim({\sf p}?\ell_{1}({\mathsf{S}}_{1}).{\sf p}?\ell_{1}({\mathsf{S}}_{1}))^{n}.{\mathsf{W}}_{1}({\sf p}!\ell_{3}({\mathsf{S}}_{3}))^{3}.{\mathsf{W}}_{3}\lesssim({\sf p}?\ell_{1}({\mathsf{S}}_{1}).{\sf p}?\ell_{1}({\mathsf{S}}_{1}))^{n}.{\sf p}!\ell_{3}({\mathsf{S}}_{3}).{\mathsf{W}}_{1}({\sf p}!\ell_{3}({\mathsf{S}}_{3}))^{2}.{\mathsf{W}}_{3}\lesssim({\sf p}?\ell_{1}({\mathsf{S}}_{1}).{\sf p}?\ell_{1}({\mathsf{S}}_{1}))^{n}.{\mathsf{W}}_{1}{\sf p}!\ell_{3}({\mathsf{S}}_{3}).{\mathsf{W}}_{3}\lesssim({\sf p}?\ell_{1}({\mathsf{S}}_{1}).{\sf p}?\ell_{1}({\mathsf{S}}_{1}))^{n}.{\sf p}?\ell_{1}({\mathsf{S}}_{1}).{\mathsf{W}}_{1}{\mathsf{W}}_{3}\lesssim({\sf p}?\ell_{1}({\mathsf{S}}_{1}).{\sf p}?\ell_{1}({\mathsf{S}}_{1}))^{n+1}.{\mathsf{W}}_{1}

{\mathsf{W}}_{3}\lesssim{\mathsf{W}}_{1}({\sf p}!\ell_{3}({\mathsf{S}}_{3}))^{3}.{\mathsf{W}}_{3}\lesssim{\sf p}!\ell_{3}({\mathsf{S}}_{3}).{\mathsf{W}}_{1}({\sf p}!\ell_{3}({\mathsf{S}}_{3}))^{2}.{\mathsf{W}}_{3}\lesssim{\mathsf{W}}_{1}{\sf p}!\ell_{3}({\mathsf{S}}_{3}).{\mathsf{W}}_{3}\lesssim{\sf p}?\ell_{1}({\mathsf{S}}_{1}).{\mathsf{W}}_{1}{\mathsf{W}}_{3}\lesssim{\sf p}?\ell_{1}({\mathsf{S}}_{1}).{\sf p}?\ell_{1}({\mathsf{S}}_{1}).{\mathsf{W}}_{1}

(2)

Case
\pi_{3}^{n}.{\mathsf{W}}_{2}\lesssim\pi_{1}^{n}.{\mathsf{W}}_{2}
,
n\geq 0
.

If
n=0
, then
{\mathsf{W}}_{2}\lesssim{\mathsf{W}}_{2}
holds by reflexivity of
\lesssim
, Lemma
3.3
.

In case
n>0
, we first show that

(15)

\pi_{3}^{n}.{\mathsf{W}}_{2}\lesssim\pi_{1}.\pi_{3}^{n-1}.{\mathsf{W}}_{2}

The tree simulation is

\begin{array}[]{lcl}{\mathcal{R}}&=&\{(\pi_{3}^{n}.{\mathsf{W}}_{2},\pi_{1}.\pi_{3}^{n-1}.{\mathsf{W}}_{2}),\\
&&(({\sf p}!\ell_{3}({\mathsf{S}}_{3}))^{3}.\pi_{3}^{n-1}.{\mathsf{W}}_{2},{\sf p}!\ell_{3}({\mathsf{S}}_{3}).\pi_{3}^{n-1}.{\mathsf{W}}_{2}),\\
&&(({\sf p}!\ell_{3}({\mathsf{S}}_{3}))^{2}.\pi_{3}^{n-1}.{\mathsf{W}}_{2},\pi_{3}^{n-1}.{\mathsf{W}}_{2}),\\
&&({\sf p}!\ell_{3}({\mathsf{S}}_{3}).\pi_{3}^{n-1}.{\mathsf{W}}_{2},\pi_{1}.{\sf p}!\ell_{3}({\mathsf{S}}_{3}).\pi_{3}^{n-2}.{\mathsf{W}}_{2}),\\
&&(\pi_{3}^{n-1}.{\mathsf{W}}_{2},\pi_{1}.\pi_{3}^{n-2}.{\mathsf{W}}_{2}),\\
&&\vdots\\
&&(\pi_{3}.{\mathsf{W}}_{2},\pi_{1}.{\mathsf{W}}_{2}),\\
&&(({\sf p}!\ell_{3}({\mathsf{S}}_{3}))^{3}.{\mathsf{W}}_{2},{\sf p}!\ell_{3}({\mathsf{S}}_{3}).{\mathsf{W}}_{2}),\\
&&(({\sf p}!\ell_{3}({\mathsf{S}}_{3}))^{2}.{\mathsf{W}}_{2},{\mathsf{W}}_{2}),\\
&&({\sf p}!\ell_{3}({\mathsf{S}}_{3}).{\mathsf{W}}_{2},{\mathsf{W}}_{2}),\\
&&({\mathsf{W}}_{2},{\mathsf{W}}_{2})\}\\
\end{array}

The refinement
\pi_{3}^{n}.{\mathsf{W}}_{2}\lesssim\pi_{1}.\pi_{3}^{n-1}.{\mathsf{W}}_{2}
is derived by the following coinductive derivation

\pi_{3}^{n}.{\mathsf{W}}_{2}\lesssim\pi_{1}.\pi_{3}^{n-1}.{\mathsf{W}}_{2}\\
\vdots\\
\pi_{3}^{2}.{\mathsf{W}}_{2}\lesssim\pi_{1}.\pi_{3}.{\mathsf{W}}_{2}({\sf p}!\ell_{3}({\mathsf{S}}_{3}))^{3}.\pi_{3}.{\mathsf{W}}_{2}\lesssim{\sf p}!\ell_{3}({\mathsf{S}}_{3}).\pi_{3}.{\mathsf{W}}_{2}({\sf p}!\ell_{3}({\mathsf{S}}_{3}))^{2}.\pi_{3}.{\mathsf{W}}_{2}\lesssim\pi_{3}.{\mathsf{W}}_{2}{\sf p}!\ell_{3}({\mathsf{S}}_{3}).\pi_{3}.{\mathsf{W}}_{2}\lesssim\pi_{1}.{\sf p}!\ell_{3}({\mathsf{S}}_{3}).{\mathsf{W}}_{2}\pi_{3}.{\mathsf{W}}_{2}\lesssim\pi_{1}.{\mathsf{W}}_{2}({\sf p}!\ell_{3}({\mathsf{S}}_{3}))^{3}.{\mathsf{W}}_{2}\lesssim{\sf p}!\ell_{3}({\mathsf{S}}_{3}).{\mathsf{W}}_{2}({\sf p}!\ell_{3}({\mathsf{S}}_{3}))^{2}.{\mathsf{W}}_{2}\lesssim{\mathsf{W}}_{2}{\sf p}!\ell_{3}({\mathsf{S}}_{3}).{\mathsf{W}}_{2}\lesssim{\mathsf{W}}_{2}{\mathsf{W}}_{2}\lesssim{\mathsf{W}}_{2}

This coinductive derivation proves all refinements

\pi_{3}^{n-k}.{\mathsf{W}}_{2}\lesssim\pi_{1}.\pi_{3}^{n-k-1}.{\mathsf{W}}_{2},\quad k=0,\ldots,n-1.

By
k
consecutive application first of
[
ref-out
]
and then
[
ref-in
]
, it holds that

\pi_{1}^{k}\pi_{3}^{n-k}.{\mathsf{W}}_{2}\lesssim\pi_{1}^{k+1}.\pi_{3}^{n-k-1}.{\mathsf{W}}_{2},\quad k=0,\ldots,n-1.

which means that

\begin{array}[]{ll}\pi_{3}^{n}.{\mathsf{W}}_{2}\lesssim\pi_{1}.\pi_{3}^{n-1}.{\mathsf{W}}_{2},&\\
\pi_{1}.\pi_{3}^{n-1}.{\mathsf{W}}_{2}\lesssim\pi_{1}^{2}.\pi_{3}^{n-2}.{\mathsf{W}}_{2},&\\
\vdots&\\
\pi_{1}^{k}\pi_{3}^{n-k}.{\mathsf{W}}_{2}\lesssim\pi_{1}^{k+1}.\pi_{3}^{n-k-1}.{\mathsf{W}}_{2},&\\
\vdots&\\
\pi_{1}^{n-1}\pi_{3}.{\mathsf{W}}_{2}\lesssim\pi_{1}^{n}.{\mathsf{W}}_{2}&\end{array}

then
\pi_{3}^{n}.{\mathsf{W}}_{2}\lesssim\pi_{1}^{n}.{\mathsf{W}}_{2}
follows by transitivity of
\lesssim
, Lemma
3.3
.

This concludes the proof that
{\mathbb{T}}\leqslant{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}
, which could not be given by
the answer (Yes or No) by the algorithm in
(
Bravetti et al. 2019b
, Example 3.21)
.

Appendix B
Appendix of Section
4

B.1.
Proof of Theorem
4.7

The main aim of this section is to prove
Theorem
4.7
.

Lemma B.1.

If
\Gamma
is live and
\Gamma\equiv\Gamma^{\prime}
then
\Gamma^{\prime}
is also live.

Proof.

From the the definition of
\Gamma\equiv\Gamma^{\prime}
,
\Gamma
and
\Gamma^{\prime}
perform exactly the same reductions (i.e., they are strongly bisimilar). Therefore, the result follows by Definition
4.4
.
∎

See
4.6

Proof.

By contradiction, assume that
\Gamma
is live,
while
\Gamma^{\prime}
is
not
live. Then, by
Definition

4.4
,
there is a fair but non-live path
(\Gamma^{\prime}_{i})_{i\in I}

such that
\Gamma^{\prime}_{0}=\Gamma^{\prime}
.
But then, consider the path that starts with
\Gamma
,
reaches
\Gamma^{\prime}
as the first step, and then follows
(\Gamma^{\prime}_{i})_{i\in I}

— more formally, the path
(\Gamma_{j})_{j\in J}

such that
\Gamma_{0}=\Gamma
, and
\forall j\in J:j\geq 1
implies
\Gamma_{j}=\Gamma^{\prime}_{j-1}
.
Such a path is fair, but not live: therefore,
by
Definition

4.4
, we obtain that
\Gamma
is not live
— contradiction. Hence, we conclude that
\Gamma^{\prime}
is live.
∎

In the rest of this section, we will use the following alternative formulation of Def.
4.4
, that is more handy to construct proofs by coinduction.

Definition B.2 (Coinductive liveness).

\varphi
is a
{\sf p}
-liveness property
iff, whenever
\varphi(\Gamma)
:

•

[
LP
\&
]

\Gamma({\sf p})=(\sigma_{\sf p},{\mathsf{T}}_{\sf p})
with
{\mathsf{T}}_{\sf p}=\texternal_{i\in I}{{\sf q}?\ell_{i}({\mathsf{S}}_{i}).{{\mathsf{T}}_{i}}}
implies that, for all fair paths
(\Gamma_{j})_{j\in J}

such that
\Gamma_{0}=\Gamma
,
\exists h\in J,k\in I
such that
\Gamma\mathrel{\longrightarrow^{\!*}}\Gamma_{h-1}\longrightarrow\Gamma_{h}
, with:

(1)

\Gamma_{h-1}({\sf p})=(\sigma_{\sf p},{\mathsf{T}}_{\sf p})
and
\Gamma_{h-1}({\sf q})=({\sf p}!\ell_{k}({\mathsf{S}})\cdot\sigma^{\prime},{\mathsf{T}}_{\sf q})
;

(2)

\Gamma_{h}({\sf p})=(\sigma_{\sf p},{\mathsf{T}}_{k})
and
\Gamma_{h}({\sf q})=(\sigma^{\prime},{\mathsf{T}}_{\sf q})
;

•

[
LP
\oplus
]

\Gamma({\sf p})=({\sf q}!\ell({\mathsf{S}})\cdot\sigma,{\mathsf{T}}_{\sf p})
implies that, for all fair paths
(\Gamma_{j})_{j\in J}

such that
\Gamma_{0}=\Gamma
,
\exists h\in J,k\in I
such that
\Gamma\mathrel{\longrightarrow^{\!*}}\Gamma_{h-1}\longrightarrow\Gamma_{h}
, with:

(1)

\Gamma_{h-1}({\sf p})=({\sf q}!\ell({\mathsf{S}})\cdot\sigma^{\prime}_{\sf p},{\mathsf{T}}^{\prime}_{\sf p})
and
\Gamma_{h-1}({\sf q})=(\sigma_{{\sf q}},\texternal_{i\in I}{{\sf p}?\ell_{i}({\mathsf{S}}_{i}).{{\mathsf{T}}_{i}}})
;

(2)

\Gamma_{h}({\sf p})=(\sigma^{\prime}_{\sf p},{\mathsf{T}}^{\prime}_{\sf p})
and
\Gamma_{h}({\sf q})=(\sigma_{\sf q},{\mathsf{T}}_{k})
;

•

[
LP
\longrightarrow
]

\Gamma\longrightarrow\Gamma^{\prime}
implies
\varphi(\Gamma^{\prime})
.

We say that
\Gamma
is
{\sf p}
-live
iff
\varphi(\Gamma)
for some
{\sf p}
-liveness property
\varphi
.
We say that
\Gamma
is live
iff
\Gamma
is
{\sf p}
-live for all
{\sf p}\in dom(\Gamma)
.

Definition B.3 (SISO tree projections).

The
projections
of a SISO tree
{\mathsf{W}}
are SISO trees coinductively defined as follows:

\small\begin{array}[]{@{}r@{\;}c@{\;}l@{}}\mathtt{end}\mathbin{\upharpoonright^{!}}{\sf p}&=&\mathtt{end}\\
({\sf p}!\ell({\mathsf{S}}).{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime})\mathbin{\upharpoonright^{!}}{\sf p}&=&{\sf p}!\ell({\mathsf{S}}).({\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}\mathbin{\upharpoonright^{!}}{\sf p})\\
({\sf q}!\ell({\mathsf{S}}).{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime})\mathbin{\upharpoonright^{!}}{\sf p}&=&\left\{\begin{array}[]{@{}l@{\;\;}l@{}}{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}\mathbin{\upharpoonright^{!}}{\sf p}&\text{if ${\sf q}\!\neq\!{\sf p}\land{\sf p}\!\in\!\mathtt{pt}({\mathsf{W}}^{\prime})$}\\
\mathtt{end}&\text{if ${\sf q}\!\neq\!{\sf p}\land{\sf p}\!\not\in\!\mathtt{pt}({\mathsf{W}}^{\prime})$}\end{array}\right.\\
({\sf q}?\ell({\mathsf{S}}).{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime})\mathbin{\upharpoonright^{!}}{\sf p}&=&\left\{\begin{array}[]{@{}l@{\;\;}l@{}}{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}\mathbin{\upharpoonright^{!}}{\sf p}&\text{if ${\sf p}\!\in\!\mathtt{pt}({\mathsf{W}}^{\prime})$}\\
\mathtt{end}&\text{if ${\sf p}\!\not\in\!\mathtt{pt}({\mathsf{W}}^{\prime})$}\end{array}\right.\end{array}

\small\begin{array}[]{@{}r@{\;}c@{\;}l@{}}\mathtt{end}\mathbin{\upharpoonright^{?}}{\sf p}&=&\mathtt{end}\\
({\sf p}?\ell({\mathsf{S}}).{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime})\mathbin{\upharpoonright^{?}}{\sf p}&=&{\sf p}?\ell({\mathsf{S}}).({\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}\mathbin{\upharpoonright^{?}}{\sf p})\\
({\sf q}?\ell({\mathsf{S}}).{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime})\mathbin{\upharpoonright^{?}}{\sf p}&=&\left\{\begin{array}[]{@{}l@{\;\;}l@{}}{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}\mathbin{\upharpoonright^{?}}{\sf p}&\text{if ${\sf q}\!\neq\!{\sf p}\land{\sf p}\!\in\!\mathtt{pt}({\mathsf{W}}^{\prime})$}\\
\mathtt{end}&\text{if ${\sf q}\!\neq\!{\sf p}\land{\sf p}\!\not\in\!\mathtt{pt}({\mathsf{W}}^{\prime})$}\end{array}\right.\\
({\sf q}!\ell({\mathsf{S}}).{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime})\mathbin{\upharpoonright^{?}}{\sf p}&=&\left\{\begin{array}[]{@{}l@{\;\;}l@{}}{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}\mathbin{\upharpoonright^{?}}{\sf p}&\text{if ${\sf p}\!\in\!\mathtt{pt}({\mathsf{W}}^{\prime})$}\\
\mathtt{end}&\text{if ${\sf p}\!\not\in\!\mathtt{pt}({\mathsf{W}}^{\prime})$}\end{array}\right.\end{array}

Definition B.4.

The
strict refinement
\sqsubseteq
between SISO trees
is coinductively defined as:

{\sf p}?\ell({\mathsf{S}}).{\mathsf{W}}\sqsubseteq{\sf p}?\ell({\mathsf{S}}^{\prime}).{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}{\mathsf{S}}^{\prime}\leq\vcentcolon{\mathsf{S}}\quad{\mathsf{W}}\sqsubseteq{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}\qquad{\sf p}!\ell({\mathsf{S}}).{\mathsf{W}}\sqsubseteq{\sf p}!\ell({\mathsf{S}}^{\prime}).{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}{\mathsf{S}}\leq\vcentcolon{\mathsf{S}}^{\prime}\quad{\mathsf{W}}\sqsubseteq{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}\qquad\mathtt{end}\sqsubseteq\mathtt{end}

By Def.
B.4
,
\sqsubseteq
is a sub-relation of
\lesssim
that does not allow to change the order of inputs nor outputs. And by Prop.
B.5
below, the refinement
\lesssim
does not alter the order of inputs nor outputs from/to a given participant
{\sf p}
: the message reorderings allowed by
\lesssim
can only alter interactions targeting different participants, or outputs wrt. inputs to a same participant.

Proposition B.5.

For all
{\mathsf{W}}
and
{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}
and
{\sf p}
, if
{\mathsf{W}}\lesssim{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}
, then
({\mathsf{W}}\mathbin{\upharpoonright^{!}}{\sf p})\sqsubseteq({\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}\mathbin{\upharpoonright^{!}}{\sf p})

and
({\mathsf{W}}\mathbin{\upharpoonright^{?}}{\sf p})\sqsubseteq({\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}\mathbin{\upharpoonright^{?}}{\sf p})
.

Proof.

By coinduction on the derivation of
{\mathsf{W}}\lesssim{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}
.
∎

Proposition B.6.

Take any
{\sf p}
-live
\Gamma
with
\Gamma({\sf p})=(\sigma,{\mathsf{T}})
. Take
\Gamma^{\prime}=\Gamma\!\left\{{{\sf p}}\mapsto{(\sigma^{\prime},{\mathsf{T}}^{\prime})}\right\}
with
\sigma^{\prime}{\cdot}{\mathsf{T}}^{\prime}\leqslant\sigma{\cdot}{\mathsf{T}}
. Then, for any fair path
(\Gamma^{\prime}_{j})_{j\in J^{\prime}}
with
\Gamma^{\prime}_{0}=\Gamma^{\prime}
:

(1)

for all
n
, the first
n
inputs/outputs of
{\sf p}
along
(\Gamma^{\prime}_{j})_{j\in J^{\prime}}
match the first
n
input/output actions of some
{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}\in\llbracket{\mathsf{U}}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SI}}}
, with
{\mathsf{U}}^{\prime}\in\llbracket{\mathsf{T}}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SO}}}
;

(2)

there is a fair path
(\Gamma_{j})_{j\in J}
with
\Gamma_{0}=\Gamma
such that, for all
n
, the first
n
inputs/outputs of
{\sf p}
along
(\Gamma_{j})_{j\in J}
match the first
n
input/output actions of some
{\mathsf{W}}\in\llbracket{\mathsf{V}}\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SO}}}
, with
{\mathsf{V}}\in\llbracket{\mathsf{T}}\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SI}}}
; and

(3)

\sigma^{\prime}{\cdot}{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}\lesssim\sigma{\cdot}{\mathsf{W}}
.

Proof.

Before proceeding, with a slight abuse of notation, we “rewind”
\Gamma
and
\Gamma^{\prime}
, i.e., we consider
\Gamma
and
\Gamma^{\prime}
in the statement to be defined such that:

(16)

\displaystyle\Gamma({\sf p})=(\epsilon,\sigma{\cdot}{\mathsf{T}})\qquad\Gamma^{\prime}({\sf p})=(\epsilon,\sigma^{\prime}{\cdot}{\mathsf{T}}^{\prime})

i.e., the outputs in
\sigma
and
\sigma^{\prime}
are not yet queued; instead, the queues of
\Gamma({\sf p})
and
\Gamma^{\prime}({\sf p})
are empty, and the outputs
\sigma
and
\sigma^{\prime}
are prefixes of the respective types, and are about to be sent. We will “undo” this rewinding at the end of the proof, to obtain the final result.

Since
\sigma^{\prime}{\cdot}{\mathsf{T}}^{\prime}\leqslant\sigma{\cdot}{\mathsf{T}}
(by hypothesis), by Def.
3.7
we have:

(17)

\displaystyle\forall{\mathsf{U}}^{\prime}\in\llbracket\sigma^{\prime}{\cdot}{\mathsf{T}}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SO}}}\quad\forall{\mathsf{V}}\in\llbracket\sigma{\cdot}{\mathsf{T}}\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SI}}}\quad\exists{\mathsf{W}}_{2}\in\llbracket{\mathsf{U}}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SI}}}\quad\exists{\mathsf{W}}_{1}\in\llbracket{\mathsf{V}}\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SO}}}\qquad{\mathsf{W}}_{2}\lesssim{\mathsf{W}}_{1}

Observe that
{\mathsf{U}}^{\prime}
and
{\mathsf{V}}
in (
17
) are quantified over sets of session trees beginning with a same sequence of singleton selections (
\sigma^{\prime}
and
\sigma
, respectively). Therefore, such sequences of selections appear at the beginning of all SISO trees extracted from any such
{\mathbb{U}}^{\prime}
and
{\mathbb{V}}^{\prime}
, which means:

(18)

\displaystyle\forall{\mathsf{U}}^{\prime}\in\llbracket{\mathsf{T}}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SO}}}\quad\forall{\mathsf{V}}\in\llbracket{\mathsf{T}}\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SI}}}\quad\exists{\mathsf{W}}_{a}\in\llbracket{\mathsf{U}}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SI}}}\quad\exists{\mathsf{W}}_{b}\in\llbracket{\mathsf{V}}\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SO}}}\qquad\sigma^{\prime}{\cdot}{\mathsf{W}}_{a}\lesssim\sigma{\cdot}{\mathsf{W}}_{b}

We now define a procedure that, using the first
m
steps of a fair path
(\Gamma^{\prime}_{j})_{j\in J^{\prime}}
, constructs the beginning of a fair path
(\Gamma_{j})_{j\in J}
; also, the procedure ensures that:

(1)

in the first
m
steps of
(\Gamma^{\prime}_{j})_{j\in J^{\prime}}
, participant
{\sf p}
follows
\sigma^{\prime}
and then a prefix of some
{\mathsf{W}}_{a}
;

(2)

the path
(\Gamma_{j})_{j\in J}
is constructed so that
{\sf p}
follows
\sigma
and then some
{\mathsf{W}}_{b}
such that
\sigma^{\prime}{\cdot}{\mathsf{W}}_{a}\lesssim\sigma{\cdot}{\mathsf{W}}_{b}
; and

(3)

such
{\mathsf{W}}_{a}
and
{\mathsf{W}}_{b}
are quantified in (
18
).

In particular, the procedure lets each participant in
\Gamma^{\prime}\setminus{\sf p}=\Gamma\setminus{\sf p}
fire the same sequences of actions along
(\Gamma^{\prime}_{j})_{j\in J^{\prime}}
and
(\Gamma_{j})_{j\in J}
. The difference is that some actions may be delayed in
(\Gamma_{j})_{j\in J}
, because
{\sf p}
in
\Gamma^{\prime}
may anticipate some outputs and inputs (thanks to subtyping) wrt.
\Gamma
, and unlock some participants in
\Gamma^{\prime}
earlier than
\Gamma
: the procedure remembers any delayed actions (and their order), and fires them as soon as they become enabled, thus ensuring fairness.

For the procedure, we use:

•

\operatorname{{{\sf p}}-actions}^{\prime}({i})
: sequence of input/output actions performed by
{\sf p}
in
\Gamma^{\prime}
when it reaches
\Gamma^{\prime}_{i}
. We begin with
\operatorname{{{\sf p}}-actions}^{\prime}({0})=\epsilon
;

•

\gamma({i})
: number of reduction steps constructed along
(\Gamma_{j})_{j\in J}
when
\Gamma^{\prime}
has reached
\Gamma^{\prime}_{i}
. We begin with
\gamma({0})=0
;

•

\operatorname{{{\sf p}}-actions}({i})
: sequence of input/output actions performed by
{\sf p}
in
\Gamma
when
\Gamma^{\prime}
reaches
\Gamma^{\prime}_{i}
(note that, at this stage,
\Gamma
has reached
\Gamma_{\gamma({i})}
). We begin with
\operatorname{{{\sf p}}-actions}({0})=\epsilon
;

•

\mathcal{W}({i})
: set of SISO tree pairs
({\mathsf{W}}_{a},{\mathsf{W}}_{b})
such that, when
\Gamma^{\prime}
has reached
\Gamma^{\prime}_{i}
, and
\Gamma
has reached
\Gamma_{\gamma({i})}
, the sequence
\operatorname{{{\sf p}}-actions}^{\prime}({i})
matches a prefix of
\sigma^{\prime}{\cdot}{\mathsf{W}}_{a}
, and the sequence
\operatorname{{{\sf p}}-actions}({i})
matches a prefix of
\sigma{\cdot}{\mathsf{W}}_{b}
, and
\sigma{\cdot}{\mathsf{W}}_{a}\lesssim\sigma^{\prime}{\cdot}{\mathsf{W}}_{b}
. We begin with
\mathcal{W}({0})
containing all pairs
{\mathsf{W}}_{a}
and
{\mathsf{W}}_{b}
quantified in (
18
);

•

\mathit{delayed({i})}
: sequence of reduction labels that have been fired by
\Gamma^{\prime}
when it reaches
\Gamma^{\prime}_{i}
, but have not (yet) been fired by
\Gamma
when it reaches
\Gamma_{\gamma({i})}
. Labels in this sequence will be fired with the highest priority. We begin with
\mathit{delayed({0})}=\epsilon
.

We also use the following function:

\operatorname{tryFire}\!\left({d},{\Gamma},{d^{\prime}},{f},{s}\right)=\begin{cases}\text{if $d=\epsilon$ then $(d^{\prime},f,s)$}\\
\text{else if $\Gamma\xrightarrow{head(d)}\Gamma^{\prime}$ then $\operatorname{tryFire}\!\left({tail(d)},{\Gamma^{\prime}},{d^{\prime}},{f{\cdot}head(d)},{s{\cdot}\Gamma}\right)$}\\
\text{else $\operatorname{tryFire}\!\left({tail(d)},{\Gamma},{d^{\prime}{\cdot}head(d)},{f},{s}\right)$}\end{cases}

The function
\operatorname{tryFire}\!\left({d},{\Gamma},{d^{\prime}},{f},{s}\right)
tries to fire the environment reduction labels in the sequence
d
from
\Gamma
. The other parameters are used along recursive calls, to build the triplet that is returned by the function:

•

d^{\prime}
: a sequence of labels that have
not
been fired. It is extended each time the topmost label in
d
cannot be fired;

•

f
: a sequence of labels that
have
been fired. It is extended each time the topmost label in
d
is fired; and

•

s
: a sequence of typing environments reducing from one into another through the sequence of labels
f
.
It is extended each time
f
is extended (see above).

When
(\Gamma^{\prime}_{j})_{j\in J^{\prime}}
performs a step
m+1
, with a label
\alpha
such that
\Gamma^{\prime}_{m}\xrightarrow{\;\alpha\;}\Gamma^{\prime}_{m+1}
, we proceed as follows:

(1)

if
\alpha
does
not
involve an input/output by
{\sf p}
, i.e.,
\alpha={{\sf q}}{:}{{\sf r}}?{\ell}
or
\alpha={{\sf q}}{:}{{\sf r}}!{\ell}
for some
{\sf q},{\sf r}\neq{\sf p}
:

(a)

\operatorname{{{\sf p}}-actions}^{\prime}({m+1})=\operatorname{{{\sf p}}-actions}^{\prime}({m})

(2)

otherwise (i.e., if
\alpha={{\sf p}}{:}{{\sf q}}!{\ell}
or
\alpha={{\sf p}}{:}{{\sf q}}?{\ell}
):

(a)

\operatorname{{{\sf p}}-actions}^{\prime}({m+1})=\operatorname{{{\sf p}}-actions}^{\prime}({m}){\cdot}\alpha
;

(3)

(d^{\prime},f,s)=\operatorname{tryFire}\!\left({\mathit{delayed({m})}},{\Gamma_{\gamma({m})}},{\epsilon},{\epsilon},{\epsilon}\right)

(i.e., try to fire each delayed action)

(4)

\Gamma^{*}=\text{if $|s|>0$ then $last(s)$ else $\Gamma_{\gamma({m})}$}

(i.e.,
\Gamma^{*}
is the latest env. reached from
\Gamma
)

(5)

\forall i=1..|s|:\Gamma_{\gamma({m})+i}=s(i)
(i.e., we extend the path of
\Gamma
to reach
\Gamma^{*}
)

(6)

if
\Gamma^{*}\xrightarrow{\;\alpha\;}\Gamma^{\prime\prime}
(for some
\Gamma^{\prime\prime}
), we add
\Gamma^{\prime\prime}
to the path of
\Gamma
, as follows:

(a)

\Gamma_{\gamma({m})+|s|+1}=\Gamma^{\prime\prime}

(b)

\gamma({m+1})=\gamma({m})+|s|+1

(c)

\mathit{delayed({m+1})}=d^{\prime}

(d)

if
\alpha
does
not
involve an input/output by
{\sf p}
, i.e.,
\alpha={{\sf q}}{:}{{\sf r}}?{\ell}
or
\alpha={{\sf q}}{:}{{\sf r}}!{\ell}
for some
{\sf q},{\sf r}\neq{\sf p}
:

(i)

\operatorname{{{\sf p}}-actions}({m+1})=\operatorname{{{\sf p}}-actions}({m})
extended with all labels in
f
involving
{\sf p}
;

(e)

otherwise (i.e., if
\alpha={{\sf p}}{:}{{\sf q}}!{\ell}
or
\alpha={{\sf p}}{:}{{\sf q}}?{\ell}
):

(i)

\operatorname{{{\sf p}}-actions}({m+1})=\operatorname{{{\sf p}}-actions}({m})
extended with all labels in
f
involving
{\sf p}
, followed by
\alpha
;

(7)

otherwise (i.e., if there is no
\Gamma^{\prime\prime}
such that
\Gamma^{*}\xrightarrow{\;\alpha\;}\Gamma^{\prime\prime}
), we add
\alpha
to the delayed actions, as follows:

(a)

\operatorname{{{\sf p}}-actions}({m+1})=\operatorname{{{\sf p}}-actions}({m})
extended with all labels in
f
involving
{\sf p}

(b)

\gamma({m+1})=\gamma({m})+|s|

(c)

\mathit{delayed({m+1})}=d^{\prime}{\cdot}\alpha

(8)

\mathcal{W}({m+1})=\left\{({\mathsf{W}}_{a},{\mathsf{W}}_{b})\in\mathcal{W}({m})\;\middle|\;\begin{array}[]{@{}l@{}}\text{${\mathsf{W}}_{a}$ matches $\operatorname{{{\sf p}}-actions}^{\prime}({m+1})$}\\
\text{${\mathsf{W}}_{b}$ matches $\operatorname{{{\sf p}}-actions}({m+1})$}\end{array}\;\right\}

The procedure has the following invariants, for all
i\geq 0
:

(i1)

\operatorname{{{\sf p}}-actions}^{\prime}({i})
is the sequence of inputs/outputs of
{\sf p}
fired along the transitions from
\Gamma^{\prime}_{0}=\Gamma^{\prime}
to
\Gamma^{\prime}_{i}
(by construction);

(i2)

\operatorname{{{\sf p}}-actions}({i})
is the sequence of inputs/outputs of
{\sf p}
fired along the transitions from
\Gamma_{0}=\Gamma
to
\Gamma_{\gamma({i})}
(by construction);

(i3)

\forall({\mathsf{W}}_{a},{\mathsf{W}}_{b})\in\mathcal{W}({i})
:
\operatorname{{{\sf p}}-actions}^{\prime}({i})
matches the beginning of
{\mathsf{W}}_{a}

and
\operatorname{{{\sf p}}-actions}({i})
matches the beginning of
{\mathsf{W}}_{b}

(by construction);

(i4)

\mathcal{W}({i})\neq\emptyset
. We prove this claim by induction on
i
. When
i=0
, the result is immediate: by definition,
\mathcal{W}({0})
contains all pairs
({\mathsf{W}}_{a},{\mathsf{W}}_{b})
quantified in (
18
), and at least one such pair exists (otherwise, the hypothesis
\sigma^{\prime}{\cdot}{\mathsf{T}}^{\prime}\leqslant\sigma{\cdot}{\mathsf{T}}

would be contradicted).

In the inductive case
i=m+1
(for some
m
), by the induction hypothesis we have that
\mathcal{W}({m})\neq\emptyset
, and (by item
(i3)
above) for each
({\mathsf{W}}_{a},{\mathsf{W}}_{b})\in\mathcal{W}({i})
,
\operatorname{{{\sf p}}-actions}^{\prime}({m})
matches the beginning of
{\mathsf{W}}_{a}

and
\operatorname{{{\sf p}}-actions}({m})
matches the beginning of
{\mathsf{W}}_{b}
. Then, by contradiction, assume that
\mathcal{W}({m+1})=\emptyset
:
this means that
\Gamma({\sf p})
or
\Gamma^{\prime}({\sf p})
,
has performed an input or output that cannot be matched by
any pair in of trees
\mathcal{W}({i})
,
which means that

\sigma^{\prime}{\cdot}{\mathsf{T}}^{\prime}\not\leqslant\sigma{\cdot}{\mathsf{T}}
— contradiction.
Therefore, we conclude
\mathcal{W}({m+1})\neq\emptyset
.

We now obtain our thesis, by invariants
(i1)
–
(i4)
above: by taking any fair path
(\Gamma^{\prime}_{j})_{j\in J^{\prime}}
with
\Gamma^{\prime}_{0}=\Gamma^{\prime}
, and applying the procedure above for any
n=|\operatorname{{{\sf p}}-actions}^{\prime}({m})|
for some
m\geq n
such that
m\in J^{\prime}
(i.e., for any number
n
of reductions of
{\sf p}
that are performed along the path, within
m
steps), we find some
{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}
such that
\sigma^{\prime}{\cdot}{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}
matches
\operatorname{{{\sf p}}-actions}^{\prime}({m})
, and we construct the beginning of a fair path
(\Gamma_{j})_{j\in J}
where
{\sf p}
behaves according to some
{\mathsf{W}}
such that
\sigma{\cdot}{\mathsf{W}}
matches
\operatorname{{{\sf p}}-actions}({m})
, and such that
\sigma^{\prime}{\cdot}{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}\lesssim\sigma{\cdot}{\mathsf{W}}
; and by increasing
n
and
m
, we correspondingly extend the sequences
\operatorname{{{\sf p}}-actions}^{\prime}({m})
and
\operatorname{{{\sf p}}-actions}({m})
.

To conclude the proof, we need to undo the “rewinding” in (
16
). Consider any path
(\Gamma^{\prime}_{j})_{j\in J^{\prime}}
,
and the corresponding
(\Gamma_{j})_{j\in J}
obtained with
\Gamma^{\prime},\Gamma
rewinded as in (
16
): we can undo the rewinding of
\sigma^{\prime}
and
\sigma
by:

(1)

choosing a path
(\Gamma^{\prime}_{j})_{j\in J^{\prime}}
that fires the outputs in
\sigma^{\prime}
in its first reductions, thus reaching the “original”
\Gamma^{\prime}
from the statement;

(2)

then, for such a path of
\Gamma^{\prime}
, the procedure gives us the beginning of a corresponding live path
(\Gamma_{j})_{j\in J}
that fires all outputs in
\sigma
, within
k
steps (for some
k
). By induction on
k
and
\sigma
, we can reorder the first
k
actions of
(\Gamma_{j})_{j\in J}
so that the outputs in
\sigma
are fired first, thus reaching the “original”
\Gamma
from the statement;

(3)

after the outputs in
\sigma
and
\sigma^{\prime}
are fired along such paths of
\Gamma^{\prime}
and
\Gamma
, we have that
{\sf p}
follows SISO trees
{\mathsf{W}}_{a}
and
{\mathsf{W}}_{b}
extracted respectively from
{\mathsf{T}}^{\prime}
and
{\mathsf{T}}
, and we have
\sigma{\cdot}{\mathsf{W}}_{a}\lesssim\sigma^{\prime}{\cdot}{\mathsf{W}}_{b}
: therefore, such paths satisfy the statement.

∎

Proposition B.7.

Take any
{\sf p}
-live
\Gamma
with
\Gamma({\sf p})=(\sigma,{\mathsf{T}})
. Take
\Gamma^{\prime}=\Gamma\!\left\{{{\sf p}}\mapsto{(\sigma^{\prime},{\mathsf{T}}^{\prime})}\right\}
with
\sigma^{\prime}{\cdot}{\mathsf{T}}^{\prime}\leqslant\sigma{\cdot}{\mathsf{T}}
. Assume that there is a fair path
(\Gamma^{\prime}_{j})_{j\in J^{\prime}}
with
\Gamma^{\prime}_{0}=\Gamma^{\prime}
such that, for some
{\sf q},\ell,{\mathsf{S}}_{\sf r}
:

(19)

\displaystyle\forall j\in J^{\prime},\sigma_{\sf r},{\mathsf{T}}_{\sf r}:\;\Gamma^{\prime}_{j}({\sf r})\neq({\sf q}!\ell({\mathsf{S}}_{\sf r}){\cdot}\sigma_{{\sf r}},{\mathsf{T}}_{\sf r})

Then, there is a fair path
(\Gamma_{j})_{j\in J}
with
\Gamma_{0}=\Gamma
such that,

(20)

\displaystyle\forall j\in J,\sigma_{\sf r},{\mathsf{T}}_{\sf r}:\;\Gamma_{j}({\sf r})\neq({\sf q}!\ell({\mathsf{S}}_{\sf r}){\cdot}\sigma_{{\sf r}},{\mathsf{T}}_{\sf r})

Proof.

Take the path
(\Gamma^{\prime}_{j})_{j\in J^{\prime}}
. By Prop.
B.6
there is a SISO tree
{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}
describing the first
n
reductions (for any
n
) of
{\sf p}
in
\Gamma^{\prime}
, and a corresponding SISO tree
{\mathsf{W}}
such that
\sigma^{\prime}{\cdot}{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}\lesssim\sigma{\cdot}{\mathsf{W}}
describing the reductions of
{\sf p}
in
\Gamma
along a fair path
(\Gamma_{j})_{j\in J}
. By Prop.
B.5
,
\sigma^{\prime}{\cdot}{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}
contains the same sequences of per-participant inputs and outputs of
\sigma{\cdot}{\mathsf{W}}
; moreover, by Def.
3.2
,
{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}
can perform the outputs appearing in
{\mathsf{W}}
, possibly earlier. And by the path construction in Prop.
B.6
, each participant
{\sf q}\in dom(\Gamma)=dom(\Gamma^{\prime})
(with
{\sf q}\neq{\sf p}
) can fire along
(\Gamma^{\prime}_{j})_{j\in J^{\prime}}
at least the same outputs and the same inputs (in the same respective order) that it fires along
(\Gamma_{j})_{j\in J}
.
Now, observe that by hypothesis (
19
), along the fair path
(\Gamma^{\prime}_{j})_{j\in J^{\prime}}
, participant
{\sf r}
never produces an output
{\sf q}!\ell({\mathsf{S}}_{\sf r})
; but then, along
(\Gamma_{j})_{j\in J}
, participant
{\sf r}
never produces the output
{\sf q}!\ell({\mathsf{S}}_{\sf r})
, either. Therefore, we obtain (
20
).∎

Proposition B.8.

Take any
{\sf p}
-live
\Gamma
with
\Gamma({\sf p})=(\sigma,{\mathsf{T}})
. Take
\Gamma^{\prime}=\Gamma\!\left\{{{\sf p}}\mapsto{(\sigma^{\prime},{\mathsf{T}}^{\prime})}\right\}
with
\sigma^{\prime}{\cdot}{\mathsf{T}}^{\prime}\leqslant\sigma{\cdot}{\mathsf{T}}
. Assume that there is a fair path
(\Gamma^{\prime}_{j})_{j\in J^{\prime}}
with
\Gamma^{\prime}_{0}=\Gamma
such that, for some
{\sf q},I,\ell_{i},{\mathsf{S}}_{{\sf r},i},{\mathsf{T}}_{{\sf r},i}\;(i\in I)
:

(21)

\displaystyle\forall j\in J^{\prime},\sigma_{\sf r}:\;\Gamma^{\prime}_{j}({\sf r})\neq\left(\sigma_{{\sf r}},\texternal_{i\in I}{\sf q}?\ell_{i}({\mathsf{S}}_{{\sf r},i}).{\mathsf{T}}_{{\sf r},i}\right)

Then, there is a fair path
(\Gamma_{j})_{j\in J}
with
\Gamma_{0}=\Gamma
such that:

(22)

\displaystyle\forall j\in J,\sigma_{\sf r}:\;\Gamma_{j}({\sf r})\neq\left(\sigma_{{\sf r}},\texternal_{i\in I}{\sf q}?\ell_{i}({\mathsf{S}}_{{\sf r},i}).{\mathsf{T}}_{{\sf r},i}\right)

Proof.

Similar to Prop.
B.7
. ∎

Definition B.9 (Queue output prefixing).

We write
\sigma{\cdot}{\mathsf{T}}
for the session tree obtained by prefixing
{\mathsf{T}}
with the sequence of singleton internal choices matching the sequence of outputs in
\sigma
.

Lemma B.10.

If
\Gamma,{\sf p}{:}(\sigma,{\mathsf{T}})
is
{\sf p}
-live and
\sigma^{\prime}{\cdot}{\mathsf{T}}^{\prime}\leqslant\sigma{\cdot}{\mathsf{T}}
, then
\Gamma,{\sf p}{:}(\sigma^{\prime},{\mathsf{T}}^{\prime})
is
{\sf p}
-live.

Proof.

Let
\mathord{\mathcal{L}}
be the set of all
{\sf p}
-live typing contexts, i.e., the largest
{\sf p}
-liveness property by Def.
B.2
. Consider the following property:

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}=\mathord{\mathcal{L}}\,\cup\,\mathord{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}\quad\text{where}\;\mathord{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}\,=\,\left\{\Gamma\!\left\{{{\sf p}}\mapsto{(\sigma^{\prime},{\mathsf{T}}^{\prime})}\right\}\;\middle|\;\begin{array}[]{@{}l@{}}\Gamma\in\mathord{\mathcal{L}}\\
\Gamma({\sf p})=(\sigma,{\mathsf{T}})\\
\sigma^{\prime}{\cdot}{\mathsf{T}}^{\prime}\leqslant\sigma{\cdot}{\mathsf{T}}\end{array}\right\}

We now prove that
\mathcal{P}
is a
{\sf p}
-liveness property — i.e., we prove that each element of
\mathcal{P}
satisfies the clauses of Def.
B.2
. Since all elements of
\mathord{\mathcal{L}}
trivially satisfy the clauses, we only need to examine the elements of
\mathord{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}
: to this purpose, we consider each
\Gamma^{\prime}\in\mathord{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}
, and observe that, by (
B.1
), there exist
\Gamma,\sigma,{\mathsf{T}},\sigma^{\prime},{\mathsf{T}}^{\prime}
such that:

(26)

\displaystyle\Gamma^{\prime}=\Gamma\!\left\{{{\sf p}}\mapsto{(\sigma^{\prime},{\mathsf{T}}^{\prime})}\right\}

(27)

\displaystyle\Gamma({\sf p})=(\sigma,{\mathsf{T}})

(28)

\displaystyle\sigma^{\prime}{\cdot}{\mathsf{T}}^{\prime}\leqslant\sigma{\cdot}{\mathsf{T}}

By (
28
) and Def.
3.7
,
we also have:

(29)

\displaystyle\forall{\mathsf{U}}^{\prime}\in\llbracket\sigma^{\prime}{\cdot}{\mathsf{T}}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SO}}}\quad\forall{\mathsf{V}}\in\llbracket\sigma{\cdot}{\mathsf{T}}\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SI}}}\quad\exists{\mathsf{W}}_{1}\in\llbracket{\mathsf{U}}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SI}}}\quad\exists{\mathsf{W}}_{2}\in\llbracket{\mathsf{V}}\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SO}}}\qquad{\mathsf{W}}_{1}\lesssim{\mathsf{W}}_{2}

Observe that by the definitions of
\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SO}}}
and
\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SI}}}
on page
3.1
, and by Def.
3.2
, all relations
{\mathsf{W}}_{1}\lesssim{\mathsf{W}}_{2}
in (
29
) are yielded by a same refinement rule. We proceed by cases on such a rule.

•

Case
[
ref-end
]
. In this case, we have
\sigma^{\prime}{\cdot}{\mathsf{T}}^{\prime}\lesssim\sigma{\cdot}{\mathsf{T}}=\mathtt{end}
, which means
\sigma^{\prime}=\sigma=\epsilon
and
{\mathsf{T}}^{\prime}={\mathsf{T}}=\mathtt{end}
. Therefore, by (
27
) and (
26
),
\Gamma^{\prime}=\Gamma\in\mathord{\mathcal{L}}
, and thus, we conclude that
\Gamma^{\prime}
satisfies the clauses of Def.
B.2
.

•

Case
[
ref-in
]
. In this case, we have:

(30)

\displaystyle\sigma=\sigma^{\prime}=\epsilon

\displaystyle\exists{\sf q},I,I^{\prime},\ell_{i},{\mathsf{S}}_{i},{\mathsf{S}}^{\prime}_{i},{\mathsf{T}}_{i},{\mathsf{T}}^{\prime}_{i}\;\text{such that:}

(31)

\displaystyle\qquad{\mathsf{T}}^{\prime}=\texternal_{i\in I\cup I^{\prime}}{\sf q}?\ell_{i}({\mathsf{S}}^{\prime}_{i}).{\mathsf{T}}^{\prime}_{i}\hskip 11.74988pt\text{and}\hskip 11.74988pt{\mathsf{T}}=\texternal_{i\in I}{\sf q}?\ell_{i}({\mathsf{S}}_{i}).{\mathsf{T}}_{i}

(32)

\displaystyle\qquad\forall i\in I:{\mathsf{S}}_{i}\leq\vcentcolon{\mathsf{S}}^{\prime}_{i}\quad\text{and}\quad{\mathsf{T}}^{\prime}_{i}\lesssim{\mathsf{T}}_{i}

We now show that
\Gamma^{\prime}
satisfies all clauses of Def.
B.2
:

–

clause
[
LP
\&
]
. Since
\Gamma
is
{\sf p}
-live, we know that, for all fair paths
(\Gamma_{j})_{j\in J}

such that
\Gamma_{0}=\Gamma
,
\exists h\in J,k\in I
such that
\Gamma\mathrel{\longrightarrow^{\!*}}\Gamma_{h-1}\longrightarrow\Gamma_{h}
, with:

(1)

\Gamma_{h-1}({\sf p})=(\sigma,{\mathsf{T}})
and
\Gamma_{h-1}({\sf q})=({\sf p}!\ell({\mathsf{S}}_{\sf q})\cdot\sigma_{\sf q},{\mathsf{T}}_{\sf q})
with
\ell=\ell_{i}
and
{\mathsf{S}}_{\sf q}\leqslant{\mathsf{S}}_{i}
for some
i\in I

(2)

\Gamma_{h}({\sf p})=(\sigma,{\mathsf{T}}_{i})
and
\Gamma_{h}({\sf q})=(\sigma_{\sf q},{\mathsf{T}}_{\sf q})
.

Now, for all such
(\Gamma_{j})_{j\in J}
, we can construct a path corresponding
(\Gamma^{\prime}_{j})_{j\in J^{\prime}}
such that:

*

\Gamma^{\prime}_{0}=\Gamma^{\prime}

*

\forall n\in 0..h-1:\Gamma^{\prime}_{n}=\Gamma_{n}\!\left\{{{\sf p}}\mapsto{(\sigma^{\prime},{\mathsf{T}}^{\prime})}\right\}
;

*

\Gamma^{\prime}_{h}=\Gamma_{h}\!\left\{{{\sf p}}\mapsto{(\sigma^{\prime},{\mathsf{T}}^{\prime}_{i})}\right\}
(i.e., the queue of
\Gamma_{h}({\sf p})
is preserved in
\Gamma^{\prime}_{h}({\sf p})
);

*

the rest of the path after the
h
-th reduction is arbitrary (but fair).

Observe that
(\Gamma^{\prime}_{j})_{j\in J^{\prime}}
is fair, reproduces the first
h
steps of
(\Gamma_{j})_{j\in J}
, and triggers the top-level input of
\Gamma^{\prime}({\sf p})
. Also, observe that
all
fair paths from
\Gamma^{\prime}
eventually trigger the top-level input of
\Gamma^{\prime}({\sf p})
: in fact, if (by contradiction) we assume that there is a fair path from
\Gamma^{\prime}
that never triggers
\Gamma^{\prime}({\sf p})
’s input, then (by inverting the construction above) we would find a corresponding fair path of
\Gamma
that never triggers
\Gamma({\sf p})
’s input — i.e., we would conclude that
\Gamma
is
not

{\sf p}
-live (contradiction).
Thus, we conclude that
\Gamma^{\prime}
satisfies clause
[
LP
\&
]
of Def.
B.2
;

–

clause
[
LP
\oplus
]
. The clause is vacuously satisfied;

–

clause
[
LP
\longrightarrow
]
. Assume
\Gamma^{\prime}\longrightarrow\Gamma^{\prime\prime}
. We have two possibilities:

(a)

the reduction does
not
involve
{\sf p}
. Then, there is a corresponding reduction
\Gamma\longrightarrow\Gamma^{\prime\prime\prime}
with
\Gamma^{\prime\prime}=\Gamma^{\prime\prime\prime}\!\left\{{{\sf p}}\mapsto{(\sigma^{\prime},{\mathsf{T}}^{\prime})}\right\}
. Observe that
\Gamma^{\prime\prime\prime}
is
{\sf p}
-live, and thus,
\Gamma^{\prime\prime\prime}\in\mathord{\mathcal{L}}
; therefore, by (
B.1
), we have
\Gamma^{\prime\prime}\in\mathord{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathcal{P}
. Thus, we conclude that
\Gamma^{\prime}
satisfies clause
[
LP
\longrightarrow
]
of Def.
B.2
;

(b)

the reduction
does
involve
{\sf p}
. There are three sub-cases:

(i)

{\sf p}
is enqueuing an output toward some participant
{\sf r}
. This case is impossible, by (
31
);

(ii)

{\sf p}
is receiving an input from
{\sf q}
, i.e.,
\Gamma^{\prime}({\sf q})=\Gamma({\sf q})=({\sf p}!\ell_{i}({\mathsf{S}}_{\sf q})\cdot\sigma_{\sf q},{\mathsf{T}}_{\sf q})
with
{\mathsf{S}}_{\sf q}\leq\vcentcolon{\mathsf{S}}^{\prime}_{i}
and
\Gamma^{\prime\prime}({\sf p})=(\sigma^{\prime},{\mathsf{T}}^{\prime}_{i})
(for some
i\in I
). Notice that, since
\Gamma
is
{\sf p}
-live, the same output from
{\sf q}
can be received by
{\sf p}
in
\Gamma
, and thus, there is a corresponding reduction
\Gamma\longrightarrow\Gamma^{\prime\prime\prime}
where
\Gamma^{\prime\prime\prime}({\sf p})=(\sigma,{\mathsf{T}}_{i})
and
\Gamma^{\prime\prime}=\Gamma^{\prime\prime\prime}\!\left\{{{\sf p}}\mapsto{(\sigma^{\prime},{\mathsf{T}}^{\prime}_{i})}\right\}
. Observe that
\Gamma^{\prime\prime\prime}
is also
{\sf p}
-live, and thus,
\Gamma^{\prime\prime\prime}\in\mathord{\mathcal{L}}
; therefore, by (
32
) and (
B.1
), we have
\Gamma^{\prime\prime}\in\mathord{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathcal{P}
. Hence, we conclude that
\Gamma^{\prime}
satisfies clause
[
LP
\longrightarrow
]
of Def.
B.2
;

(iii)

one of
{\sf p}
’s queued outputs in
\sigma^{\prime}
is received by another participant. This case is impossible, by (
30
).

•

Case
[
ref-
\mathcal{A}
]
. In this case, we have:

(33)

\displaystyle\sigma=\sigma^{\prime}=\epsilon

(34)

\displaystyle\exists I,I^{\prime},\ell_{i},{\mathsf{S}}^{\prime}_{i},{\mathsf{T}}^{\prime}_{i}:\;{\mathsf{T}}^{\prime}=\texternal_{i\in I\cup I^{\prime}}{\sf q}?\ell_{i}({\mathsf{S}}^{\prime}_{i}).{\mathsf{T}}^{\prime}_{i}

\displaystyle\text{for all ${\mathsf{W}}_{1},{\mathsf{W}}_{2}$ in \eqref{eq:subttt-liveness:prop:siso-trees}, }\exists i\in I,\mathcal{A}^{({\sf q})},\ell_{i},{\mathsf{S}}_{i},{\mathsf{S}}^{\prime}_{i},{\mathsf{W}},{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime},\;\text{such that:}

(35)

(36)

clause
[

\&
]
. We proceed by contradiction: we show that if there is a fair path

{\sf p}
-live. Such a hypothetical path
