\displaystyle\forall j\in J:\not\exists{\mathsf{T}}_{\sf q},\sigma_{\sf q},{\mathsf{S}}_{\sf q}:\Gamma_{j}({\sf q})=({\sf p}!\ell_{i}({\mathsf{S}}_{\sf q}){\cdot}\sigma_{{\sf q}},{\mathsf{T}}_{\sf q})\text{ with }{\mathsf{S}}_{\sf q}\leq\vcentcolon{\mathsf{S}}_{i}\text{ (for any $i\in I$)}

\&

of Def.

;

clause
[

\oplus
]
. The clause is vacuously satisfied;

clause
[

\longrightarrow
]
. Assume

);

(ii)

{\sf p}

(39)

\displaystyle\Gamma^{\prime}({\sf q})=\Gamma({\sf q})=({\sf p}!\ell_{i}({\mathsf{S}}_{\sf q})\cdot\sigma_{\sf q},{\mathsf{T}}_{\sf q})\;\text{ with ${\mathsf{S}}_{\sf q}\leq\vcentcolon{\mathsf{S}}^{\prime}_{i}$}

(40)

\displaystyle\Gamma^{\prime}\xrightarrow{{{\sf p}}{:}{{\sf q}}?{\ell_{i}}}\Gamma^{\prime\prime}\;\text{ with $\Gamma^{\prime\prime}({\sf p})=(\sigma^{\prime},{\mathsf{T}}^{\prime}_{i})$}

By Prop.

, the fair paths of
\Gamma
that match some witness

in (

) eventually perform the reduction
\alpha
above. But then, consider the session tree
{\mathsf{T}}^{*}
that only has SISO trees similar to

in (

), except that each one performs the input
{\sf q}?\ell_{j}({\mathsf{S}})
immediately — i.e.:

\displaystyle\hskip-14.22636pt\begin{array}[]{@{}l@{}}\forall{\mathsf{U}}^{\prime}\in\llbracket\sigma^{\prime}{\cdot}{\mathsf{T}}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SO}}}\quad\forall{\mathsf{V}}^{*}\in\llbracket\sigma{\cdot}{\mathsf{T}}^{*}\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SI}}}\\
\exists{\mathsf{W}}_{1}={\sf q}?\ell_{i}({\mathsf{S}}^{\prime}_{i}).{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}\in\llbracket{\mathsf{U}}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SI}}}\quad\exists{\mathsf{W}}^{*}={\sf q}?\ell_{i}({\mathsf{S}}_{i}).\mathcal{A}^{({\sf q})}.{\mathsf{W}}\in\llbracket{\mathsf{V}}^{*}\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SO}}}\quad{\mathsf{W}}_{1}\lesssim{\mathsf{W}}^{*}\end{array}

And now, consider the typing environment

{\sf p}
-live: it realises (part of) the fair paths of

, except that it consumes

. Now, consider
\Gamma^{\prime\prime\prime}
such that
\Gamma^{*}\xrightarrow{{{\sf p}}{:}{{\sf q}}?{\ell_{i}}}\Gamma^{\prime\prime\prime}
: by clause
[

\longrightarrow
]
of Def.
B.2
,
we have
\Gamma^{\prime\prime\prime}\in\mathord{\mathcal{L}}
. Also, we have
\Gamma^{\prime\prime\prime}({\sf p})=(\sigma,{\mathsf{T}}^{**})
such that:

\displaystyle\hskip-14.22636pt\begin{array}[]{@{}l@{}}\forall{\mathsf{U}}^{\prime}\in\llbracket\sigma^{\prime}{\cdot}{\mathsf{T}}^{\prime}_{i}\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SO}}}\quad\forall{\mathsf{V}}^{**}\in\llbracket\sigma{\cdot}{\mathsf{T}}^{**}\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SI}}}\\
\exists{\mathsf{W}}_{1}={\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}\in\llbracket{\mathsf{U}}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SI}}}\quad\exists{\mathsf{W}}^{*}=\mathcal{A}^{({\sf q})}.{\mathsf{W}}\in\llbracket{\mathsf{V}}^{**}\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SO}}}\quad{\mathsf{W}}_{1}\lesssim{\mathsf{W}}^{*}\end{array}

(by Def.
4.3
and (
36
))

Therefore, by Def.
3.7
, we have
\Gamma^{\prime\prime\prime}({\sf p})=(\sigma,{\mathsf{T}}^{**})
, and
\Gamma^{\prime\prime}=\Gamma^{\prime\prime\prime}\!\left\{{{\sf p}}\mapsto{(\sigma^{\prime},{\mathsf{T}}^{\prime}_{i})}\right\}
, with
\sigma^{\prime}{\cdot}{\mathsf{T}}^{\prime}_{i}\leqslant\sigma{\cdot}{\mathsf{T}}^{**}

Proposition B.11.

Assume that
\Gamma
is live, and that
\Gamma({\sf p})=(\sigma,{\mathsf{T}})
. If
\sigma^{\prime}{\cdot}{\mathsf{T}}^{\prime}\leqslant\sigma{\cdot}{\mathsf{T}}
, then
\Gamma\!\left\{{{\sf p}}\mapsto{(\sigma^{\prime},{\mathsf{T}}^{\prime})}\right\}
is live.

Proof.

By Def.
B.2
, we need to show that
\Gamma^{\prime}
is
{\sf q}
-live for all participants
{\sf q}\in dom(\Gamma^{\prime})=dom(\Gamma)
. By hypothesis, we know that
\Gamma
is
{\sf q}
-live for all participants
{\sf q}\in dom(\Gamma)
. By Lemma
B.10
, we know that
\Gamma^{\prime}
is
{\sf p}
-live — and in particular,
\Gamma^{\prime}
is in the
{\sf p}
-liveness property
\mathcal{P}
defined in (
B.1
). We are left to prove that
\Gamma^{\prime}
is
{\sf q}
-live for all other participants
{\sf q}\neq{\sf p}
.

By contradiction, assume that
\Gamma^{\prime}
is
not

{\sf q}
-live for some
{\sf q}\in dom(\Gamma^{\prime})
. This means that we can find some
\Gamma^{\prime\prime\prime}
such that
\Gamma^{\prime}\mathrel{\longrightarrow^{\!*}}\Gamma^{\prime\prime\prime}
and
\Gamma^{\prime\prime\prime}
is not
{\sf q}
-live because it violates clause
[
LP
\&
]
or
[
LP
\oplus
]
of Def.
B.2
. Now, consider the
{\sf p}
-liveness property
\mathcal{P}
in (
B.1
): since
\mathcal{P}
contains
\Gamma
, it also contains
\Gamma^{\prime\prime\prime}
(by iteration of clause
[
LP
\longrightarrow
]
of Def.
B.2
), and by (
B.1
), there exists a corresponding
{\sf p}
-live
\Gamma^{\prime\prime}
such that:

(77)

\displaystyle\Gamma^{\prime\prime\prime}\setminus{\sf p}=\Gamma^{\prime\prime}\setminus{\sf p}

(78)

\displaystyle\Gamma^{\prime\prime\prime}({\sf p})=(\sigma^{\prime\prime\prime},{\mathsf{T}}^{\prime\prime\prime})\;\text{ and }\;\Gamma^{\prime\prime}({\sf p})=(\sigma^{\prime\prime},{\mathsf{T}}^{\prime\prime})\;\text{ such that }\;\sigma^{\prime\prime\prime}{\cdot}{\mathsf{T}}^{\prime\prime\prime}\leqslant\sigma^{\prime\prime}{\cdot}{\mathsf{T}}^{\prime\prime}

Let us examine the two (non-mutually exclusive) cases that can make
\Gamma^{\prime\prime\prime}
not
{\sf q}
-live:

•

\Gamma^{\prime\prime\prime}
violates clause
[
LP
\&
]

of Def.
B.2
. This means that in
\Gamma^{\prime\prime\prime}
there is a participant
{\sf r}
with a top-level external choice from some participant
{\sf q}
, but some fair path of
\Gamma^{\prime\prime\prime}
never enqueues a corresponding output by
{\sf q}
. In this case, similarly to the proof of Prop.
B.10
(case
[
ref-
\mathcal{A}
]
[
LP
\&
]
), we conclude that
\Gamma^{\prime\prime}
is
not

{\sf r}
-live, hence not live — contradiction;

•

\Gamma^{\prime\prime\prime}
violates clause
[
LP
\oplus
]

of Def.
B.2
. This means that in
\Gamma^{\prime\prime\prime}
there is a participant
{\sf r}
with a top-level queued output toward participant
{\sf q}
, but some fair path of
\Gamma^{\prime\prime\prime}
where
{\sf q}
never consumes the message. In this case, similarly to the proof of Prop.
B.10
(case
[
ref-out
]
[
LP
\oplus
]
), we conclude that
\Gamma^{\prime\prime}
is
not

{\sf r}
-live, hence not live — contradiction.

Summing up: if we assume that
\Gamma^{\prime}
is
not

{\sf q}
-live for some
{\sf q}\in dom(\Gamma^{\prime})
, then we derive a contradiction. Therefore, we obtain that
\Gamma^{\prime}
is
{\sf q}
-live for all
{\sf q}\in dom(\Gamma^{\prime})
. Thus, by Def.
B.2
, we conclude that
\Gamma^{\prime}
is live.
∎

See
4.7

Proof.

Assume
dom(\Gamma)\cap dom(\Gamma^{\prime})=\{{\sf p}_{1},{\sf p}_{2},\ldots,{\sf p}_{n}\}
. We first first show that:

\Gamma_{i}\;=\;\Gamma\!\left\{{{\sf p}_{1}}\mapsto{\Gamma^{\prime}({\sf p}_{1})}\right\}\ldots\!\left\{{{\sf p}_{2}}\mapsto{\Gamma^{\prime}({\sf p}_{2})}\right\}\ldots\!\left\{{{\sf p}_{i}}\mapsto{\Gamma^{\prime}({\sf p}_{i})}\right\}\;\text{ is live, for all $i\in 0..n$}

We proceed by induction on
i\in 0..n
. The base case
i=0
is trivial: we apply no updates to
\Gamma
, which is live by hypothesis.

In the inductive case
i=m+1
, we have (by the induction hypothesis) that
\Gamma_{m}
is live. By Definition of
\Gamma^{\prime}\leqslant\Gamma
(see page
4.2
), we know that
\Gamma^{\prime}({\sf p}_{i})\leqslant\Gamma({\sf p}_{i})
.
Therefore, by Prop.
B.11
, we obtain that
\Gamma_{i}
is live.

To conclude the proof, consider the set
dom(\Gamma^{\prime})\setminus dom(\Gamma_{n})=dom(\Gamma^{\prime})\setminus dom(\Gamma)=\{{\sf q}_{1},{\sf q}_{2},\ldots,{\sf q}_{k}\}
: it contains all participants that are in
\Gamma^{\prime}
, but not in
\Gamma
. By Definition of
\Gamma^{\prime}\leqslant\Gamma
(see page
4.2
) we know that
\forall i\in 1..k:\Gamma^{\prime}({\sf q}_{i})\equiv(\epsilon,\mathtt{end})
. Therefore, if we extend
\Gamma_{n}
by adding an entry
{\sf q}_{i}:(\epsilon,\mathtt{end})
for each
i\in 1..k
, we obtain an environment
\Gamma^{\prime\prime}
such that
\Gamma^{\prime\prime}\equiv\Gamma_{n}
and
\Gamma^{\prime\prime}\equiv\Gamma^{\prime}
— hence,
\Gamma_{n}\equiv\Gamma^{\prime}
. Therefore, since
\Gamma_{n}
is live, by Lemma
B.1
we conclude that
\Gamma^{\prime}
is live.
∎

B.2.
Proofs of Subject Reduction and Type Safety

Lemma B.12 (Typing Inversion).

Let
\Theta\vdash P:{\mathsf{T}}
: Then,

(1)

P=\mu X.P_{1}
implies
\Theta,X:{\mathsf{T}}_{1}\vdash P_{1}:{\mathsf{T}}_{1}
and
{\mathsf{T}}_{1}\leqslant{\mathsf{T}}
for some
{\mathsf{T}}_{1};

(2)

P=\sum_{i\in I}{\sf q}?\ell_{i}(x_{i}).{P_{i}}
implies

(a)

\texternal_{i\in I}{\sf q}?\ell_{i}({\mathsf{S}}_{i}).{{\mathsf{T}}_{i}}\leqslant{\mathsf{T}}
and

(b)

\forall i\in I

\Theta,x_{i}:{\mathsf{S}}_{i}\vdash P_{i}:{\mathsf{T}}_{i}
;

(3)

P={\sf q}!\ell\langle\mathsf{e}\rangle.P_{1}
implies

(a)

{\sf q}!\ell({\mathsf{S}}_{1}).{\mathsf{T}}_{1}\leqslant{\mathsf{T}}
and

(b)

\Theta\vdash\mathsf{e}:{\mathsf{S}}
and
{\mathsf{S}}_{1}\leq\vcentcolon{\mathsf{S}}
and

(c)

\Theta\vdash P_{1}:{\mathsf{T}}_{1};

(4)

P=\mathsf{if}\penalty\ \mathsf{e}\penalty\ \mathsf{then}\penalty\ P_{1}\penalty\ \mathsf{else}\penalty\ P_{2}
implies

(a)

\Theta\vdash\mathsf{e}:\mathtt{bool}
and

(b)

\Theta\vdash P_{1}:{\mathsf{T}}

(c)

\Theta\vdash P_{2}:{\mathsf{T}}

Let
\vdash h:\sigma
. Then:

(5)

h=\varnothing
implies
\sigma=\epsilon

(6)

h=({\sf q},\ell(\mathsf{v}))\!\cdot\!h_{1}
implies
\vdash\mathsf{v}:{\mathsf{S}}
and
\sigma\equiv{\sf q}!\ell({\mathsf{S}})\!\cdot\!\sigma^{\prime}
and
\vdash h_{1}:\sigma^{\prime}.

Let
\Gamma\vdash\mathcal{M}
. Then:

(7)

If
\Gamma\vdash\prod_{i\in I}({\sf p}_{i}\triangleleft P_{i}\penalty\ |\penalty\ {\sf p}_{i}\triangleleft h_{i})
then

(a)

\forall i\in I:

\vdash P_{i}:{\mathsf{T}}_{i}
and

(b)

\forall i\in I:

\vdash h_{i}:\sigma_{i}
and

(c)

\Gamma=\{{\sf p}_{i}:(M_{i},{\mathsf{T}}_{i}):i\in I\}

Proof.

The proof is by induction on type derivations.
∎

Lemma B.13 (Typing congruence).

(1)

If
\Theta\vdash P:{\mathsf{T}}
and
P\equiv Q
then
\Theta\vdash Q:{\mathsf{T}}.

(2)

If
\vdash h_{1}:\sigma_{1}
and
h_{1}\equiv h_{2}
then there is
\sigma_{2}
such that
\sigma_{1}\equiv\sigma_{2}
and
\vdash h_{2}:\sigma_{2}.

(3)

If
\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{M}\equiv\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
, then there is
\Gamma
such that
\Gamma\equiv\Gamma^{\prime}
and
\Gamma\vdash\mathcal{M}
.

Proof.

The proof is by case analysis.
∎

Lemma B.14 (Substitution).

If
\Theta,x:{\mathsf{S}}\vdash P:{\mathsf{T}}
and
\Theta\vdash\mathsf{v}:{\mathsf{S}}
,  then
\Theta\vdash P\{\mathsf{v}/x\}:{\mathsf{T}}
.

Proof.

By structural induction on
P
.
∎

Lemma B.15.

If
\emptyset\vdash\mathsf{e}:{\mathsf{S}}
then there is
\mathsf{v}
such that
\mathsf{e}\downarrow\mathsf{v}.

Proof.

The proof is by induction on the derivation of
\emptyset\vdash\mathsf{e}:{\mathsf{S}}.

∎

Lemma B.16.

Let
\vdash h:\sigma
. If

h\not\equiv({\sf p},-(-))\cdot h^{\prime}

then

\sigma\not\equiv{\sf p}!-(-)\cdot{\sigma^{\prime}}
.

Proof.

The proof is by contrapositive: assume
\sigma\equiv{\sf p}!-(-)\cdot{\sigma^{\prime}}
.
Then, by induction on the derivation of
\sigma\equiv{\sf p}!-(-)\cdot{\sigma^{\prime}}
,
we may show that
h\equiv({\sf p},-(-))\cdot h^{\prime}
.
∎

Lemma B.17.

If
\Theta\vdash P:{\mathsf{T}}
then there is
{\mathsf{T}}^{\prime}
such that

{\mathsf{T}}^{\prime}\leqslant{\mathsf{T}}
and
\Theta\vdash P:{\mathsf{T}}^{\prime}
and
\mathtt{act}({\mathsf{T}}^{\prime})\subseteq\mathtt{act}(P)
.

Proof.

By induction on
\Theta\vdash P:{\mathsf{T}}
.
The only interesting case is
[
t-cond
]
.
In this case, we have that

\Theta\vdash\mathsf{if}\penalty\ \mathsf{e}\penalty\ \mathsf{then}\penalty\ P_{1}\penalty\ \mathsf{else}\penalty\ P_{2}:{\mathsf{T}}

is derived from

\Theta\vdash\mathsf{e}:\mathtt{bool}
,

\Theta\vdash P_{1}:{\mathsf{T}}
and
\Theta\vdash P_{2}:{\mathsf{T}}
.
By induction hypothesis we derive that there exist
{\mathsf{T}}_{1}
and
{\mathsf{T}}_{2}

such that

(79)

\displaystyle\Theta\vdash P_{1}:{\mathsf{T}}_{1}\;\text{ and }\;{\mathsf{T}}_{1}\leqslant{\mathsf{T}}\;\text{ and }\;\mathtt{act}({\mathsf{T}}_{1})\subseteq\mathtt{act}(P_{1})

(80)

\displaystyle\Theta\vdash P_{2}:{\mathsf{T}}_{2}\;\text{ and }\;{\mathsf{T}}_{2}\leqslant{\mathsf{T}}\;\text{ and }\;\mathtt{act}({\mathsf{T}}_{2})\subseteq\mathtt{act}(P_{2})

We will show that there is
{\mathsf{T}}^{\prime}
such that

{\mathsf{T}}_{1}\leqslant{\mathsf{T}}^{\prime}\leqslant{\mathsf{T}}
and

{\mathsf{T}}_{2}\leqslant{\mathsf{T}}^{\prime}\leqslant{\mathsf{T}}
and

\mathtt{act}({\mathsf{T}}^{\prime})\;\subseteq\;\mathtt{act}({\mathsf{T}}_{1})\cup\mathtt{act}({\mathsf{T}}_{2})\;(\subseteq\;\mathtt{act}(P_{1})\cup\mathtt{act}(P_{1})\;=\;\mathtt{act}(P))

Let us now expand the derivations of
{\mathsf{T}}_{1}\leqslant{\mathsf{T}}
and
{\mathsf{T}}_{2}\leqslant{\mathsf{T}}

given in (
79
) and (
80
):

(81)

\displaystyle\forall{\mathsf{U}}_{1}\in\llbracket{\mathsf{T}}_{1}\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SO}}}\quad\forall{\mathsf{V}}^{\prime}\in\llbracket{\mathsf{T}}\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SI}}}\quad\exists{\mathsf{W}}_{1}\in\llbracket{\mathsf{U}}_{1}\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SI}}}\quad\exists{\mathsf{W}}_{1}^{\prime}\in\llbracket{\mathsf{V}}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SO}}}\qquad{\mathsf{W}}_{1}\lesssim{\mathsf{W}}_{1}^{\prime}

(82)

\displaystyle\forall{\mathsf{U}}_{2}\in\llbracket{\mathsf{T}}_{2}\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SO}}}\quad\forall{\mathsf{V}}^{\prime}\in\llbracket{\mathsf{T}}\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SI}}}\quad\exists{\mathsf{W}}_{2}\in\llbracket{\mathsf{U}}_{2}\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SI}}}\quad\exists{\mathsf{W}}_{2}^{\prime}\in\llbracket{\mathsf{V}}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SO}}}\qquad{\mathsf{W}}_{1}\lesssim{\mathsf{W}}_{2}^{\prime}

Let us consider the sets:

•

A_{1}
as the set of all actions occurring in any
{\mathsf{U}}_{1}
in (
81
);

•

A_{2}
as the set of all actions occurring in any
{\mathsf{U}}_{2}
in (
82
);

•

A^{\prime}
as the set of all actions occurring in any
{\mathsf{V}}^{\prime}
in (
81
)
(or equivalently in (
82
)).

Notice that
\mathtt{act}({\mathsf{T}}_{1})=A_{1},\mathtt{act}({\mathsf{T}}_{2})=A_{2}
and
\mathtt{act}({\mathsf{T}})=A^{\prime}
.

Furthermore, let us also consider the sets:

•

B_{1}
as the set of all actions occurring in any
{\mathsf{W}}_{1}
selected in (
81
);

•

B_{2}
as the set of all actions occurring in any
{\mathsf{W}}_{2}
selected in (
82
);

•

B_{1}^{\prime}
as the set of all actions occurring in any
{\mathsf{W}}_{1}^{\prime}
selected in (
81
);

•

B_{2}^{\prime}
as the set of all actions occurring in any
{\mathsf{W}}_{2}^{\prime}
selected in (
82
).

Now we have:

(83)

\displaystyle B_{1}\subseteq A_{1}

(84)

\displaystyle B_{2}\subseteq A_{2}

(85)

\displaystyle B_{1}^{\prime}\cup B_{2}^{\prime}\subseteq A^{\prime}

(86)

\displaystyle B_{1}=B_{1}^{\prime}\quad(\text{from}\;{\mathsf{W}}_{1}\lesssim{\mathsf{W}}_{1}^{\prime})

(87)

\displaystyle B_{2}=B_{2}^{\prime}\quad(\text{from}\;{\mathsf{W}}_{2}\lesssim{\mathsf{W}}_{2}^{\prime})

Therefore, by (
86
) and (
87
) we have

(88)

B_{1}\cup B_{2}=B_{1}^{\prime}\cup B_{2}^{\prime}

and thus, by (
83
), (
84
), (
79
)
and (
80
), we obtain

(89)

B_{1}^{\prime}\cup B_{2}^{\prime}\;\subseteq\;A_{1}\cup A_{2}\;\subseteq\;\mathtt{act}(P_{1})\cup\mathtt{act}(P_{2})\;=\;\mathtt{act}(P)

Now, consider the set
D=A^{\prime}\setminus(A_{1}\cup A_{2})
:
it contains all actions that occur in
{\mathsf{T}}
, but not in
{\mathsf{T}}_{1}
nor
{\mathsf{T}}_{2}
.
Consider any action
\alpha\in D
: it must belong to some SISO tree
{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime\prime}

which was not selected neither as
{\mathsf{W}}_{1}^{\prime}
in (
81
),
nor as
{\mathsf{W}}_{2}^{\prime}
in (
82
).
Therefore, it must belong to some action of some SO tree in

\llbracket{\mathsf{V}}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SO}}}
that is never selected by
{\mathsf{T}}_{1}
nor
{\mathsf{T}}_{2}
.
This means that
\alpha
belongs to some internal choice branches of
{\mathsf{T}}
that are never selected by

{\mathsf{T}}_{1}
nor
{\mathsf{T}}_{2}
.
Therefore, if we prune
{\mathbb{T}}
(i.e., the syntactic type with tree
{\mathsf{T}}
) by removing all such internal choice branches,
we get a session type
{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime\prime}
with tree
{\mathsf{T}}^{\prime\prime}\leqslant{\mathsf{T}}
such that:

(90)

\displaystyle\forall{\mathsf{U}}_{1}\in\llbracket{\mathsf{T}}_{1}\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SO}}}\quad\forall{\mathsf{V}}^{\prime}\in\llbracket{\mathsf{T}}^{\prime\prime}\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SI}}}\quad\exists{\mathsf{W}}_{1}\in\llbracket{\mathsf{U}}_{1}\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SI}}}\quad\exists{\mathsf{W}}_{1}^{\prime}\in\llbracket{\mathsf{V}}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SO}}}\qquad{\mathsf{W}}_{1}\lesssim{\mathsf{W}}_{1}^{\prime}

(91)

\displaystyle\forall{\mathsf{U}}_{2}\in\llbracket{\mathsf{T}}_{2}\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SO}}}\quad\forall{\mathsf{V}}^{\prime}\in\llbracket{\mathsf{T}}^{\prime\prime}\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SI}}}\quad\exists{\mathsf{W}}_{2}\in\llbracket{\mathsf{U}}_{2}\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SI}}}\quad\exists{\mathsf{W}}_{2}^{\prime}\in\llbracket{\mathsf{V}}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SO}}}\qquad{\mathsf{W}}_{1}\lesssim{\mathsf{W}}_{2}^{\prime}

Hence,
{\mathsf{T}}_{1}\leqslant{\mathsf{T}}^{\prime\prime}
and
{\mathsf{T}}_{2}\leqslant{\mathsf{T}}^{\prime\prime}
.

Now let
A^{\prime\prime}=\mathtt{act}({\mathsf{T}}^{\prime\prime})
.
If we compute
D^{\prime}=A^{\prime\prime}\setminus(A_{1}\cup A_{2})
(i.e., the set of all actions that occur in
{\mathsf{T}}^{\prime\prime}
,
but not in
{\mathsf{T}}_{1}
nor
{\mathsf{T}}_{2}
)
using (
90
) and (
91
),
we obtain
D^{\prime}\subset D
, because
\alpha
(and possibly some other actions in
D
) have been removed by the pruning.
By iterating the procedure (i.e., by induction on the number of actions in
D
), noticing that
D
is finite (because
{\mathsf{T}}
is syntax-derived from some
{\mathbb{T}}
), we will eventually find some
{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}
with tree
{\mathsf{T}}^{\prime}
such that:

\displaystyle{\mathsf{T}}^{\prime}\leqslant{\mathsf{T}}\;\text{ and }\;{\mathsf{T}}_{1}\leqslant{\mathsf{T}}^{\prime}\;\text{ and }\;{\mathsf{T}}_{2}\leqslant{\mathsf{T}}^{\prime}

\displaystyle\mathtt{act}({\mathsf{T}}^{\prime})\subseteq\mathtt{act}({\mathsf{T}}_{1})\cup\mathtt{act}({\mathsf{T}}_{2})

\displaystyle\Theta\vdash P:{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}

\displaystyle\mathtt{act}({\mathsf{T}}^{\prime})\subseteq\mathtt{act}(P)

∎

We may now show Type Safety and Progress results, that are both corollaries of Subject Reduction and Error subject reduction results.

See
4.9

Proof.

Assume
\Gamma\vdash\mathcal{M}
with
\Gamma
live, and
\mathcal{M}\longrightarrow^{*}\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
. By Theorem
4.8
(subject reduction), there is some live
\Gamma^{\prime}
such that:

(235)

\displaystyle\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash\mathcal{M}^{\prime}

This implies that
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
cannot be an (untypable)
\mathsf{error}
— which is the first part of the thesis. For the
“also…”
part of the statement, we have two possibilities:

(1)

\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\equiv{\sf p}\triangleleft\mathbf{0}\penalty\ |\penalty\ {\sf p}\triangleleft\varnothing
. This is the thesis; or,

(2)

\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\not\equiv{\sf p}\triangleleft\mathbf{0}\penalty\ |\penalty\ {\sf p}\triangleleft\varnothing
. We have two sub-cases:

(a)

there is
\mathcal{M}^{\prime\prime}
such that
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\longrightarrow\mathcal{M}^{\prime\prime}\neq\mathsf{error}
. This is the thesis; or,

(b)

there is no
\mathcal{M}^{\prime\prime}
such that
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\longrightarrow\mathcal{M}^{\prime\prime}\neq\mathsf{error}
. This case is impossible, because it means that either:

(i)

\not\!\exists\mathcal{M}^{\prime\prime}:\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\longrightarrow\mathcal{M}^{\prime\prime}
. This case is impossible. In fact, it would imply that
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
cannot reduce by rules
[
r-send
]
,
[
r-rcv
]
,
[
r-cond-t
]
, or
[
r-cond-f
]
(possibly via
[
r-struct
]
) in Table
4
. Since
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\not\equiv{\sf p}\triangleleft\mathbf{0}\penalty\ |\penalty\ {\sf p}\triangleleft\varnothing
, this can only happen because
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
has some process stuck on an external choice without a matching message, or has some expression (in conditionals or outputs) that cannot be evaluated. But then, by the rules in Table
4
, we have
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\longrightarrow\mathsf{error}
by at least one of the rules
[
err-mism
]
,
[
err-dlock
]
,
[
err-eval
]
or
[
err-eval2
]
. This leads to case
2(b)ii
below;

(ii)

\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\longrightarrow\mathsf{error}
. This case is impossible. In fact, if we admit this case, by (
235
) and the contrapositive of Lemma
B.18
, we have that
\Gamma^{\prime}
is not live, which means (by the contrapositive of Proposition
4.6
) that
\Gamma
is not live — contradiction.

∎

Appendix C
Appendix of Section
5

C.1.
Proof of
Lemma

5.3

Our aim is to prove that
a pair of SISO trees is
not
related by the coinducteve relation
\lesssim

if and only if
they are related by the inductive relation
\not\lesssim
.

We adopt an approach inspired by
Blackburn et al. 2012
;
Ligatti
et al. 2017
, by introducing the notions of
rule function
and
failing derivation
for our SISO refinement:

(1)

we define a
rule function
\Phi
(
Definition

C.3
below), to associate a judgment of the form “
{\mathsf{W}}\lesssim{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}
”  to its coinductive premise in
Definition

3.2
(if any),
so that
{\mathsf{W}}\lesssim{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}
can be derived (by
Definition

3.2
) if and only if

(1)

\Phi\!\left({{\mathsf{W}}\lesssim{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}}\right)
yields “
\mathsf{true}
,” or

(2)

\Phi\!\left({{\mathsf{W}}\lesssim{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}}\right)
yields
a derivable judgement according to the rules in
Definition

3.2
;

(2)

then, in
Definition

C.4

we use the rule function
\Phi
to define sequences of judgements
representing
successful or failing derivations

according to the rules in
Definition

3.2
;

(3)

finally, in
Proposition

C.7

we show that if we have a failing derivation,
then for each one of its judgements “
{\mathsf{W}}\lesssim{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}
”,
we can derive
{\mathsf{W}}\not\lesssim{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}

under the rules in
Table

6
.
Symmetrically, in
Proposition

C.8

we prove that for each judgement
{\mathsf{W}}\not\lesssim{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}
in a derivation
based on
Table

6
we can construct a failing derivation for “
{\mathsf{W}}\lesssim{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}
”.
These results lead to the proof of
Lemma

5.3
.

Before proceeding,
it is handy to introduce some auxiliary rules for
\not\lesssim

(
Definition

C.1
) that are sound wrt. the rules in
Table

6

(as per
Proposition

C.2
below).
Such auxiliary rules will only be used in the proofs of this section.

Definition C.1 (Auxiliary SISO refinement rules).

We define the following additional rules for the SISO refinement relation:

{\sf p}?\ell({\mathsf{S}}).{\mathsf{W}}\not\lesssim\mathcal{A}^{({\sf p})}.{{\sf p}?\ell({\mathsf{S}}^{\prime}).{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}}\lx@proof@logical@and{\mathsf{S}}^{\prime}\leq\vcentcolon{\mathsf{S}}\mathtt{act}({\mathsf{W}})\neq\mathtt{act}(\mathcal{A}^{({\sf p})}.{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime})\qquad{\sf p}!\ell({\mathsf{S}}).{\mathsf{W}}\not\lesssim\mathcal{B}^{({\sf p})}.{{\sf p}!\ell({\mathsf{S}}^{\prime}).{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}}\lx@proof@logical@and{\mathsf{S}}\leq\vcentcolon{\mathsf{S}}^{\prime}\mathtt{act}({\mathsf{W}})\neq\mathtt{act}(\mathcal{B}^{({\sf p})}.{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime})

Proposition C.2 (Soundness of the auxiliary SISO refinement rules).

Assume that
{\mathsf{W}}_{1}\not\lesssim{\mathsf{W}}_{2}
holds by one of the auxiliary rules
introduced in
Definition

C.1
.
Then,
{\mathsf{W}}_{1}\not\lesssim{\mathsf{W}}_{2}
can also be derived
by only using the rules in
Table

6
.

Proof.

We have two cases.

•

{\mathsf{W}}_{1}\not\lesssim{\mathsf{W}}_{2}
holds by
[
n-
{\mathcal{A}}
-act
]
. In this case, we have:

(236)

\displaystyle{\mathsf{W}}_{1}={\sf p}?\ell({\mathsf{S}}).{\mathsf{W}}\quad\text{and}\quad{\mathsf{W}}_{2}=\mathcal{A}^{({\sf p})}.{{\sf p}?\ell({\mathsf{S}}^{\prime}).{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}}

(237)

\displaystyle{\mathsf{S}}^{\prime}\leq\vcentcolon{\mathsf{S}}

(238)

\displaystyle\mathtt{act}({\mathsf{W}})\neq\mathtt{act}(\mathcal{A}^{({\sf p})}.{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime})

Observe that, by (

),

.
In this case, assume that a prefix

Definition C.3.

The
rule function
\Phi
maps judgments of the form “
{\mathsf{W}}_{1}\lesssim{\mathsf{W}}_{2}
”
to judgment of the same form, or “
\mathsf{true}
,” or “
\mathsf{false},
” as follows:

\Phi\!\left({{\mathsf{W}}_{1}\lesssim{\mathsf{W}}_{2}}\right)\;=\;\begin{cases}{\mathsf{W}}\lesssim{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}&\text{if }\begin{cases}{\mathsf{W}}_{1}={\sf p}?\ell({\mathsf{S}}).{\mathsf{W}}\\
\text{and }{\mathsf{W}}_{2}={\sf p}?\ell({\mathsf{S}}^{\prime}).{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}\\
\text{and }{\mathsf{S}}^{\prime}\leq\vcentcolon{\mathsf{S}}\end{cases}\\
{\mathsf{W}}\lesssim\mathcal{A}^{({\sf p})}.{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}&\text{if }\begin{cases}{\mathsf{W}}_{1}={\sf p}?\ell({\mathsf{S}}).{\mathsf{W}}\\
\text{and }{\mathsf{W}}_{2}=\mathcal{A}^{({\sf p})}.{{\sf p}?\ell({\mathsf{S}}^{\prime}).{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}}\\
\text{and }{\mathsf{S}}^{\prime}\leq\vcentcolon{\mathsf{S}}\\
\text{and }\mathtt{act}({\mathsf{W}})=\mathtt{act}(\mathcal{A}^{({\sf p})}.{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime})\end{cases}\\
{\mathsf{W}}\lesssim{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}&\text{if }\begin{cases}{\mathsf{W}}_{1}={\sf p}!\ell({\mathsf{S}}).{\mathsf{W}}\\
\text{and }{\mathsf{W}}_{2}={\sf p}!\ell({\mathsf{S}}^{\prime}).{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}\\
\text{and }{\mathsf{S}}\leq\vcentcolon{\mathsf{S}}^{\prime}\end{cases}\\
{\mathsf{W}}\lesssim\mathcal{B}^{({\sf p})}.{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}&\text{if }\begin{cases}{\mathsf{W}}_{1}={\sf p}!\ell({\mathsf{S}}).{\mathsf{W}}\\
\text{and }{\mathsf{W}}_{2}=\mathcal{B}^{({\sf p})}.{\sf p}!\ell({\mathsf{S}}^{\prime}).{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}\\
\text{and }{\mathsf{S}}\leq\vcentcolon{\mathsf{S}}^{\prime}\\
\text{and }\mathtt{act}({\mathsf{W}})=\mathtt{act}(\mathcal{B}^{({\sf p})}.{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime})\end{cases}\\
\mathsf{true}&\text{if ${\mathsf{W}}_{1}={\mathsf{W}}_{2}=\mathtt{end}$}\\
\mathsf{false}&\text{in all other cases}\end{cases}

Definition C.4.

Consider a (possibly infinite) sequence
(J_{i})_{i\in I}
, where
I
contains consecutive natural numbers starting with
1
. We say that the sequence is a
derivation of
{\mathsf{W}}\lesssim{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}
iff it satisfies the following requirements:

(1)

for all
i\in I
,

J_{i}
is a judgement of the form “
{\mathsf{W}}_{i}\lesssim{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}_{i}
”;

(2)

J_{1}
is  “
{\mathsf{W}}\lesssim{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}
”;

(3)

for all
i\in I
,
i>1
implies
J_{i}=\Phi\!\left({{\mathsf{W}}_{i-1}\lesssim{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}_{i-1}}\right)
.

We say that a derivation
(J_{i})_{i\in I}
is
successful
iff it is infinite, or if it has a finite length
n
and
\Phi\!\left({J_{n}}\right)=\mathsf{true}
.

We say that a derivation
(J_{i})_{i\in I}
is
failing
iff it has a finite length
n
and
\Phi\!\left({J_{n}}\right)=\mathsf{false}
.

Proposition C.5.

The refinement
{\mathsf{W}}\lesssim{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}

is derivable (resp.
not
derivable)
with the rules of
Definition

3.2
iff there is a successful (resp. failing) derivation of
{\mathsf{W}}\lesssim{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}
.

Proof.

Straightforward by
Definition

C.4
.
∎

Corollary C.6.

Assume that the sequence of judgements
(J_{1},\ldots,J_{n})
(for some
n\geq 1
) is a failing derivation
of
{\mathsf{W}}_{1}\lesssim{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}_{1}
.
Then,
J_{n}
is a judgement of the form “
{\mathsf{W}}_{n}\lesssim{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}_{n}
”
such that
{\mathsf{W}}_{n}\not\lesssim{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}_{n}
holds
by at least one of the axioms in
Table

6

or
Definition

C.1
, i.e.:

[
n-out
]
,
[
n-inp
]
,
[
n-out-R
]
,
[
n-inp-R
]
,

[
n-inp-
\ell
]
,
[
n-inp-
{\mathsf{S}}
]
,

[
n-
{\mathcal{A}}
-
\ell
]
,
[
n-
{\mathcal{A}}
-
{\mathsf{S}}
]
,

[
n-i-o-1
]
,
[
n-i-o-2
]
,

[
n-out-
\ell
]
,
[
n-out-
{\mathsf{S}}
]
,

[
n-
{\mathcal{B}}
-
\ell
]
,
[
n-
{\mathcal{B}}
-
{\mathsf{S}}
]
,

[
n-
{\mathcal{A}}
-act
]
,
[
n-
{\mathcal{B}}
-act
]
.

Proof.

By
Proposition

C.5
, we have
\Phi\!\left({J_{n}}\right)=\emptyset
. Therefore, we obtain the thesis from
Definition

C.3
,
by inspecting all cases that falsify all its “if…” clauses:
each case corresponds to either at least one axiom in
Table

6
,
or at least one additional axiom in
Definition

C.1
.
∎

Proposition C.7.

Assume that
(J_{1},\ldots,J_{n})
(for some
n\geq 1
) is a failing derivation
of
{\mathsf{W}}\lesssim{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}
.
Then, for all
i\in 1..n
, we have that
{\mathsf{W}}_{i}\not\lesssim{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}_{i}
is derivable by the rules in
Table

6
.

Proof.

The proof is by induction on
n
, decreasing toward
1
:

•

base case
n
. In this case, by
Corollary

C.6
, we have either:

(a)

{\mathsf{W}}_{n}\not\lesssim{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}_{n}
holds
by some axiom in
Table

6
,
which is the thesis; or

(b)

{\mathsf{W}}_{n}\not\lesssim{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}_{n}
holds
by some axiom in
Definition

C.1
.
In this case, by
Proposition

C.2
,
there is a derivation of
{\mathsf{W}}_{n}\not\lesssim{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}_{n}

that only uses the rules in
Table

6
, which is the thesis;

•

inductive case
i<n
.
By
Definition

C.4
,
we know that
J_{i+1}=\Phi\!\left({{\mathsf{W}}_{i}\lesssim{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}_{i}}\right)\neq\mathsf{false}
.
Therefore, by
Definition

C.3
,
we have one of the following possibilities:

–

{\mathsf{W}}_{i}={\sf p}?\ell({\mathsf{S}}).{\mathsf{W}}

and
{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}_{i}={\sf p}?\ell({\mathsf{S}}^{\prime}).{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}

and
{\mathsf{S}}^{\prime}\leq\vcentcolon{\mathsf{S}}
.
In this case, by
Definitions

C.4
and
C.3
,
we also have
J_{i+1}=\Phi\!\left({{\mathsf{W}}_{i}\lesssim{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}_{i}}\right)=\text{``${\mathsf{W}}\lesssim{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}$''}
—
hence, by the induction hypothesis,

{\mathsf{W}}\not\lesssim{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}
is derivable by the rules in
Table

6
.
Thus, we conclude that

{\mathsf{W}}_{i}\not\lesssim{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}_{i}
is derivable by
[
n-inp-
{\mathsf{W}}
]
,
only using the rules in
Table

6
;

–

{\mathsf{W}}_{i}={\sf p}?\ell({\mathsf{S}}).{\mathsf{W}}

and
{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}_{i}=\mathcal{A}^{({\sf p})}.{{\sf p}?\ell({\mathsf{S}}^{\prime}).{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}}

and
{\mathsf{S}}^{\prime}\leq\vcentcolon{\mathsf{S}}

and
\mathtt{act}({\mathsf{W}})=\mathtt{act}(\mathcal{A}^{({\sf p})}.{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime})
.
In this case, by
Definitions

C.4
and
C.3
,
we also have
J_{i+1}=\Phi\!\left({{\mathsf{W}}_{i}\lesssim{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}_{i}}\right)=\text{``${\mathsf{W}}\lesssim\mathcal{A}^{({\sf p})}.{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}$''}
—
hence, by the induction hypothesis,

{\mathsf{W}}\not\lesssim\mathcal{A}^{({\sf p})}.{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}

is derivable by the rules in
Table

6
.
Thus, we conclude that

{\mathsf{W}}_{i}\not\lesssim{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}_{i}
is derivable by
[
n-
\mathcal{A}
-
{\mathsf{W}}
]
,
only using the rules in
Table

6
;

–

{\mathsf{W}}_{i}={\sf p}!\ell({\mathsf{S}}).{\mathsf{W}}

and
{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}_{i}={\sf p}!\ell({\mathsf{S}}^{\prime}).{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}

and
{\mathsf{S}}\leq\vcentcolon{\mathsf{S}}^{\prime}
.
In this case, by
Definitions

C.4
and
C.3
,
we also have
J_{i+1}=\Phi\!\left({{\mathsf{W}}_{i}\lesssim{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}_{i}}\right)=\text{``${\mathsf{W}}\lesssim{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}$''}
—
hence, by the induction hypothesis,

{\mathsf{W}}\not\lesssim{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}
is derivable by the rules in
Table

6
.
Thus, we conclude that

{\mathsf{W}}_{i}\not\lesssim{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}_{i}
is derivable by
[
n-out-
{\mathsf{W}}
]
,
only using the rules in
Table

6
;

–

{\mathsf{W}}_{i}={\sf p}!\ell({\mathsf{S}}).{\mathsf{W}}

and
{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}_{i}=\mathcal{B}^{({\sf p})}.{{\sf p}!\ell({\mathsf{S}}^{\prime}).{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}}

and
{\mathsf{S}}\leq\vcentcolon{\mathsf{S}}^{\prime}

and
\mathtt{act}({\mathsf{W}})=\mathtt{act}(\mathcal{A}^{({\sf p})}.{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime})
.
In this case, by
Definitions

C.4
and
C.3
,
we also have
J_{i+1}=\Phi\!\left({{\mathsf{W}}_{i}\lesssim{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}_{i}}\right)=\text{``${\mathsf{W}}\lesssim\mathcal{B}^{({\sf p})}.{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}$''}
—
hence, by the induction hypothesis,

{\mathsf{W}}\not\lesssim\mathcal{A}^{({\sf p})}.{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}

is derivable by the rules in
Table

6
.
Thus, we conclude that

{\mathsf{W}}_{i}\not\lesssim{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}_{i}
is derivable by
[
n-
\mathcal{B}
-
{\mathsf{W}}
]
,
only using the rules in
Table

;

–

{\mathsf{W}}_{i}={\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}_{i}=\mathtt{end}
. This case is impossible,
as it would imply

,
hence
(J_{1},\ldots,J_{n})
would
not

according to

C.4
,
thus contradicting the hypothesis in the statement.

∎

Proposition C.8.

Assume that
{\mathsf{W}}\not\lesssim{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}
holds, by the rules in
Table

6
;
also assume that its derivation has
n\geq 1
rule applications,
counting upwards (i.e., the
1
st
rule concludes
{\mathsf{W}}\not\lesssim{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}
,
while the
n
th
rule is the axiom),
with the
i
th
rule concluding

{\mathsf{W}}_{i}\not\lesssim{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}_{i}
(for
i\in 1..n
).
Then,
\Phi\!\left({{\mathsf{W}}_{n}\lesssim{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}_{n}}\right)=\mathsf{false}
;
moreover, for all
i\in 1..n{-}1
,
we have either
\Phi\!\left({{\mathsf{W}}_{i}\lesssim{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}_{i}}\right)=\mathsf{false}
,  or

\Phi\!\left({{\mathsf{W}}_{i}\lesssim{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}_{i}}\right)=\text{``${\mathsf{W}}_{i+1}\lesssim{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}_{i+1}$''}
.

Proof.

The judgement
{\mathsf{W}}_{n}\not\lesssim{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}_{n}
holds by at least one axiom among

[
n-out
]
,
[
n-inp
]
,
[
n-out-R
]
,
[
n-inp-R
]
,

[
n-inp-
\ell
]
,
[
n-inp-
{\mathsf{S}}
]
,

[
n-
{\mathcal{A}}
-
\ell
]
,
[
n-
{\mathcal{A}}
-
{\mathsf{S}}
]
,

[
n-i-o-1
]
,
[
n-i-o-2
]
,

[
n-out-
\ell
]
,
[
n-out-
{\mathsf{S}}
]
,

[
n-
{\mathcal{B}}
-
\ell
]
,
[
n-
{\mathcal{B}}
-
{\mathsf{S}}
]
.
We proceed by cases on such axioms, inspecting the corresponding shapes
of
{\mathsf{W}}_{n}
and
{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}_{n}
: in all cases we conclude
\Phi\!\left({{\mathsf{W}}_{n}\lesssim{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}_{n}}\right)=\mathsf{false}
.
For any other
m<n
, the judgement
{\mathsf{W}}_{m}\not\lesssim{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}_{m}

holds by one of the inductive rules in
Table

6
.
Hence, we have the following possibilities:

•

[
n-inp-
{\mathsf{W}}
]
. In this case, we have
{\mathsf{W}}_{m}={\sf p}?\ell({\mathsf{S}}).{\mathsf{W}}_{m+1}
,

{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}_{m}={\sf p}?\ell({\mathsf{S}}^{\prime}).{\mathsf{W}}_{m+1}
,
and
{\mathsf{W}}_{m+1}\not\lesssim{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}_{m+1}
and
{\mathsf{S}}^{\prime}\lesssim{\mathsf{S}}
.
Therefore, by
Definition

C.3
we conclude

\Phi\!\left({{\mathsf{W}}_{m}\lesssim{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}_{m}}\right)=\text{``${\mathsf{W}}_{m+1}\lesssim{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}_{m+1}$''}
;

•

[
n-
{\mathcal{A}}
-
{\mathsf{W}}
]
. In this case, we have
{\mathsf{W}}_{m}={\sf p}?\ell({\mathsf{S}}).{\mathsf{W}}_{*}
,

{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}_{m}=\mathcal{A}^{({\sf p})}.{\sf p}?\ell({\mathsf{S}}^{\prime}).{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}_{*}
,
and
{\mathsf{S}}^{\prime}\lesssim{\mathsf{S}}
,
and
{\mathsf{W}}_{*}\not\lesssim\mathcal{A}^{({\sf p})}.{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}_{*}
.
Observe that the latter is the
m+1
th

step of the derivation, i.e., we have
{\mathsf{W}}_{m+1}\not\lesssim{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}_{m+1}

with
{\mathsf{W}}_{m+1}={\mathsf{W}}_{*}
and
{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}_{m+1}=\mathcal{A}^{({\sf p})}.{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}_{*}
.
Therefore, we have the following sub-cases:

–

\mathtt{act}({\mathsf{W}}_{m+1})\neq\mathtt{act}({\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}_{m+1})
. In this case, by
Definition

C.3
we conclude

\Phi\!\left({{\mathsf{W}}_{m}\lesssim{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}_{m}}\right)=\mathsf{false}
;

–

\mathtt{act}({\mathsf{W}}_{m+1})=\mathtt{act}(WT^{\prime}_{m+1})
. In this case,
by
Definition

C.3
we conclude

\Phi\!\left({{\mathsf{W}}_{m}\lesssim{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}_{m}}\right)=\text{``${\mathsf{W}}_{m+1}\lesssim{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}_{m+1}$''}
;

•

[
n-out-
{\mathsf{W}}
]
. Similar to case
[
n-inp-
{\mathsf{W}}
]
above;

•

[
n-
{\mathcal{B}}
-
{\mathsf{W}}
]
. Similar to case
[
n-
\mathcal{A}
-
{\mathsf{W}}
]
above.

∎

See
5.3

Proof.

(
\implies
) Assume that
{\mathsf{W}}\lesssim{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}
is
not
derivable:
then, by
Proposition

C.5
and
Definition

C.4
,
there is a failing derivation
(J_{1},\ldots,J_{n})
(for some finite
n\geq 1
)
where
J_{1}
is the judgement  “
{\mathsf{W}}\lesssim{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}
”. Then, by
Proposition

C.7
, we obtain the thesis.

(
\impliedby
) Assume that
{\mathsf{W}}\not\lesssim{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}
holds,
by the rules in
Table

6
, with a derivation with
n\geq 1
rule applications.
Then, by
Proposition

C.8
,
there is a failing derivation for “
{\mathsf{W}}\not\lesssim{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}
”
(of length
n
or less),
and by
Proposition

C.5
, we conclude that

{\mathsf{W}}\not\lesssim{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}
is
not
derivable.
∎

C.2.
Regular representatives for subtyping negation

In the sequel, we will always consider only regular representatives of SO and SI trees that appear in the definition of the negation of subtyping. Before we adopt that approach, we will prove that whenever there exist a pair of (possibly non-regular) representatives
{\mathsf{U}}\in\llbracket\operatorname{\mathcal{T}}\!\left({{\mathbb{T}}}\right)\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SO}}}
and
{\mathsf{V}}^{\prime}\in\llbracket\operatorname{\mathcal{T}}\!\left({{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}}\right)\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SI}}}
with
{\mathsf{U}}\not\lesssim{\mathsf{V}}^{\prime},
there is also a pair of regular representatives
\operatorname{\mathcal{T}}\!\left({{\mathbb{U}}_{1}}\right)\in\llbracket\operatorname{\mathcal{T}}\!\left({{\mathbb{T}}}\right)\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SO}}}
and
\operatorname{\mathcal{T}}\!\left({{\mathbb{V}}_{1}^{\prime}}\right)\in\llbracket\operatorname{\mathcal{T}}\!\left({{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}}\right)\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SI}}}
such that
\operatorname{\mathcal{T}}\!\left({{\mathbb{U}}_{1}}\right)\not\lesssim\operatorname{\mathcal{T}}\!\left({{\mathbb{V}}_{1}^{\prime}}\right).

We start by proving that for each irregular tree
{\mathsf{U}}\in\llbracket\operatorname{\mathcal{T}}\!\left({{\mathbb{T}}}\right)\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SO}}}
there is a regular tree
{\mathsf{U}}_{1}\in\llbracket\operatorname{\mathcal{T}}\!\left({{\mathbb{T}}}\right)\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SO}}}
such that
{\mathsf{U}}
and
{\mathsf{U}}_{1}
overlap in at least top
n
levels, for a given
n.
For that purpose, we introduce two auxiliary functions,
{\tt reg}_{\text{\tiny\sf{SO}}}({\mathsf{U}},i,{\mathbb{T}},{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime})
and
{\tt mu}^{-}({\mathbb{T}}).

The function
{\tt reg}_{\text{\tiny\sf{SO}}}({\mathsf{U}},i,{\mathbb{T}},{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}),
with
{\mathsf{U}}\in\llbracket\operatorname{\mathcal{T}}\!\left({{\mathbb{T}}}\right)\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SO}}},
follows in the tree of
{\mathbb{T}}
the pattern determined by top
i
levels of
{\mathsf{U}}
and extracts (step by step) a type
{\mathbb{U}}
with the tree
\operatorname{\mathcal{T}}\!\left({{\mathbb{U}}}\right)
that follows the same pattern. The type
{\mathbb{U}}
might not be unique, but all such types have the same top
i
levels. Each step of the procedure applies one of the three options that are introduced and clarified along the following lines.

(1)

If
{\mathbb{T}}=\mu\textbf{t}.{\mathbb{T}}_{1},
then

\displaystyle{\tt reg}_{\text{\tiny\sf{SO}}}({\mathsf{U}},i,\mu\textbf{t}.{\mathbb{T}}_{1},{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime})=

\displaystyle\mu\textbf{t}.{\tt reg}_{\text{\tiny\sf{SO}}}({\mathsf{U}},i,{\mathbb{T}}_{1},\mu\textbf{t}.{\mathbb{T}}_{1}),\text{ for every }i\geq 0;

The function goes behind
\mu\textbf{t}
and in the same time the forth parameter keeps the information on the form of the
\mu
type (it might be needed later on for unfolding).

(2)

If
{\mathbb{T}}\neq\mu\textbf{t}.{\mathbb{T}}_{1},
for any
t
and
{\mathbb{T}}_{1},
and
i>0
, then

\displaystyle{\tt reg}_{\text{\tiny\sf{SO}}}({\mathsf{U}},i,{\mathbb{T}},{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime})=

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\mathtt{end}&,{\mathsf{U}}=\mathtt{end}\\
{\sf p}!\ell_{j}({\mathsf{S}}_{j}).{\tt reg}_{\text{\tiny\sf{SO}}}({\mathsf{U}}_{j},i-1,{\mathbb{T}}_{j},{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime})&,{\mathsf{U}}={\sf p}!\ell_{j}({\mathsf{S}}_{j}).{\mathsf{U}}_{j},j\in K,\\
&{\mathbb{T}}=\tinternal_{k\in K}{\sf p}!\ell_{k}({\mathsf{S}}_{k}).{{\mathbb{T}}_{k}},\\
\texternal_{k\in K}{\sf p}?\ell_{k}({\mathsf{S}}_{k}).{\tt reg}_{\text{\tiny\sf{SO}}}({\mathsf{U}}_{k},i-1,{\mathbb{T}}_{k},{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime})&,{\mathsf{U}}=\texternal_{k\in K}{\sf p}?\ell_{k}({\mathsf{S}}_{k}).{{\mathsf{U}}_{k}},\\
&{\mathbb{T}}=\texternal_{k\in K}{\sf p}?\ell_{k}({\mathsf{S}}_{k}).{{\mathbb{T}}_{k}},\\
{\tt reg}_{\text{\tiny\sf{SO}}}({\mathsf{U}},i,{\mathbb{T}}_{1},{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime})&,{\mathbb{T}}=\textbf{t},{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}=\mu\textbf{t}.{\mathbb{T}}_{1}\\
\end{array}\right.

The function extracts from
{\mathbb{T}}
the prefix for
{\mathbb{U}}
that will induce the same level in its tree as
{\mathsf{U}}.
If
{\mathbb{T}}=\textbf{t},
it first applies unfolding, recovering the form for substitution from the forth parameter.

(3)

If
{\mathbb{T}}\neq\mu\textbf{t}.{\mathbb{T}}_{1},
for any
t
and
{\mathbb{T}}_{1},
and
i=0
, then

{\tt reg}_{\text{\tiny\sf{SO}}}({\mathsf{U}},0,{\mathbb{T}},{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime})={\mathbb{U}}_{1}
for some
\operatorname{\mathcal{T}}\!\left({{\mathbb{U}}_{1}}\right)\in\llbracket\operatorname{\mathcal{T}}\!\left({{\mathbb{T}}\{\nicefrac{{{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime\prime\prime}}}{{\textbf{t}_{1}}}\}}\right)\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SO}}},
where
{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}=\mu\textbf{t}_{1}.{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime\prime}
and
{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime\prime\prime}=\mu\textbf{t}_{2}.{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime\prime}\{\nicefrac{{\textbf{t}_{2}}}{{\textbf{t}_{1}}}\}
(we choose here a fresh name
\textbf{t}_{2}
). The choice of
{\mathbb{U}}_{1}
is not unique and we will always choose one that satisfies
\mathtt{act}({\mathsf{U}})\supseteq\mathtt{act}(\operatorname{\mathcal{T}}\!\left({{\mathbb{U}}_{1}}\right)).

One can notice that the previous procedure might create some terms of the form
\mu\textbf{t}.{\mathbb{U}}^{\prime}
with
\textbf{t}\not\in{\mathbb{U}}^{\prime}.
These terms are cleaned up by

{\tt mu}^{-}({\mathbb{T}})
, that is defined as follows:

{\tt mu}^{-}({\mathbb{T}})=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\mathtt{end}&{\mathbb{T}}=\mathtt{end}\\
\tinternal_{k\in K}{\sf p}!\ell_{k}({\mathsf{S}}_{k}).{\tt mu}^{-}({\mathbb{T}}_{k})&{\mathbb{T}}=\tinternal_{k\in K}{\sf p}!\ell_{k}({\mathsf{S}}_{k}).{\mathbb{T}}_{k}\\
\texternal_{k\in K}{\sf p}?\ell_{k}({\mathsf{S}}_{k}).{\tt mu}^{-}({\mathbb{T}}_{k})&{\mathbb{T}}=\texternal_{k\in K}{\sf p}?\ell_{k}({\mathsf{S}}_{k}).{\mathbb{T}}_{k}\\
\mu\textbf{t}.{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}&{\mathbb{T}}=\mu\textbf{t}.{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime},\textbf{t}\in{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}\\
{\tt mu}^{-}({\mathbb{T}}^{\prime})&{\mathbb{T}}=\mu\textbf{t}.{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime},\textbf{t}\not\in{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}\\
\end{array}\right.

Lemma C.9.

If
{\mathsf{U}}\in\llbracket\operatorname{\mathcal{T}}\!\left({{\mathbb{T}}}\right)\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SO}}}
then there is
{\mathbb{U}}_{1}
such that
\operatorname{\mathcal{T}}\!\left({{\mathbb{U}}_{1}}\right)\in\llbracket\operatorname{\mathcal{T}}\!\left({{\mathbb{T}}}\right)\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SO}}}
and
\operatorname{\mathcal{T}}\!\left({{\mathbb{U}}_{1}}\right)
overlaps with
{\mathsf{U}}
at top
n
levels.

Proof.

If
{\mathsf{U}}
is finite, then we choose
{\mathbb{U}}_{1}={\mathsf{U}}.

If
{\mathsf{U}}
is infinite, then we choose
{\mathbb{U}}_{1}={\tt mu}^{-}({\tt reg}_{\text{\tiny\sf{SO}}}({\mathsf{U}},n,{\mathbb{T}},{\mathbb{T}})).

∎

In the following two examples we illustrate the procedure on some interesting cases.

Example C.10.

Take
{\mathbb{T}}=\mu\textbf{t}_{1}.({\sf p}!\ell_{1}({\mathsf{S}}_{1}).\textbf{t}_{1}\&{\sf p}!\ell_{2}({\mathsf{S}}_{2}).\mu\textbf{t}_{2}.{\sf p}!\ell_{3}({\mathsf{S}}_{3}).\textbf{t}_{2})
and choose
{\mathsf{U}}\in\llbracket\operatorname{\mathcal{T}}\!\left({{\mathbb{T}}}\right)\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SO}}}
such that
{\mathsf{U}}={\sf p}!\ell_{1}({\mathsf{S}}_{1}).{\sf p}!\ell_{2}({\mathsf{S}}_{2}).{\sf p}!\ell_{3}({\mathsf{S}}_{3})\ldots
We show here that the procedure introduced above gives a regular
{\mathbb{U}}_{1}
that overlaps with
{\mathsf{U}}
(at least) in the top 3 levels and
\operatorname{\mathcal{T}}\!\left({{\mathbb{U}}_{1}}\right)\in\llbracket\operatorname{\mathcal{T}}\!\left({{\mathbb{T}}}\right)\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SO}}}
.

\displaystyle{\mathbb{U}}^{\prime}_{1}=

\displaystyle{\tt reg}_{\text{\tiny\sf{SO}}}({\mathsf{U}},3,{\mathbb{T}},{\mathbb{T}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mu\textbf{t}_{1}.{\tt reg}_{\text{\tiny\sf{SO}}}({\mathsf{U}},3,{\sf p}!\ell_{1}(\textbf{t})_{1}\&{\sf p}!\ell_{2}({\mathsf{S}}_{2}).\mu\textbf{t}_{2}.{\sf p}!\ell_{3}({\mathsf{S}}_{3}).\textbf{t}_{2},{\mathbb{T}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mu\textbf{t}_{1}.{\sf p}!\ell_{1}({\mathsf{S}}_{1}).{\tt reg}_{\text{\tiny\sf{SO}}}({\sf p}!\ell_{2}({\mathsf{S}}_{2}).{\sf p}!\ell_{3}({\mathsf{S}}_{3})\ldots,2,\textbf{t}_{1},{\mathbb{T}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mu\textbf{t}_{1}.{\sf p}!\ell_{1}({\mathsf{S}}_{1}).{\tt reg}_{\text{\tiny\sf{SO}}}({\sf p}!\ell_{2}({\mathsf{S}}_{2}).{\sf p}!\ell_{3}({\mathsf{S}}_{3})\ldots,2,{\sf p}!\ell_{1}({\mathsf{S}}_{1}).\textbf{t}_{1}\&{\sf p}!\ell_{2}({\mathsf{S}}_{2}).\mu\textbf{t}_{2}.{\sf p}!\ell_{3}({\mathsf{S}}_{3}).\textbf{t}_{2},{\mathbb{T}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mu\textbf{t}_{1}.{\sf p}!\ell_{1}({\mathsf{S}}_{1}).{\sf p}!\ell_{2}({\mathsf{S}}_{2}).{\tt reg}_{\text{\tiny\sf{SO}}}({\sf p}!\ell_{3}({\mathsf{S}}_{3})\ldots,1,\mu\textbf{t}_{2}.{\sf p}!\ell_{3}({\mathsf{S}}_{3}).\textbf{t}_{2},{\mathbb{T}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mu\textbf{t}_{1}.{\sf p}!\ell_{1}({\mathsf{S}}_{1}).{\sf p}!\ell_{2}({\mathsf{S}}_{2}).\mu\textbf{t}_{2}.{\tt reg}_{\text{\tiny\sf{SO}}}({\sf p}!\ell_{3}({\mathsf{S}}_{3})\ldots,1,{\sf p}!\ell_{3}({\mathsf{S}}_{3}).\textbf{t}_{2},\mu\textbf{t}_{2}.{\sf p}!\ell_{3}({\mathsf{S}}_{3}).\textbf{t}_{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mu\textbf{t}_{1}.{\sf p}!\ell_{1}({\mathsf{S}}_{1}).{\sf p}!\ell_{2}({\mathsf{S}}_{2}).\mu\textbf{t}_{2}.{\sf p}!\ell_{3}({\mathsf{S}}_{3}).{\tt reg}_{\text{\tiny\sf{SO}}}({\sf p}!\ell_{3}({\mathsf{S}}_{3})\ldots,0,\textbf{t}_{2},\mu\textbf{t}_{2}.{\sf p}!\ell_{3}({\mathsf{S}}_{3}).\textbf{t}_{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mu\textbf{t}_{1}.{\sf p}!\ell_{1}({\mathsf{S}}_{1}).{\sf p}!\ell_{2}({\mathsf{S}}_{2}).\mu\textbf{t}_{2}.{\sf p}!\ell_{3}({\mathsf{S}}_{3}).\mu\textbf{t}_{3}.{\sf p}!\ell_{3}({\mathsf{S}}_{3}).\textbf{t}_{3}.

After erasure of the meaningless
\mu
terms, we get

\displaystyle{\mathbb{U}}_{1}=

\displaystyle{\tt mu}^{-}({\mathbb{U}}^{\prime})={\tt mu}^{-}(\mu\textbf{t}_{1}.{\sf p}!\ell_{1}({\mathsf{S}}_{1}).{\sf p}!\ell_{2}({\mathsf{S}}_{2}).\mu\textbf{t}_{2}.{\sf p}!\ell_{3}({\mathsf{S}}_{3}).\mu\textbf{t}_{3}.{\sf p}!\ell_{3}({\mathsf{S}}_{3}).\textbf{t}_{3})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\tt mu}^{-}({\sf p}!\ell_{1}({\mathsf{S}}_{1}).{\sf p}!\ell_{2}({\mathsf{S}}_{2}).\mu\textbf{t}_{2}.{\sf p}!\ell_{3}({\mathsf{S}}_{3}).\mu\textbf{t}_{3}.{\sf p}!\ell_{3}({\mathsf{S}}_{3}).\textbf{t}_{3})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\sf p}!\ell_{1}({\mathsf{S}}_{1}).{\sf p}!\ell_{2}({\mathsf{S}}_{2}).{\tt mu}^{-}(\mu\textbf{t}_{2}.{\sf p}!\ell_{3}({\mathsf{S}}_{3}).\mu\textbf{t}_{3}.{\sf p}!\ell_{3}({\mathsf{S}}_{3}).\textbf{t}_{3})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\sf p}!\ell_{1}({\mathsf{S}}_{1}).{\sf p}?\ell_{2}({\mathsf{S}}_{2}).{\sf p}!\ell_{3}({\mathsf{S}}_{3}).{\tt mu}^{-}(\mu\textbf{t}_{3}.{\sf p}!\ell_{3}({\mathsf{S}}_{3}).\textbf{t}_{3})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\sf p}!\ell_{1}({\mathsf{S}}_{1}).{\sf p}?\ell_{2}({\mathsf{S}}_{2}).{\sf p}!\ell_{3}({\mathsf{S}}_{3}).\mu\textbf{t}_{3}.{\sf p}!\ell_{3}({\mathsf{S}}_{3}).\textbf{t}_{3}.

Example C.11.

Take
{\mathbb{T}}=\mu\textbf{t}.({\sf p}!\ell_{1}({\mathsf{S}}_{1}).{\sf p}?\ell_{4}({\mathsf{S}}_{4}).\textbf{t}\&{\sf p}!\ell_{2}({\mathsf{S}}_{2}).\textbf{t})
and choose
{\mathsf{U}}\in\llbracket\operatorname{\mathcal{T}}\!\left({{\mathbb{T}}}\right)\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SO}}}
such that
{\mathsf{U}}={\sf p}!\ell_{1}({\mathsf{S}}_{1})\ldots
We consutruct here a regular
{\mathbb{U}}_{1}
that overlaps with
{\mathsf{U}}
at the top level and
\operatorname{\mathcal{T}}\!\left({{\mathsf{U}}_{1}}\right)\in\llbracket\operatorname{\mathcal{T}}\!\left({{\mathbb{T}}}\right)\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SO}}}
.

\displaystyle{\mathbb{U}}_{1}^{\prime}=

\displaystyle{\tt reg}_{\text{\tiny\sf{SO}}}({\mathsf{U}},1,{\mathbb{T}},{\mathbb{T}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mu\textbf{t}.{\tt reg}_{\text{\tiny\sf{SO}}}({\mathsf{U}},1,{\sf p}!\ell_{1}({\mathsf{S}}_{1}).{\sf p}?\ell_{4}({\mathsf{S}}_{4}).\textbf{t}\&{\sf p}!\ell_{2}({\mathsf{S}}_{2}).\textbf{t},{\mathbb{T}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mu\textbf{t}.{\sf p}!\ell_{1}({\mathsf{S}}_{1}).{\tt reg}_{\text{\tiny\sf{SO}}}({\mathsf{U}},0,{\sf p}?\ell_{4}({\mathsf{S}}_{4}).\textbf{t},{\mathbb{T}})

We can now choose any
{\mathbb{U}}_{2}
such that

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathcal{T}}\!\left({{\mathbb{U}}_{2}}\right)\in\llbracket\operatorname{\mathcal{T}}\!\left({{\sf p}?\ell_{4}({\mathsf{S}}_{4}).\mu\textbf{t}_{1}.({\sf p}!\ell_{1}({\mathsf{S}}_{1}).{\sf p}?\ell_{4}({\mathsf{S}}_{4}).\textbf{t}\&{\sf p}!\ell_{2}({\mathsf{S}}_{2}).\textbf{t}_{1})}\right)\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SO}}}.

For example, with

{\mathbb{U}}_{2}={\sf p}?\ell_{4}({\mathsf{S}}_{4}).\mu\textbf{t}_{2}.{\sf p}!\ell_{2}({\mathsf{S}}_{2}).\textbf{t}_{2}
we get

\displaystyle{\mathbb{U}}_{1}=

\displaystyle{\tt mu}^{-}(\mu\textbf{t}.{\sf p}!\ell_{1}({\mathsf{S}}_{1}).{\mathbb{U}}_{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\tt mu}^{-}(\mu\textbf{t}.{\sf p}!\ell_{1}({\mathsf{S}}_{1}).{\sf p}?\ell_{4}({\mathsf{S}}_{4}).\mu\textbf{t}_{2}.{\sf p}!\ell_{2}({\mathsf{S}}_{2}).\textbf{t}_{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\sf p}!\ell_{1}({\mathsf{S}}_{1}).{\sf p}?\ell_{4}({\mathsf{S}}_{4}).\mu\textbf{t}_{2}.{\sf p}!\ell_{2}({\mathsf{S}}_{2}).\textbf{t}_{2}.

Lemma C.12.

If
{\mathsf{V}}^{\prime}\in\llbracket\operatorname{\mathcal{T}}\!\left({{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}}\right)\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SO}}}
then there is
{\mathbb{V}}^{\prime}_{1}
such that
\operatorname{\mathcal{T}}\!\left({{\mathbb{V}}^{\prime}_{1}}\right)\in\llbracket\operatorname{\mathcal{T}}\!\left({{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}}\right)\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SI}}}
and
\operatorname{\mathcal{T}}\!\left({{\mathbb{V}}^{\prime}_{1}}\right)
overlaps with
{\mathsf{V}}^{\prime}
at top
n
levels.

Proof.

The construction is analogous to the one from the previous lemma.
∎

Corollary C.13.

Let
{\mathsf{U}}\in\llbracket\operatorname{\mathcal{T}}\!\left({{\mathbb{T}}}\right)\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SO}}}
and
{\mathsf{V}}^{\prime}\in\llbracket\operatorname{\mathcal{T}}\!\left({{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}}\right)\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SI}}}
be such that
{\mathsf{U}}\not\leqslant{\mathsf{V}}^{\prime}.

Then, there are
{\mathbb{U}}_{1}
and
{\mathbb{V}}^{\prime}_{1}
such that
\operatorname{\mathcal{T}}\!\left({{\mathbb{U}}_{1}}\right)\in\llbracket\operatorname{\mathcal{T}}\!\left({{\mathbb{T}}}\right)\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SO}}}
and
\operatorname{\mathcal{T}}\!\left({{\mathbb{V}}_{1}^{\prime}}\right)\in\llbracket\operatorname{\mathcal{T}}\!\left({{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}}\right)\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SI}}}

and
\operatorname{\mathcal{T}}\!\left({{\mathbb{U}}_{1}}\right)\not\leqslant\operatorname{\mathcal{T}}\!\left({{\mathbb{V}}_{1}^{\prime}}\right).

Proof.

If
{\mathsf{U}}\not\leqslant{\mathsf{V}}^{\prime}
was derived in
n
steps
(n\geq 1)
, there is
k
such that prefixes from top
n
levels of
{\mathsf{U}}
that appear in
{\mathsf{V}}^{\prime}
are placed in the top
k
levels of
{\mathsf{V}}^{\prime}
(those that are considered for the negation derivation). By Lemma
C.9
and Lemma
C.12
, there are
{\mathbb{U}}_{1}
and
{\mathbb{V}}_{1}^{\prime}
such that
\operatorname{\mathcal{T}}\!\left({{\mathbb{U}}_{1}}\right)\in\llbracket\operatorname{\mathcal{T}}\!\left({{\mathbb{T}}}\right)\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SO}}}
and
\operatorname{\mathcal{T}}\!\left({{\mathbb{V}}_{1}^{\prime}}\right)\in\llbracket\operatorname{\mathcal{T}}\!\left({{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}}\right)\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SI}}}
such that
\operatorname{\mathcal{T}}\!\left({{\mathbb{U}}_{1}}\right)
ovelaps with
{\mathsf{U}}
in top
n
levels and
\operatorname{\mathcal{T}}\!\left({{\mathbb{V}}_{1}}\right)
ovelaps with
{\mathsf{V}}^{\prime}
in top
k
levels. It can be derived in
n
steps that
\operatorname{\mathcal{T}}\!\left({{\mathbb{U}}_{1}}\right)\not\leqslant\operatorname{\mathcal{T}}\!\left({{\mathbb{V}}_{1}^{\prime}}\right).

∎

Step2: characteristic process

The proof that characteristic process of
{\mathbb{U}}
is typable by
\operatorname{\mathcal{T}}\!\left({{\mathbb{U}}}\right)
is exactly the same as in the case of synchronous multiparty sessions (See
(
Ghilezan et al. 2019
)
).
We consider only single-output processes and for such processes there is no difference in typing rules. The whole proof is replicated here, adapted to single-output processes.

Lemma C.14 (Strengthening).

If
\Theta,X:{\mathsf{U}}^{\prime}\vdash P:{\mathsf{U}}
and
X\not\in\mathsf{fv}\{P\}
then
\Theta\vdash P:{\mathsf{U}}.

Lemma C.15 (Weakening).

If
\Theta\vdash P:{\mathsf{U}}
and
X\not\in dom(\Theta)
then
\Theta,X:{\mathsf{U}}^{\prime}\vdash P:{\mathsf{U}}.

Lemma C.16.

If
\Theta,X_{\textbf{t}}:{\mathsf{U}}_{1}\vdash{\mathcal{P}}\!\left({\mathbb{U}}\right):\operatorname{\mathcal{T}}\!\left({{\mathbb{U}}\sigma}\right)
where
{\mathsf{U}}_{1}=\operatorname{\mathcal{T}}\!\left({{\mathbb{U}}_{1}}\right)
(for some
{\mathbb{U}}_{1}
),
and
\sigma=\{\nicefrac{{\Theta(X_{\textbf{t}^{\prime}})}}{{\textbf{t}^{\prime}}}\;|\;\textbf{t}^{\prime}\in\mathsf{fv}({\mathbb{U}})\},
then
\Theta,X_{\textbf{t}}:{\mathsf{U}}_{2}\vdash{\mathcal{P}}\!\left({\mathbb{U}}\right):\operatorname{\mathcal{T}}\!\left({{\mathbb{U}}\sigma^{\prime}}\right),

for any
{\mathsf{U}}_{2}=\operatorname{\mathcal{T}}\!\left({{\mathbb{U}}_{2}}\right)
(for some
{\mathbb{U}}_{2}
) and
\sigma^{\prime}=(\sigma\setminus\{\nicefrac{{{\mathbb{U}}_{1}}}{{\textbf{t}}}\})\cup\{\nicefrac{{{\mathbb{U}}_{2}}}{{\textbf{t}}}\}.

Proof.

By induction on the structure of
{\mathbb{U}}
.
∎

Lemma C.17.

For every (possibly open) type
{\mathbb{U}}
, there are
\Theta
and
\sigma
such that
\mathrm{dom}(\Theta)=\{X_{\textbf{t}}\,\;|\;\,\textbf{t}\in\mathsf{fv}({\mathbb{U}})\}

and
\Theta\vdash{\mathcal{P}}\!\left({\mathbb{U}}\right):\operatorname{\mathcal{T}}\!\left({{\mathbb{U}}\sigma}\right),
where
\sigma
is a substitution such that

\sigma=\{\nicefrac{{{\mathbb{U}}_{\!\textbf{t}}}}{{\textbf{t}}}\;\;|\;\;\textbf{t}\in\mathsf{fv}({\mathbb{U}})\text{ and }\Theta(X_{\textbf{t}})=\operatorname{\mathcal{T}}\!\left({{\mathbb{U}}_{\!\textbf{t}}}\right)\}.

Proof.

By induction on the structure of
{\mathbb{U}}
.

•

{\mathbb{U}}\equiv\mathtt{end}:

{\mathcal{P}}\!\left(\mathtt{end}\right)=\mathbf{0}
and, by
[
t-
\mathbf{0}
]
,
\vdash{\mathcal{P}}\!\left(\mathtt{end}\right):\mathtt{end}.

•

{\mathbb{U}}\equiv\textbf{t}:

{\mathcal{P}}\!\left(\textbf{t}\right)=X_{\textbf{t}}

By
[
t-var
]
,
X_{\textbf{t}}:\operatorname{\mathcal{T}}\!\left({{\mathbb{U}}^{\prime}}\right)\vdash X_{\textbf{t}}:\operatorname{\mathcal{T}}\!\left({{\mathbb{U}}^{\prime}}\right)
for any
{\mathbb{U}}^{\prime}.
For
\sigma=\{\nicefrac{{{\mathbb{U}}^{\prime}}}{{\textbf{t}}}\}
, we have

X_{\textbf{t}}:\operatorname{\mathcal{T}}\!\left({{\mathbb{U}}^{\prime}}\right)\vdash{\mathcal{P}}\!\left(\textbf{t}\right):\operatorname{\mathcal{T}}\!\left({{\mathbb{U}}\sigma}\right).

•

{\mathbb{U}}\equiv\texternal\limits_{i\in I}{\sf p}?\ell_{i}({\mathsf{S}}_{i}).{\mathbb{U}}_{i}:

{\mathcal{P}}\!\left({\mathbb{U}}\right)=\sum\limits_{i\in I}{\sf p}?\ell_{i}(x_{i}).\mathsf{if}\penalty\ \text{\rm{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\lx@text@underline{expr$\llparenthesis{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x_{i}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\mathsf{S}}_{i}}\rrparenthesis$}}}\penalty\ \mathsf{then}\penalty\ {\mathcal{P}}\!\left({\mathbb{U}}_{i}\right)\penalty\ \mathsf{else}\penalty\ {\mathcal{P}}\!\left({\mathbb{U}}_{i}\right)

By the induction hypothesis,
\Theta_{i}\vdash{\mathcal{P}}\!\left({\mathbb{U}}_{i}\right):\operatorname{\mathcal{T}}\!\left({{\mathbb{U}}_{i}\sigma_{i}}\right),
where
\sigma_{i}=\{\nicefrac{{{\mathbb{U}}_{\textbf{t}}}}{{\textbf{t}}}\;|\;\textbf{t}\in\mathsf{fv}({\mathbb{U}}_{i})
and
\operatorname{\mathcal{T}}\!\left({{\mathbb{U}}_{\textbf{t}}}\right)=\Theta_{i}(X_{\textbf{t}})\}
for some
\Theta_{i}
, and every
i\in I
.
Let us denote by
\Theta
the environment consisting of assignments
X_{\textbf{t}}:\operatorname{\mathcal{T}}\!\left({{\mathbb{U}}_{\textbf{t}}}\right)
for arbitrarily chosen
{\mathbb{U}}_{\textbf{t}}
, where
X_{\textbf{t}}\in dom(\Theta_{i})
for some
i\in I
. By typing rules,
\Theta,x_{i}:{\mathsf{S}}_{i}\vdash\text{\rm{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\lx@text@underline{expr$\llparenthesis{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x_{i}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\mathsf{S}}_{i}}\rrparenthesis$}}}:{\mathtt{bool}},
for every
i\in I
. By Lemma
C.16
,
[
t-cond
]
and weakening, for each
i\in I
, we have the judgements:

\Theta,x_{i}:{\mathsf{S}}_{i}\vdash\mathsf{if}\penalty\ \text{\rm{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\lx@text@underline{expr$\llparenthesis{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x_{i}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\mathsf{S}}_{i}}\rrparenthesis$}}}\penalty\ \mathsf{then}\penalty\ {\mathcal{P}}\!\left({\mathbb{U}}_{i}\right)\penalty\ \mathsf{else}\penalty\ {\mathcal{P}}\!\left({\mathbb{U}}_{i}\right):{\operatorname{\mathcal{T}}\!\left({{\mathbb{U}}_{i}\sigma_{i}^{\prime}}\right)}

where

\sigma_{i}^{\prime}=\left\{\nicefrac{{{\mathbb{U}}_{\textbf{t}}}}{{\textbf{t}}}\;\middle|\;\begin{array}[]{@{}l@{}}\textbf{t}\in\mathsf{fv}({\mathbb{U}}_{i})\text{ and\; }\\
\operatorname{\mathcal{T}}\!\left({{\mathbb{U}}_{\textbf{t}}}\right)\!=\!\Theta(X_{\textbf{t}})\end{array}\right\}.

Now, by
[
t-ext
]
, we have

\Theta\vdash\sum\limits_{i\in I}{\sf p}?\ell(x_{i}).\mathsf{if}\penalty\ \text{\rm{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\lx@text@underline{expr$\llparenthesis{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x_{i}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\mathsf{S}}_{i}}\rrparenthesis$}}}\penalty\ \mathsf{then}\penalty\ {\mathcal{P}}\!\left({\mathbb{U}}_{i}\right)\penalty\ \mathsf{else}\penalty\ {\mathcal{P}}\!\left({\mathbb{U}}_{i}\right):\texternal\limits_{i\in I}{\sf p}?\ell({\mathsf{S}}_{i}).\operatorname{\mathcal{T}}\!\left({{\mathbb{U}}_{i}\sigma_{i}^{\prime}}\right).

We conclude this case by remarking that

\texternal\limits_{i\in I}{\sf p}?\ell({\mathsf{S}}_{i}).\operatorname{\mathcal{T}}\!\left({{\mathbb{U}}_{i}\sigma_{i}^{\prime}}\right)=\operatorname{\mathcal{T}}\!\left({{\mathbb{U}}\sigma}\right)

for

\sigma=\cup_{i\in I}\sigma_{i}^{\prime}=\{\nicefrac{{{\mathbb{U}}_{\textbf{t}}}}{{\textbf{t}}}\;\;|\;\;\textbf{t}\in\mathsf{fv}({\mathbb{U}})\text{ and }\operatorname{\mathcal{T}}\!\left({{\mathbb{U}}_{\textbf{t}}}\right)=\Theta(X_{\textbf{t}})\}.

•

{\mathbb{U}}\equiv{\sf p}!\ell({\mathsf{S}}).{\mathbb{U}}^{\prime}:

{\mathcal{P}}\!\left({\mathbb{U}}\right)={\sf p}!\ell\langle\text{\rm{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\lx@text@underline{val$\llparenthesis{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\mathsf{S}}}\rrparenthesis$}}}\rangle.{\mathcal{P}}\!\left({\mathbb{U}}^{\prime}\right)

By the induction hypothesis,
\Theta^{\prime}\vdash{\mathcal{P}}\!\left({\mathbb{U}}^{\prime}\right):\operatorname{\mathcal{T}}\!\left({{\mathbb{U}}^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}}\right),
where
\sigma^{\prime}=\{\nicefrac{{{\mathbb{U}}_{\textbf{t}}}}{{\textbf{t}}}\;|\;\textbf{t}\in\mathsf{fv}({\mathbb{U}}^{\prime})
and
\operatorname{\mathcal{T}}\!\left({{\mathbb{U}}_{\textbf{t}}}\right)=\Theta^{\prime}(X_{\textbf{t}})\}
for some
\Theta^{\prime}
. Let us denote by
\Theta
the environment consisting of assignments
X_{\textbf{t}}:\operatorname{\mathcal{T}}\!\left({{\mathbb{U}}_{\textbf{t}}}\right)
for arbitrarily chosen
{\mathbb{U}}_{\textbf{t}}
, where
X_{\textbf{t}}\in dom(\Theta^{\prime})
.

By
[
t-out
]
,

\Theta^{\prime}\vdash{{\sf p}!\ell(\text{\rm{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\lx@text@underline{val$\llparenthesis{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}S}\rrparenthesis$}}}).{\mathcal{P}}\!\left({\mathbb{U}}\right)}:{{\sf p}!\ell({\mathsf{S}}).\operatorname{\mathcal{T}}\!\left({{\mathbb{U}}^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}}\right)}.

•

{\mathbb{U}}\equiv\mu\textbf{t}.{\mathbb{U}}^{\prime}:

{\mathcal{P}}\!\left({\mathbb{U}}\right)=\mu X_{\textbf{t}}.{\mathcal{P}}\!\left({\mathbb{U}}^{\prime}\right)

By induction hypothesis, there is
\Theta^{\prime}
such that

\Theta^{\prime}\vdash{\mathcal{P}}\!\left({\mathbb{U}}^{\prime}\right):\operatorname{\mathcal{T}}\!\left({{\mathbb{U}}^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}}\right)\qquad\text{
where
$\sigma^{\prime}=\{\nicefrac{{{\mathbb{U}}_{\textbf{t}^{\prime}}}}{{\textbf{t}^{\prime}}}\;|\;\textbf{t}^{\prime}\in\mathsf{fv}({\mathbb{U}}^{\prime})\text{ and }\operatorname{\mathcal{T}}\!\left({{\mathbb{U}}_{\textbf{t}^{\prime}}}\right)=\Theta^{\prime}(X_{\textbf{t}^{\prime}})\}$
}

We have two cases:

(i)

\textbf{t}\not\in\mathsf{fv}({\mathbb{U}}^{\prime})
. In this case,
X_{\textbf{t}}\not\in\mathsf{fv}({\mathcal{P}}\!\left({\mathbb{U}}^{\prime}\right))
and
\Theta^{\prime\prime},X_{\textbf{t}}:\operatorname{\mathcal{T}}\!\left({{\mathbb{U}}^{\prime\prime}}\right)\vdash{\mathcal{P}}\!\left({\mathbb{U}}^{\prime}\right):\operatorname{\mathcal{T}}\!\left({{\mathbb{U}}^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}}\right)
, for some
{\mathbb{U}}^{\prime\prime}

(either
\Theta^{\prime}=\Theta^{\prime\prime},X_{\textbf{t}}:\operatorname{\mathcal{T}}\!\left({{\mathbb{U}}^{\prime\prime}}\right)
or it is obtained by weakening of
\Theta^{\prime}
). By
[
t-rec
]
, we get
\Theta^{\prime\prime}\vdash\mu X_{\textbf{t}}.{\mathcal{P}}\!\left({\mathbb{U}}^{\prime}\right):\operatorname{\mathcal{T}}\!\left({{\mathbb{U}}^{\prime}\sigma}\right)
with
\sigma^{\prime}=\sigma.

(ii)

\textbf{t}\in\mathsf{fv}({\mathbb{U}}^{\prime})
. In this case,
X_{\textbf{t}}\in\mathsf{fv}({\mathcal{P}}\!\left({\mathbb{U}}^{\prime}\right))
and
\Theta^{\prime}=\Theta^{\prime\prime},X_{\textbf{t}}:\operatorname{\mathcal{T}}\!\left({{\mathbb{U}}^{\prime\prime}}\right)
for some
{\mathbb{U}}^{\prime\prime}.
By Lemma
C.16
,

\begin{array}[]{c}\Theta^{\prime\prime},X_{\textbf{t}}:\operatorname{\mathcal{T}}\!\left({{\mathbb{U}}^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}}\right)\vdash{\mathcal{P}}\!\left({\mathbb{U}}^{\prime}\right):\operatorname{\mathcal{T}}\!\left({{\mathbb{U}}^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime\prime}}\right)\\
\text{where}\quad\sigma^{\prime\prime}\,=\,\left\{\nicefrac{{{\mathbb{U}}_{\textbf{t}^{\prime}}}}{{\textbf{t}^{\prime}}}\;\;\middle|\;\;\textbf{t}^{\prime}\in\mathsf{fv}({\mathbb{U}}^{\prime})\setminus\{\textbf{t}\}\text{ and }\operatorname{\mathcal{T}}\!\left({{\mathbb{U}}_{\textbf{t}^{\prime}}}\right)=\Theta^{\prime\prime}(X_{\textbf{t}^{\prime}})\right\}\cup\{\nicefrac{{{\mathbb{U}}^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}}}{{\textbf{t}}}\}\end{array}

i.e., the difference between
\sigma^{\prime}
and
\sigma^{\prime\prime}
is that
\sigma^{\prime\prime}
contains
\nicefrac{{{\mathbb{U}}^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}}}{{\textbf{t}}}

instead of
\nicefrac{{{\mathbb{U}}^{\prime\prime}}}{{\textbf{t}}}
. Then, by
[
t-rec
]
, we conclude

\Theta^{\prime\prime}\vdash\mu X_{\textbf{t}}.{\mathcal{P}}\!\left({\mathbb{U}}^{\prime}\right):\operatorname{\mathcal{T}}\!\left({{\mathbb{U}}^{\prime}\sigma}\right)

where

\sigma\,=\,\{\nicefrac{{{\mathbb{U}}_{\textbf{t}}}}{{\textbf{t}}}\;|\;\textbf{t}\in\mathsf{fv}({\mathbb{U}})

and

\operatorname{\mathcal{T}}\!\left({{\mathbb{U}}_{\textbf{t}}}\right)=\Gamma^{\prime\prime}(X_{\textbf{t}})\}\,=\,\sigma^{\prime\prime}\setminus\{\nicefrac{{{\mathbb{U}}^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}}}{{\textbf{t}}}\}.

∎

Lemma C.18.

Let
{\mathsf{T}}
be a session type tree. Then

(i)

\forall{\mathsf{U}}\in\llbracket{\mathsf{T}}\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SO}}}

{\mathsf{U}}\leqslant{\mathsf{T}}

(ii)

\forall{\mathsf{V}}^{\prime}\in\llbracket{\mathsf{T}}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SI}}}

{\mathsf{T}}^{\prime}\leqslant{\mathsf{V}}^{\prime}

Proof.

Follows from the definition of decompositions.
∎

See
5.6

Proof.

As a direct consequence of Lemma
C.17

we get
\vdash{\mathcal{P}}\!\left({\mathbb{U}}\right):{\mathbb{U}}.

Since by Lemma
C.18
for every
{\mathbb{U}}
with
\operatorname{\mathcal{T}}\!\left({{\mathbb{U}}}\right)\in\llbracket\operatorname{\mathcal{T}}\!\left({{\mathbb{T}}}\right)\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SO}}},

\operatorname{\mathcal{T}}\!\left({{\mathbb{U}}}\right)\leqslant\operatorname{\mathcal{T}}\!\left({{\mathbb{T}}}\right),
by
[
t-sub
]
,
\vdash{\mathcal{P}}\!\left({\mathbb{U}}\right):{\mathbb{T}}.

∎

Step3: characteristic session

See
5.10

Proof.

Follows directly from the construction of the characteristic session types and the definition of the live typing environments.
∎

See
5.11

Proof.

By Proposition
5.10
,
\vdash Q_{1}:{\mathbb{V}}^{\prime}
implies
there is a live typing environment
\Gamma^{\prime\prime}
such that

\Gamma^{\prime\prime}\vdash{\sf r}\triangleleft Q_{1}\penalty\ |\penalty\ {\sf r}\triangleleft\varnothing\penalty\ |\penalty\ \mathcal{M}_{{\sf r},{\mathbb{V}}^{\prime}}

where
\Gamma^{\prime\prime}=\Gamma^{\prime},{\sf r}:(\varnothing,{\mathbb{V}}^{\prime})
.
By Lemma
C.18
we have
{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}\leqslant{\mathbb{V}}^{\prime}
.
Since for
\Gamma=\Gamma^{\prime},{\sf r}:(\varnothing,{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime})

we have
\Gamma\leqslant\Gamma^{\prime\prime}
, by
Lemma
4.7
we obtain that
\Gamma
is also live.
Hence, if
\vdash Q:{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime},
then we may show

\Gamma\vdash{\sf r}\triangleleft Q\penalty\ |\penalty\ {\sf r}\triangleleft\varnothing\penalty\ |\penalty\ \mathcal{M}_{{\sf r},{\mathbb{V}}^{\prime}}

and
\Gamma
is live.
∎

Table 7
.
Shapes of unrelated
{\mathsf{U}}
and
{\mathsf{V}}^{\prime}
type trees

In order to describe the shape of
{\mathsf{V}}^{\prime}
type when
{\mathsf{U}}\not\leqslant{\mathsf{V}}^{\prime}

is derived using cases that involve context
\mathcal{B}^{({\sf p})}

(for the corresponding projections that satisfy
{\mathsf{W}}\not\lesssim{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}
)
we define context
\mathcal{C}^{({\sf p})}
with holes, that (as
{\mathsf{V}}
) have only single inputs,
and, in which there are no outputs on
{\sf p}
.

\mathcal{C}^{({\sf p})}::=[\;]^{n}\penalty\ \penalty\ \mathbf{|\!\!|}\penalty\ \penalty\ {\sf q}?\ell({\mathsf{S}}).\mathcal{C}^{({\sf p})}\penalty\ \penalty\ \mathbf{|\!\!|}\penalty\ \penalty\ \tinternal_{i\in I}{\sf r}!\ell_{i}({\mathsf{S}}_{i}).\mathcal{C}^{({\sf p})}\oplus\tinternal_{i\in I^{\prime}}{\sf r}!\ell_{i}({\mathsf{S}}_{i}).{\mathsf{V}}^{\prime}_{i}\hskip 28.80005pt{\sf r}\not={\sf p}\text{ and }{\sf p}!\not\in\mathtt{act}({\mathsf{V}}^{\prime}_{i})

Note that, in case of selection, context
\mathcal{C}^{({\sf p})}
may have holes only in some branches
while the rest of the branches contain no outputs on
{\sf p}
.
This allows us to determine all the “first” outputs appearing in some
{\mathsf{V}}^{\prime}
type tree.
We write
\mathcal{C}^{({\sf p})}[\;]^{n\in N}
to denote a context
with holes indexed by elements of
N

and
\mathcal{C}^{({\sf p})}[{\mathsf{V}}^{\prime}_{n}]^{n\in N}
to denote the same context when the hole

[\;]^{n}
has been filled with
{\mathsf{V}}^{\prime}_{n}
.
We index the holes in contexts in order to distinguish them.
For the rest of the paper we assume
\mathcal{C}^{({\sf p})}
are nonempty, i.e.,
it always holds that
\mathcal{C}^{({\sf p})}\neq[\;]
.

Furthermore, for a context
\mathcal{C}^{({\sf p})}[\;]^{n\in N}
we may apply
a mapping
\mathcal{C}^{({\sf p})}\upharpoonright n
that produces the projection of the context
into the path that leads to the hole indexed with
n
,
i.e., it produces the corresponding
\mathcal{B}^{({\sf p})}.[\;]^{n}
.

Lemma C.19.

If
\neg({\mathsf{U}}\leqslant{\mathsf{V}}^{\prime})
then
{\mathsf{U}}\not\leqslant{\mathsf{V}}^{\prime}
can be derived
by the inductive rules given in Table
7
.

Proof.

If
\neg({\mathsf{U}}\leqslant{\mathsf{V}}^{\prime})
then
\forall{\mathsf{W}}\in\llbracket{\mathsf{U}}\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SI}}}

and
\forall{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}\in\llbracket{\mathsf{V}}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SO}}}
holds
{\mathsf{W}}\not\lesssim{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}
.

{\mathsf{W}}\not\lesssim{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}
.

•

Case
[
n-out
]
:

and

[
n-out
]
,
then from

we conclude

,
by the definition of
\llbracket\;\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SI}}}
.

(by definition of
\llbracket\;\rrbracket_{\text{\tiny\sf{SO}}}
).
Hence, we get that

and
